Corrigendum dated 9th September, 2021.



 M.P. Power Generating Company Ltd, Jabalpur:                            PETITIONER

Vs.

1. M.P. Power Management Company Ltd., Jabalpur

2. M.P. Power Transmission Co. Ltd., Jabalpur

3. Rajasthan Rajya Vidyut Prasaran Nigam Ltd., Jaipur                        RESPONDENTS

4. Uttar Pradesh Power Corporation Ltd. (UPPCL), Lucknow

5. MSEB (Holding Co) & Maharashtra State Transmission
   Co. Ltd., Mumbai




M.P.Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                   Page 1
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

                                              ORDER
                                (Passed on this day of 8th April, 2025)

1. M.P. Power Generating Company Ltd. (hereinafter called “the petitioner” or “MPPGCL”)
   has filed the subject petition for true-up of generation tariff for FY 2023-24 determined
   by the Madhya Pradesh Electricity Regulatory Commission (hereinafter called “the
   Commission or MPERC”) vide Multi-Year Tariff order dated 19thMay, 2021 in petition No
   53 of 2020 read with corrigendum dated 9th September, 2021.

2. The subject true-up petition has been filed by MPPGCL under Section 62 and Section
   64 of the Electricity Act, 2003, read with proviso 9.4 of MPERC (Terms and Conditions
   for Determination of Generation Tariff) Regulations, 2020, (hereinafter referred to as
   “the Regulations, 2020”). The subject petition is based on the Annual Audited Accounts
   of MPPGCL for FY 2023-24.

3. The petitioner is a wholly owned company of Government of M.P. engaged in
   generation of electricity in the state of Madhya Pradesh. It is a successor entity of
   erstwhile Madhya Pradesh State Electricity Board.

4. MP Power Management Company Ltd “(hereinafter referred to as Respondent No. 1 or
   MPPMCL) is the holding company of all three DISCOMS within the state of Madhya
   Pradesh. MPPMCL is entitled to undertake transition of bulk sale and purchase of
   electricity on behalf of distribution licenses, viz. MP discoms.

5. M.P. Power Transmission Company Ltd. Jabalpur, (hereinafter referred as Respondent
   No. 2) or MPPTCL is a government owned company responsible for bulk power
   transmission at extra high voltage within the state of Madhya Pradesh.

6. Rajasthan Rajya Vidyut Prasaran Nigam Ltd.(RRVPNL), Jaipur, (hereinafter referred
   as Respondent No. 3) has 50% share in Gandhi Sagar Hydro Power Station operated
   by MPPGCL. At the same time, MPPGCL has 50% share in the Rana pratap Sagar
   Hydro Power Station and Jawahar Sagar Hydro Power Station operated by the
   Respondent No. 3. Similarly, Uttar Pradesh Power Corporation Ltd (UPPCL), Lucknow
   (hereinafter referred as Respondent No. 4) has 40.32% share in Rajghat Hydro Power
   Station and MSEB (Holding Co) & Maharashtra State Transmission Co. Ltd., Mumbai
   (hereinafter referred as Respondent No. 5) has 33.3% share in Pench Hydro Power
   Station operated by MPPGCL.

   below:

8. Earlier, the petitioner filed petition No. 53 of 2020 for determination of Multi-year Tariff
   (MYT) for the control period FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24 based on the Regulations, 2020.
   Vide Order dated 19th May, 2021, the Commission determined the Multi-Year Tariff for
   the Control period of FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24 subject to True-Up based on Annual
   Audited Accounts for the respective years. Subsequently, a corrigendum to the
   aforesaid MYT Order was issued on 9th September, 2021.

9. Further, vide Order dated 27.07.2023 in Petition No. 11 of 2023, the Commission
   allowed special allowance for Unit No 1 & 2 (2x210MW) of SGTPS, Birsinghpur, PH-1
   after completion of useful life (25 years) of Units. Vide Order dated 17.01.2024 in
   Petition No. 52 of 2023, the Commission also allowed special allowance for Unit No.3
   of SGTPS, Birsinghpur, PH-2 for the period from 28.02.2024 to 31.03.2024 (33 days
   only) for FY 2023-24.

11.          In the subject true-up petition, the petitioner broadly submitted the following:
            a)     MPPGCL entered into Power Purchase Agreements (PPAs) with MP
                   Tradeco (now MPPMCL) on 29.11.2006 for existing power stations and on
                   04.01.2011 for new Thermal Power Stations. Further, the first amendment
                   to the Power Purchase Agreement (PPA) dated 29.11.2006 towards
                   Purchase of Power from then existing and ongoing Thermal and Hydel Units
                   of M. P. Power Generating Company Limited, has been signed on
  M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                               Page 4
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

                 18.07.2017. This First Amendment in Agreement provides term period till
                 retirement/ decommissioning of all Thermal and Hydel Power Stations of
                 MPPGCL listed in the agreement. The PPAs for new power stations have
                 also been amended from time to time. These PPAs provide that the tariff
                 payable to Genco and terms & conditions related thereto shall be as
                 determined by the State Commission.
      b)         The Commission notified, MPERC (Terms & Conditions for determination of
                 Generation Tariff) Regulations, 2020{RG-26 (IV) of 2020} on 28.02.2020.
                 This regulation was based on multi-year tariff principles and incorporated
                 norms of operation for control period FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24.
      c)         In the instant True up petition, MPPGCL has proposed true up for FY 2023-
                 24 in respect of existing/new stations whose tariff was determined by the
                 Commission vide Multi-Year Tariff Order dated 19.05.2021 for the control
                 period FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24 read with corrigendum to aforesaid MYT
                 order vide order dated 09.09.2021.

      d)         MPPGCL has prepared the Books of Accounts for FY 2023-24 and got the
                 same audited by the Statutory Auditor. The Audit Officers from O/o Dy.
                 Accountant General (ES-III), Bhopal, has conducted Supplementary Audit of
                 Annual Statement of Accounts of MPPGCL for FY 2023-24.

      e)         MPPGCL is submitting the True up petition for FY 2023-24 based on the
                 Audited Annual Statements of Accounts for FY 2023-24 under Section 62 &
                 64 of the Electricity Act 2003 read with proviso-9 of the Regulations, 2020.

      f)         The Annual Statement of Accounts for FY 2023-24 has been prepared on
                 the basis of Indian Accounting Standards (Ind AS) prescribed under Section
                 133 of the Companies Act read with the rule 3 of Companies (Indian
                 Accounting Standard) Rules, 2015 and Companies (Indian Accounting
                 Standard) amendment Rules, 2016.
      g)         The Petitioner has adopted following approach for True-up of Generation
                 Tariff for FY 2023-24: -
           i.      The Energy Charges (Variable Charges) has been billed in accordance
                   with proviso 18, 19 & 43 of the Regulation, 2020. Therefore, no truing up
                   of Energy Charges has been considered in this petition.

           ii.     Other Charges comprising of MPERC Fees, Water Charges, Rent, Rates
                   & taxes, Cost of Chemical & Consumable, Publication Expenses and EL
                   Encashment have been claimed on actuals based on Audited Books of
                   Accounts for FY 2023-24.



M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                               Page 5
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024



           iii.   The expense shown in Audited Annual Statements of Accounts for FY
                  2023-24 pertains to MPPGCL’s share. The expenses, as extracted from
                  Audited Annual Statements of Accounts for FY 2023-24 for the shared
                  portion have been factored to represent 100% capacity operated by
                  MPPGCL to match with MPERC’s Multi Year Tariff Order dated
                  19.05.2021.

           iv.    The expenses of Rana Pratap Sagar and Jawahar Sagar indicated in the
                  Annual Statements of Accounts for FY 2023-24 of MPPGCL have not
                  been considered in this True up Petition since the Commission has not
                  considered these projects in Tariff order, being operated by Rajasthan
                  authorities.
            v.    At present, expenditure towards Pension & Terminal benefits of all the
                  Companies i.e. unbundled entities of MPSEB are being allowed in the
                  MYT/ True up of Transmission Licensee i.e. MPPTCL on ‘pay as you go’
                  principle, subject to true-up in each year on availability of actual figures.
                  Accordingly, MPPGCL has not claimed these expenses in this True-up
                  tariff petition
           vi.    Commission vide Second amendment to MPERC (Terms and Conditions
                  for determination of Generation Tariff) (Second Amendment)
                  Regulations, 2020 {ARG-26 (IV)(ii) of 2023} dated 21.02.2023 at Para 27
                  has issued Amendment to Regulation 65 of the principal Regulations,
                  wherein Expenses towards Fly Ash Utilization and transportation shall be
                  payable in accordance to the directives issued by Government of India,
                  Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change vide Notification No.
                  S.O. 5481 (E) dated 31.12.2021 and subsequent amendment issued from
                  time to time.
                  Accordingly, the details of expenditure incurred on Transportation of Fly
                  Ash as captured in Note 31.1 “Other Expenses” Serial No. 8 of Audited
                  Financial Statements of FY 2023-24 amounts to Rs. 176.41 Crore.
                  MPPGCL request Commission to permit the same.

14. The Petitioner has sought true-up of Annual Capacity (Fixed) Charges for FY 2023-24
    in respect of additional capital expenditure incurred during FY 2023-24 in accordance
    with Regulation 9.4 of the Regulations, 2020 which provides as under:

        “A generating company shall file a petition at the beginning of the Tariff period. A
        review shall be undertaken by the Commission to scrutinize and true up the Tariff
        on the basis of the capital expenditure and additional capital expenditure actually
        incurred in the Year for which the true up is being requested. The generating
        company shall submit for the purpose of truing up, details of capital expenditure
        and additional capital expenditure incurred for each year of the period from
        1.4.2019 to 31.3.2024, duly audited and certified by the auditors.”
    M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                    Page 9
                                                     MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


 15. In the subject petition, the petitioner has filed additional capitalization of Rs. 79.61 Crore
     in thermal and hydel power stations during FY 2023-24 as per Annual Audited Accounts
     and Asset-cum-Depreciation registers of respective power stations. The petitioner also
     filed the write-off/ deductions of assets of Rs. 11.02 Crore in some of the power stations
     during FY 2023-24 as per Annual Audited Accounts and Asset-cum Depreciation
     Registers of respective power stations.

 16. With the above submissions, the petitioner prayed the following:
       (a)    Approve Annual Capacity (Fixed) Charges and Other charges for FY 2023-24
              and permit recovery of True up amount as per Para 17 & 18 in six equal monthly
              instalments.

       (b)    Allow additional capitalization as per audited Annual Statements of Accounts
              for FY 2023-24 and accordingly permit additional Depreciation, RoE and
              Interest on Normative Loan/excess equity. Allow additional Capitalizations of
              prior period corresponding to Assets Written-off in this Financial year.

       (c)    Allow Other Charges comprising of MPERC Fees, Water Charges, Rent, Rates
              & taxes, Cost of Chemical & Consumable, Publication Expenses and EL
              Encashment on actual based on Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2023-24.
       (d)    Allow additional expenses incurred towards Transportation of Ash amounting
              to Rs. 176.41 Crore.
       (e)    In accordance with proviso 9.11 of Regulations, 2020, allow interest on
              differential true-up amount, if any.

     Procedural History:

17. With regard to time line for filing of true up petition, Regulation 9.7 of the Regulations,
    2020 provides that a generating company shall make an application to the Commission
    for carrying out truing up exercise for each financial year in respect of a generating
    station/ unit by15th November of each year. MPPGCL vide letter dated 06.11.2024
    sought time extension for submission of true up petition as the supplementary audit of
    the Company’s accounts for FY 2023-24 has not been done by the Dy. Accountant
    General (ES-III), Bhopal. The Commission vide letter dated 12.11.2025 directed
    MPPGCL to file the true up petition by 25.11.2025. Accordingly, by affidavit dated
    25.11.2024 MPPGCL filed the subject true up petition for FY 2023-24, without certifying
    its Annual Audited Accounts by AG, Audit, Bhopal and the same were submitted by
    MPPGCL on 06.12.2024.

18. Motion hearing in the subject true up petition was held on 17th December, 2024, wherein
    the petition was admitted and petitioner was directed to serve copies of petition to all

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 10
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     Respondents in the matter. The Respondents were also directed to file their responses
     on the petition within 15 days. The petitioner was asked to file rejoinder within a week,
     thereafter.

19. Vide Commission’s letter dated 31st December, 2024, the information gaps and
    requirements of additional details/documents in the subject petition were
    communicated to the petitioner seeking its comprehensive reply along with all relevant
    supporting documents by 20th January, 2025.

20. The public notices inviting comments/suggestions from stakeholders were published
    on 23rd January, 2025 in the following newspapers:

             (i)     Dainik Bhaskar, Jabalpur (Hindi)
             (ii)    Patrika, Gwalior (Hindi)
             (iii)   Nai Duniya, Indore (Hindi)
             (iv)    Raj Express, Bhopal (Hindi)
             (v)     Free Express, Bhopal (English)

     Last date of filing comment/objections/suggestions was 13th February, 2025.

21. By affidavit dated 20th January, 2025, the petitioner filed its partial response on the
    queries raised by the Commission and requested to grant additional time extension of
    10 days for submission of balance information. By affidavit dated 6th February, 2025
    15th February, 2025 and 11th March, 2025, the petitioner filed its response to the balance
    information issues/queries raised by the Commission.

22. By affidavit dated 05th February, 2025, Respondent No. 1 (M.P. Power Management
    Co. Ltd) filed its comments/ response on the subject petition.

23. By affidavit dated 27th February, 2025, the petitioner filed rejoinder on the response/
    comments filed by the Respondent No. 1. The petitioner’s response on each comment
    offered by the Respondent No.1 along with the observations is annexed as Annexure-
    I of this Order.

24. Vide letter dated 10th February, 2025, comments from only one stakeholder, Shri
    Rajendra Agarwal were received in this matter. By affidavit dated 27th February, 2025,
    the petitioner filed its reply to the aforesaid comments filed by the stakeholder. The
    petitioner’s reply on each comment/suggestion offered by the stakeholder along with
    the observations is annexed as Annexure-II with this Order.

25. The public hearing in the subject true-up petition was held on 19th February, 2025
    through video conferencing, wherein the representatives of the petitioner, Respondent
    No 1 and stakeholder appeared. After hearing the parties, the case was reserved for
    Order.
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 11
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

 26. The Commission has noted that the petitioner filed partial replies to various queries in
     subject true up petition within the time limit, however, the reply on balance issues filed
     by the petitioner at later stage. The Commission express its displeasure on account of
     inordinate delay took place at petitioner’s end in filing of annual audited accounts as
     well as replies to various queries. It is further observed that other state owned power
     companies of MP filed their true up petitions along with Annual Audited Accounts within
     the time lines. It must be realized by the petitioner that MP Genco true up consists of
     large numbers of additionally capitalized assets. It requires cross checking from audited
     accounts and delay in submission of audited accounts leads to delay in issue of true up
     order. In view of the above, the petitioner is directed to maintain the time lines in
     submission of all required documents in respect of all tariff related petitions in future.

     Disclaimer for Rounding
 27. In this Order, certain numbers as a whole, upto several decimal places have been
     rounded up or down. Therefore, there may be discrepancies between the totals of the
     individual numbers shown in the tables upto two decimal places and numbers given in
     the corresponding analysis in the text of this order.

    Provisions in Regulation:
30. Regarding capital cost of the existing generating stations, Regulation 21.3 and 21.4 of
    the Regulations, 2020 provides as under:

      21.3 “The Capital cost of an existing project shall include the following:
        (i) the capital cost admitted by the Commission prior to 1.4.2019 duly trued up by
              excluding liability, if any, up to last true-up order issued by the Commission;
        (ii) additional capitalization and de-capitalization for the respective year of tariff as
              determined in accordance with these Regulations;
        (iii) capital expenditure on account of renovation and modernization as admitted by
              the Commission in accordance with these Regulations;
        (iv) capital expenditure on account of ash disposal including handling and
              transportation facility;
        (v) capital expenditure incurred towards railway infrastructure and its augmentation
              for transportation of coal upto the receiving end of generating station but does
              not include the transportation cost and any other appurtenant cost paid to the
              railway; and
        (vi) capital cost incurred or projected to be incurred by a thermal generating station,
              on account of implementation of the norms under Perform, Achieve and Trade
              (PAT) scheme of Government of India shall be considered by the Commission
              subject to sharing of benefits accrued under the PAT scheme with the
              beneficiaries


      21.4 The capital cost in case of existing/new hydro generating station shall also
          include:
          (i) cost of approved rehabilitation and resettlement (R&R) plan of the project in
               conformity with National R&R Policy and R&R package as approved; and
          (ii) cost of the developer’s 10% contribution towards Rajiv Gandhi Grameen
               Vidyutikaran Yojana (RGGVY) and Deen Dayal Upadhyaya Gram Jyoti Yojana
               (DDUGJY) project in the affected area.”




     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 13
                                                       MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


     Commission’s Analysis:
 31. With regard to capital cost, Regulation 7.4 of the Regulations, 2020, provides that in
     case existing project, the petition shall be based on admitted capital cost in last true-up
     order. Accordingly, the petitioner has filed the Opening Gross Fixed Assets (GFA) of Rs.
     25,213.21 Crore as on 01st April, 2023 for its thermal and hydel power stations covered
     in the subject true-up petition same as admitted by the Commission in last true up order
     dated 07th March, 2024 for FY 2022-23. As such, the figures of Gross Fixed Assets,
     Normative Loan and Normative Equity considered in subject petition for FY 2023-24
     are as per the figures already admitted by the Commission in the last True up Order for
     FY 2022-23 in Petition. No. 71/2023.

 32. On scrutiny of the capital cost and additional capitalization filed in the petition, mismatch
     in the figures of assets additions as per Annual Audited Accounts and figures filed in
     the subject petition was observed. It was also observed that the petitioner has not filed
     Power Station/House wise Assets-cum-Depreciation Registers for FY 2023-24.
     Therefore, vide Commission’s letter dated 31st December, 2024, the petitioner was
     asked to file power station-wise Assets-cum-Depreciation registers for FY 2023-24. The
     petitioner was also asked to reconcile the figures of addition of assets recorded in the
     Assets-cum-Depreciation registers with the figures in Annual Audited Accounts for FY
     2023-24 as well as with the figures filed in the subject true-up petition.

33. By affidavit dated 20th January, 2025 petitioner submitted the Power Station-wise
    Assets-cum-Depreciation Registers for FY 2023-24 prepared in accordance with the
    provisions under Regulations, 2020. The petitioner also filed the Power Station wise
    comparative statements elaborating the difference between the figures as per Annual
    Audited Accounts of FY 2023-24 & recorded in Asset-Cum-Depreciation Registers vis-
    à-vis as claimed in subject petition with respect to Opening Gross fixed Assets, Assets
    Addition, Write-off/Adjustment of Assets-not-in-use etc., along with reasons.

35. The petitioner submitted the power station-wise justification for the difference in
    opening GFA as on 01.4.2023 as filed in the petition vis-a-vis recorded in Annual
    Audited Accounts. From the above, it is observed that the difference in GFA of Rs.
    25,213.21 Crore as on 31st March, 2023 filed by the petitioner as per last true-up order
    dated 7th March, 2024 and the opening GFA as per Annual Audited Accounts for FY
    2023-24 is on account of those assets which were not considered by the Commission
    in the previous tariff / true up orders due to various reasons such as not allowing asset
    additions over and above the cut-off date or capitalization not falling under the
    respective Regulations, etc., however, the petitioner has recorded/included all such
    asset additions in the Annual Audited Accounts.

36. It was further observed that the total asset addition claimed in the subject petition is
    Rs.79.61 Crore, whereas, the total asset addition of Rs.125.03 Crore (before assets
    not in use and INDAS adjustment) is indicated in the Annual Audited Accounts. It is
    noted that the difference in additional capitalization of Rs. 2.08 Crore is due to asset
    addition towards need based R&M works carried out in SGTPS PH 1&2. Since, the
    petitioner has already availed special allowance in this Power Station, therefore the
    additional capitalization of Rs. 2.08 Crore in SGTPS PH-1&2 is not claimed by the
    petitioner. Further in Annual Audited Accounts petitioner has recorded Rs. 42.32 Crore
    towards one of the solar plant transferred to petitioner by WRD and Rs. 1.03 Crore
    toward HQ, which are not the part of instant petition.
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                Page 15
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


37. It was also observed that the total deduction/write-off of assets filed in the petition is of
    Rs.11.02 Crore, whereas, the total deduction/write-off of Rs.14.53 Crore (before assets
    not in use and INDAS adjustment) is recorded in the Annual Audited Accounts. This
    mismatch of figures was observed in PH-1 & 2 of SGTPS and Pench HPS. As per
    Annual Audited Accounts, write-off amount is Rs.0.49 Crore in SGTPS PH-1 & 2,
    whereas, the write-off amount filed in the petition in this power station was Rs. 0.09
    Crore. In this regard petitioner submitted that Deduction/Adjustment towards Asset
    Retirement Obligation at SGTPS PH-1&2 of Rs.0.39 Crore is not considered, as the
    same was not allowed by the Commission as asset addition in earlier True Up orders.
    Further, as per Annual Audited Accounts, write-off amount is Rs.4.10 Crore in Pench,
    whereas, the write-off amount filed in the petition in this power station is Rs. 1.12 Crore.
    In this regard petitioner submitted that Deduction/Adjustment towards Capital Overhaul
    Expenses amounting to Rs. 2.98 Crore is not considered as the same was not permitted
    by the Commission as asset addition in earlier True Up orders. Moreover, in annual
    audited accounts, petitioner has written off the assets amounting to Rs. 0.13 Crore
    toward HQ which is not part of the petition. Accordingly, adjustment of Rs. 3.51 Crore
    with respect to assets added during FY 2013-14 is not considered by the petitioner,
    while filing the write-off amount in the subject petition.

     Provision in Regulation

40. Regarding additional capitalization of the generating stations, Regulations 27.1, 27.2
    and 28.1 of the Regulations, 2020 provide that:

        27. Additional Capitalization within the original scope and after the cut-off date:
     27.1 The additional capital expenditure incurred or projected to be incurred in respect
           of an existing project or a new project on the following counts within the original
           scope of work and after the cut-off date may be admitted by the Commission,
           subject to prudence check:
     (i) Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the directions or order
           of any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law;
     (ii) Change in law or compliance of any existing law:
     (iii) Deferred works relating to ash pond or ash handling system including ash
           transportation facility in the original scope of work;
     (iv) Liability for works executed prior to the cut-off date;
     (v) Force majeure events;
     (vi) Liability for works admitted by the Commission after the cut-off date to the extent
           of discharge of such liabilities by actual payment; and
     (vii) Additional capitalization on account of raising of ash dyke as a part of ash disposal
           system.

     27.2 In case of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope of the existing
          project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted by the
          Commission, after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and
          the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check on the following grounds:
     (a) The useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project
          and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of
          these Regulations;
     (b) The replacement of the asset or equipment if necessary, on account of change in
          law or Force Majeure conditions;
     (c) The replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary on account of
          obsolescence of technology; and
     (d) The replacement of such asset or equipment has otherwise been allowed by the
          Commission.

     28. Additional Capitalization beyond the original scope:
     28.1 The capital expenditure in respect of existing generating station incurred or
          projected to be incurred on the following counts beyond the original scope, may
          be admitted by the Commission, subject to prudence check:


     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 18
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     (a)   Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions
           of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law;
     (b)   Change in law or compliance of any existing law;
     (c)   Force Majeure Events;
     (d)   Any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher security and
           safety of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or
           statutory authorities responsible for national security/ internal security;
     (e)   Deferred works relating to ash pond or ash handling system in addition to the
           original scope of work, on case to case basis:
              Provided that if any expenditure has been claimed under Renovation and
           Modernisation (R&M) or repairs and maintenance under O&M expenses, the same
           expenditure cannot be claimed under this Regulation; and
     (f)   Usage of water from sewage treatment plant in thermal generating station

41. With regard to additional capitalization, by affidavit dated 13th February, 2025, the
    Respondent No. 1 (MPPMCL) in its response on the subject petition has submitted the
    that the Petitioner has not disclosed the previously approved projections, if any. It is
    further submitted that the necessity for alleged Additional Capitalization has not been
    established by the Petitioner. MPPMCL mentioned that the Petitioner cannot claim
    Additional Capitalization in a mechanical manner for no justifications in every financial
    years throughout the plant life and till this belated stage. The translation of said
    Additional Capitalization that may be reflecting in better plant-performance and
    reduction in tariff has also not been set out in the Petition. It is also submitted that even
    otherwise, some of the said expenses are in the nature of Repairs & Maintenance
    expenses and nevertheless are Capital items.

    Additional capitalization under Thermal Power Stations:
    Commission’s Analysis:
42. In the subject true-up petition, petitioner has filed additional capitalization of Rs.79.61
    Crore during FY 2023-24 in its thermal and hydel power stations. The Commission has
    examined Power Station/House-wise additional capitalization claimed by the petitioner
    in light of the Annual Audited Accounts, Asset-cum-Depreciation Registers, provisions
    under the Regulations, details and documents filed by the petitioner and the
    comments/suggestions filed by the Respondent No 1 and other stakeholder in this
    regard.

43. Vide Commission’s letter dated 31st December, 2024, the petitioner was asked to
    submit various details/documents regarding additional capitalization in its thermal and
    hydel power stations in terms of the Regulations, 2020. By affidavit dated 15th February,
    2025 the petitioner filed its response on the issues of additional capitalization raised by

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 19

     carried out by the Commission is as given below:

     a) Amarkantak Thermal Power Station (ATPS) PH-3:

    on the Annual Audited Accounts.

46. On scrutiny of the aforesaid details filed by the petitioner, it is observed that the
    generating unit of ATPS PH-3 achieved CoD on 10th September, 2009, therefore this
    power station is considered as existing project in terms of Regulation 3 (20) of the
    Regulations, 2020, which provides that the existing project means a project which have
    been declared under commercial operation on a date prior to 01.04.2019. The
    additional capital expenditure claimed in ATPS PH-3 capitalized after the cut-off date of
    the unit. Therefore, the said additional capitalization has been analyzed in terms of
    Regulation 27 of the Regulations, 2020, which provides that “the additional
    capitalization within the original scope of work and after the cut-off date of the project
    may be admitted by the Commission, subject to prudence check”. It is also observed

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 20
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     that the petitioner has claimed some of the assets under additional capitalization
     beyond the original scope of work, hence the aforesaid additional capitalization filed by
     the petitioner has also been analysed in terms of Regulation 28.1 of the Regulations,
     2020.

47. Vide Commission’s letter dated 31st December, 2024, the petitioner was asked to file
    certain details/ documents regarding additional capitalization with respect to ATPS PH-
    3. In response, by affidavit dated 15th February, 2025, the petitioner submitted the such
    details of additional capitalization claimed in ATPS PH-3.

48. The additional capitalization claimed by the petitioner in ATPS PH-3 has been analysed
    under different heads of assets as given below:

        i. Land Owned Under Full Title:-
           Petitioner’s Submission:
           The Additional Capitalization amounting to Rs 0.06 Crore is towards Land Owned
           Under Full Title vide Journal Voucher No. 680658 dated 31.03.2024 under Asset
           Retirement Obligation (ARO) during FY 2023-24.

           As per INDAS-16, an Asset Retirement Obligation is a legal or contractual
           obligation to dismantle and remove an asset and to restore the site in which it is
           located on retirement of a tangible asset. The company estimates its obligation
           for assets retirement based on technical assessment and timing of future
           expenditure for required work considering the inflation & discounting factor.
           Therefore, as per statutory requirement of INDAS-16, MPPGCL has recognized
           the amount of Asset Retirement Obligation and capitalized the same in the Books
           of Accounts for FY 2023-24.

           The aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per proviso 28.1 (b) of MPERC
           Regulations 2020, which provides for additional capitalization on account of
           change in law or compliance of any existing law.

           Commission’s Analysis
           The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.06 Crore towards land
           owned under full title under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 which
           provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on
           account of change in law or compliance of any existing law. It is observed that
           the aforesaid additional capitalization is only book entry with respect to future
           Asset Retirement Obligation in accordance to IND-AS, whereas Commission
           determining tariff under GAAP (Generally Accepted Accounting Principles).
           Therefore, the aforesaid additional capitalization is not covered under Regulation
           28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence, expenditure towards land owned under
           full title is not allowed in this Order.
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 21
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


  ii. Land held under lease:-
      Petitioner’s Submission:
      The Capitalization amounting to Rs 0.05 Crore is towards Land held under Lease

      Retirement Obligation is a legal or contractual obligation to dismantle and remove
      an asset and to restore the site in which it is located on retirement of a tangible
      asset. The company estimates its obligation for assets retirement based on
      technical assessment and timing of future expenditure for required work
      considering the inflation & discounting factor.

      the amount of Asset Retirement Obligation and capitalized the same in the Books
      of Accounts for FY 2023-24. The aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per
      proviso 28.1 (b) of MPERC Regulations 2020, which provides for additional
      capitalization on account of change in law or compliance of any existing law.

      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.05 Crore towards land
      held under lease under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020, which

      account of change in law or compliance of any existing law. It is observed that

      determining tariff under GAAP (Generally Accepted Accounting Principles).

 iii. Drainage and Sewerage Residential Colony :-
      The Capitalization of Rs 0.011 Crore is towards procurement of 5.5KW/ 7.5 HP
      Sq. Cage Induction motor for Aerator Tank of Sewage Treatment Plant (STP),

      aforesaid capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(f) of MPERC Regulations,
      2020 which provides for “Usage of water from sewage treatment plant in thermal
      generating station.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 0.01 Crore towards 5.5
      KW induction motor for aerator tank of drainage and sewerage treatment plant in

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 22
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

      FY 2023-24. The aforesaid additional capitalization is claimed under Regulation
      28.1 (f) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Usage of water from sewage
      treatment plant in thermal generating station”. On perusal of the details filed by
      the petitioner, it is observed that the aforesaid expenditure under additional
      capitalization is made in compliance to Water (prevention & Control of Pollution)
      Act, 1974. It is observed that the assets under additional capitalization incurred
      and capitalized after the cut-off date of the project and beyond the original scope

      and Capitalized Annual Audited Accounts, therefore, same is covered under
      Regulation 28.1 (f) of Regulations, 2020. Hence, the said expenditure is allowed
      in this Order.

 iv. Instrumentation and Controls:-
     Petitioner’s Submission
     These additional Capitalization is booked under Account Code 10.520 in the
     Books of Accounts amounting to Rs 0.18 Crore towards following work orders
     for:-
         a) Supply erection and commissioning of Dry Blow Kits for Atlas Copco make
            Air Compressors amounting to Rs. 0.12 Crore for main plant at 210 MW,
            ATPS, Chachai vide order No. 159 dated 08.04.2022. This new item has
            been added to enhance the performance of existing old Air Compressors.
            The said capitalization is claimed under Proviso 27.2(c) of MPERC
            Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement of such assets or
            equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology.
          b) Procurement of Portable table top Multi Gas Analyzer amounting to Rs.
             0.06 Crore for 210 MW, ATPS, Chachai vide order No. 3279 dated
             07.12.2022). This gas analyzer is required for monitoring of emission
             standards pertaining to PM, SOx & NOx for compliance of AIR (Prevention
             & Control of Pollution) ACT, 1981. The said capitalization is claimed
             Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations, 2020 which provides for
             compliance of existing Law.

          c) Procurement of Digital Vibration meter cum spike energy detector with
             standard accessories amounting to Rs. 0.01 Crore for 210 MW, ATPS,
             Chachai, vide order No. 2906 dated 05.12.2023. The asset has been
             booked in Books of Accounts in Account Code 10.520 against the write-
              off of 01 No. old Portable Vibration meter of 210 MW, ATPS, Chachai.
              The Write-off report of old Portable vibrator meter annexed. Aforesaid
              Capitalization is claimed under proviso 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020
              which provides for replacement of such asset or equipment where the
              useful life is not commensurate with the useful life of the project.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 23
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.18 Crore towards
      Instrumentation and Controls. Aforesaid additional capitalization mainly towards
      Dry Blow Kits for Air Compressors, portable table top Multi Gas Analyzer & digital
      vibration meter cum spike energy detector and are claimed under Regulation
      27.2 (c), 28.1 (b) and 27.2 (a) respectively of the Regulations, 2020. The
      commission has examined aforesaid assets separately in accordance to the
      Regulations, 2020 as below:

      a). Additional capitalization of Rs 0.12 Crore has been claimed towards Supply
          erection and commissioning of Dry Blow Kits for Air Compressors and claimed
          under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides that the
          additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment is
          necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation 27.2 further
          provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
          scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization
          may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in
          the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence
          check.”

          In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the
          said expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation
          27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the
          replacement of existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The
          petitioner has also not justified/established any adjustments in the gross fixed
          assets and the cumulative depreciation towards replacement of existing
          assets in its Asset-cum-Depreciation register as well as its Annual Audited
          Accounts. Hence not allowed in this Order.

      b). Additional capitalization of Rs. 0.06 Crore towards Procurement of Portable
          table top Multi Gas Analyzer under Regulation 28.1 (b) which provides
          additional capital expenditure beyond the original scope of work on account of
          Change in law or compliance of any existing law. The petitioner stated that the
          aforesaid expenditure, i.e., gas analyzer is required for monitoring of emission
          standards pertaining to PM, SOx & NOx and was made towards compliance of
          AIR (Prevention & Control of Pollution) Act, 1981, therefore, the same is
          covered under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence, this
          expenditure of Rs 0.06 Crore is allowed in this Order.

      c). Additional capitalization of Rs 0.01 Crore towards Procurement of Digital
          Vibration meter cum spike energy detector with standard accessories under
          Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides that the
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 24
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

          additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment where
          the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the
          project and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the
          provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case
          of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope of the existing
          project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted by the
          Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets
          and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

          With regard to procurement of digital vibration meter cum spike energy
          detector, although the capitalization has been made against the write-off of
          old assets and necessary adjustments have been made in the Assets cum-
          Depreciation Register, but the Commission is of the view that these minor
          expenses towards procurement of digital vibration meter cum spike energy
          detector are in the nature of O&M expenses, hence, such expenditure is not
          allowed in this order.

  v. Switch Yard:-
     Petitioner’s Submission

      The additional Capitalization under Account Code 10.523 amounting to Rs 0.04
      Crore is towards supply and installation of Control and Relay panel for Islanding
      Panel Scheme at ATPS, Chachai, vide order No. 372 dated 03.05.2023.

      The IEGC / MPEGC, 2019, Part-IV “Protection Code” specifies minimum
      protection requirements for any equipment connected to the State Transmission
      System to minimize disruption due to faults. Where in Under-Frequency/ Islanding
      schemes have been approved by WRPC/MPERC. Under emergency conditions,
      it provides isolation to Grid, thus preventing total Grid collapse and/or enables early
      restoration of power supply. Therefore, serious damage to costly equipment is
      prevented/ avoided.

      In compliance to the above and directions from SLDC, Islanding scheme with
      Control and Replay panels have been implemented at ATPS, Chachai (Grid
      Interface point between State of Madhya Pradesh and State of Chhattisgarh).

      The said capitalization is claimed Proviso 28.1(a) of the Regulations, 2020 which
      provides for compliance of “directions given by Statutory authorities”.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 0.04 Crore towards
      Switch yard under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities to meet award

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 25
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

      of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions of the any statutory
      authority, or order or decree of any court of law”. The petitioner has mentioned
      that the aforesaid capitalization is made towards compliance of IEGC and MPEGC
      and also in compliance to directions of SLDC, Islanding scheme with control and
      relay panels. The IEGC/MPEGC, 2019 Part-IV “Protection Code” specifies
      minimum protection requirements for any equipment connected to the State
      Transmission System to minimize disruption due to faults. Considering above, the
      said expenditure of Rs. 0.04 Crore towards switch yard (which is beyond original
      scope of work) is allowed under Regulation 28.1 (a) in this Order.

                                             MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

      (iii) Supply of 01 No. Laptop Computer, vide order No. 2915 dated 05.12.2023,
            amounting to Rs 0.01 Crore.
      (iv) Supply of 04 No. Laptop Computer, vide order No. GEMC-511687708275347
           dated 09.10.2023 & Voucher No. 286105, amounting to Rs 0.029 Crore.

      where the useful life is not commensurate with the useful life of the project. The
      Survey Reporting/ Write-off of old Desk Top Computers, Laptops, Laser
      Printers, Type writers, Computer accessories at ATPS, Chachai is annexed.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 0.01 Crore towards
      Furniture & Fixtures, Rs. 0.05 Crore towards office equipments & Rs. 0.07 Crore
      towards Computers under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, which
      provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or
      equipment where the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful
      life of the project and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with
      the provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in
      case of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope of the existing
      project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted by the
      Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and
      the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

      In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
      expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (a)
      of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the adjustments of such
      assets in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation and has
      submitted that Survey Reporting/Write-off of old furniture and old AC at ATPS,
      Chachai is in process, hence, not covered under Regulations, 2020. Further, the
      Commission is of the view that the additional expenditure incurred by the
      petitioner towards furniture & fixtures, office equipments are in the nature of
      Operation & Maintenance Expenses. Hence, expenditure towards Furniture &
      Fixtures and office equipments is not allowed in this Order.

      Further, with regard to computers, although the capitalization has been made
      against the write-off of old assets and necessary adjustments have been made
      in the Assets cum-Depreciation Register, but the Commission is of the view that
      these minor expenses towards i.e. computers are in the nature of O&M
      expenses, hence, such expenditure is not allowed in this Order.

56. On perusal of the aforesaid details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed
    that the generating Unit No. 11 of STPS PH-4 achieved CoD on 16.03.2014, hence, the
    Cut-off date of the project in terms of proviso 4.1 (l) of MPERC (Terms and Conditions
    for Determination of Generation Tariff) Regulations, 2015 was 31.03.2017. Therefore,
    this power station is considered as existing project in terms of Regulation 3.1(20) of the
    Regulations, 2020. The additional capitalization claimed in STPS PH-4 is after the cut-
    off date of the project. Therefore, aforesaid additional capitalization needs to be
    examined in light of the Regulation 27 of the Regulations, 2020. It is also observed that
    the petitioner has claimed some of the assets under additional capitalization beyond
    the original scope of work of project, hence, the said additional capitalization needs to
    be examined in light of the Regulation 28.1 of the Regulations, 2020.

           Therefore, as per statutory requirement of INDAS-16, MPPGCL has recognized

           The aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per proviso 28.1 (b) of MPERC

ii.   Pucca Roads:-
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change on 31.12.2021 has
      issued Notification on Ash Utilization from Coal or Lignite Thermal Power
      Stations (Change in Law), wherein every Coal or lignite based thermal Power
      plant is primarily responsible to ensure 100% utilization of Ash generated by it in
      an eco-friendly manner. The earlier notification dated 25.01.2016 at Para 2(b) (9)
      had categorically mentioned that every coal or lignite based thermal power plant
      shall install dedicated dry ash silos having separate access road so as to ease
      delivery of fly ash.

      In compliance to the above, an order was placed on M/s Annapurna Traders for
      RCC road construction, box culvert and road side drains in front of Silos (HCSD
      Pump house) in order to facilitate movement of incoming and outgoing ash lifting
      vehicles at STPS, Sarni vide order No. 942 dated 20.02.2017 amounting to Rs.
      0.96 Crore. Another Order was placed M/s Annapurna Traders for construction
      of RCC Road at chainage (-)200m to chainage 1900m of HCSD extended bund
      for providing proper approach to Ash lifting vehicle, vide order No. 101 dated
      01.05.2023, amounting to 1.91 Crore.

      The total additional Capitalization has been booked under Account Code 10.401,
      amounting to Rs 2.87 Crore, for Pucca Road construction in front of Ash Silo
      The said capitalization is being claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC
      Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Change in law or compliance of any
      existing law”.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs 2.87 Crore towards RCC road
      construction, box culvert and road side drains in front of Silos (HCSD Pump
      house) in order to facilitate movement of incoming and outgoing ash lifting
      vehicles at STPS, Sarni. Aforesaid additional capitalization is claimed under
      Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides for additional capital
      expenditure beyond original scope of work on account of Change in law or

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 31
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

       compliance of any existing law. Since, the aforesaid capitalization is related to ash
       ponds works to facilitate smooth Ash handling and transportation to ensure 100%
       ash utilization as mandated by MoEFCC, these expenses has been done towards
       compliance of MoEF&CC notification towards Ash Utilization, hence, additional
       capitalization of Rs 2.87 Crore towards Pucca Roads is allowed in this Order
       under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020.

iii.   Boiler Plant & Equipments:-
       Petitioner’s Submission
       The capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.501 is procurement of 01 No.
       BHEL Make 6.6KV, 450KW Coal Mill Motor for unit No. 10 & 11 at STPS, Sarni.
       vide order No. 175 dated 09.05.2023, amounting to Rs. 0.65 Crore.

       Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed under proviso 27.2 (a) of MPERC
       Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement of such asset or equipment
       where the useful life is not commensurate with the useful life of the project. The
       Survey report / write-off of old Mill motor is in process.

       Commission’s Analysis:
       The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.65 Crore towards
       replacement of old Mill motor under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020

       (Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016 mandates that no
       person carrying on any industry, operation or process shall discharge or emit or
       be permitted to discharge or emit pollutants. Further, no person shall handle or

       procedure and after complying with such safeguards as may be prescribed. In

       Transformer Oil Filtering machine and evacuation system confirming to IS 6034.
       This Transformer Oil Filtering machine shall be utilized at PH-4, STPS Sarni for
       filtering and recycling Generator Transformer Oil, Station Transformer Oils and
       other transformers oils, thus avoiding discard of used Oil (Hazardous material).

       The Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 and MoEF&CC Rules, therefore
       aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulation

ix.   Chemical Lab Equipment :-
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The Capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.588 amounting to Rs. 0.10
      Crore is towards:
      a) Procurement of 01 No. Portable Hydrogen purity analyzer for Chemical Lab
          (2x250 MW), at STPS, Sarni vide Order No. 1457 dated 06.02.2023,
          amounting to Rs. 0.054 Crore. The Hydrogen analyzer consists of thermal
          conductivity detector, Amplifier board read out range switch, gas flow
          regulator valve, pump and flow indicator.

           Hydrogen analyzer is the necessary equipment of Chemical laboratories for
           determination / analysis of Hydrogen % present in 250MW Generator casing
           (pressurized system).

           The aforesaid capitalization is claimed under regulation 28.1(d) of MPERC
           Regulations 2020, which provides for admittance of any capital expenditure
           to be incurred on account of need for higher security and safety of the plant
           as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory
           authorities.

       b) Procurement of Trace O2 Analyzer for Hydrogen Plant at PH-4 of STPS, Sarni
          vide order No. 489 dated 14.07.2022 amounting to Rs. 0.05 Crore from M/s
          Vortex India, Surat.


M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 38
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

           Hydrogen Generation Plant is very critical as H2 gas is explosive gas if the
           ratio of H2 & O2 gas reaches in explosive zone. Therefore, it is essential to
           monitor O2 to H2 ratio continuously & ensure protection to safeguard of plant,
           machinery and manpower. Hence it was essential to install additional Trace
           O2 analyzer for safe operation of Hydrogen Generation Plant.

           The aforesaid capitalization is claimed under regulation 28.1(d) of MPERC
           Regulations 2020, which provides for admittance of any capital expenditure
           to be incurred on account of need for higher security and safety of the plant
           as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory
           authorities.
      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs. 0.10 Crore towards Chemical lab
      Equipments, the said expenditure claimed by the petitioner under Regulation 28.1
      (d) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides for any capital expenditure to be
      incurred on account of need for higher security and safety of the plant as advised
      or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory authorities
      responsible for national security/ internal security. On perusal of the details and
      documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed that Hydrogen analyzer is the
      necessary equipment of Chemical laboratories for determination / analysis of
      Hydrogen % present in 250 MW Generator casing (pressurized system). Also,
      Hydrogen Generation Plant is very critical as H2 gas is explosive gas if the ratio
      of H2 & O2 gas reaches in explosive zone. Therefore, it is essential to monitor O 2
      to H2 ratio continuously & ensure protection to safeguard of plant, machinery and
      manpower. Considering the above, the same is covered under the Regulation
      28.1 (d) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence, the said expenditure of Rs 0.10 Crore
      towards such assets is allowed in this Order.

 Commission’s Analysis:
a)   The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.01 Crore towards HP
     Multi-Function Machine under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020 which
     provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or
     equipment where the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful
     life of the project and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with
     the provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case

 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 41
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope of the existing project
     after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission
     after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
     depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

     In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
     expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (a) of
     the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the adjustments of such
     assets in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, hence, not
     covered under Regulations, 2020.

b)   The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.014 Crore towards
     commissioning of CCTV Cameras under Regulation 28.1 (a) of Regulations, 2020,
     which provides additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on
     account of Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or
     directions of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law. The
     petitioner has mentioned that the aforesaid capitalization is made towards
     compliance of MP Pollution Control Board as detailed in Consent Order who has
     directed to monitor Sector Specific Air Condition at Power Station by installing and
     regularly operating Outdoor HD Industrial Grade IP Cameras with PTZ features,
     etc., with temper proof mechanism at suitable locations to display all emission
     sources / stack, Coal Yards, Coal conveyors / Crushers and effluent discharge
     points and to ensure its uninterrupted connectivity for remote surveillance.
     Therefore, the said expenditure of Rs 0.014 Crore claimed by the petitioner towards
     cctv cameras is allowed in this Order under Regulation 28.1 (a) of the Regulations,
     2020.

c)   The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.002 Crore towards
     Bullet Wifi Cameras under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities to
     meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions of the any
     statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law read with Regulation
     28.1(d) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides any capital expenditure to be
     incurred on account of need for higher security and safety of the plant as advised
     or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory authorities
     responsible for national security/ internal security”. The petitioner informed that the
     aforesaid capitalization were procured and installation was done nearby Road
     Weigh Bridge for more monitoring for vigilance & security purpose in Coal Handing
     Plant area of Ph-4 Sarni towards the compliance order by MP Pollution control
     Board as detailed in Consent Order. Considering above, the expenditure of Rs.



 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 42
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

       0.002 Crore has been allowed under Regulation 28.1 (a) and 28.1(d) of the
       Regulations, 2020.

       Exhaust Fans under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides
       that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment where
       the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project
       and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of
       these Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of
       assets deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date,
       the additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making
       necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation,
       subject to prudence check.”

       In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
       expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (a) of

       water Cooler and Rs 0.004 Crore towards printers under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the
       Regulations, 2020 which provides that the additional capitalization towards
       replacement of assets or equipment where the useful life of the assets is not
       commensurate with the useful life of the project and such assets have been fully
       depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations. Regulation
       27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the
       original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization
       may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the
       gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

       With regard to water coolers and printers, although the capitalization has been
       made against the write-off of old assets and necessary adjustments have been
       made in the Assets cum-Depreciation Register, but the Commission is of the view
       that these minor expenses towards water coolers and printers are in the nature of
       O&M expenses, hence, such expenditure of Rs 0.006 Crore and Rs 0.004 Crore is
       not allowed in this Order.

xii.    Computers: -
       The Capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.905 amounting to Rs. 0.02
       Crore is towards purchase order of 02 Nos. all in one computer configuration, with
 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 43
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

          MS office genuine license for O/o chief chemist at STPS, Sarni vide order No. 717
          dated 14.09.2023. These purchases have been made as replacement against
          write-off of old computers.

         The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (a) of MPERC
         Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement of asset when the useful life of
         the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project
     Commission’s Analysis:
     The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.01 Crore towards Furniture
     and Fixtures, Rs. 0.04 Crore towards other office equipments and Rs 0.02 Crore
     towards Computers under Regulation 27.2 (a) and Regulation 28.1 (a) of the
     Regulations, 2020.

     The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization towards Computers under
     Regulation 27.2 (a) which provides that the additional capitalization towards
     replacement of assets or equipment where the useful life of the assets is not
     commensurate with the useful life of the project and such assets have been fully
     depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2
     further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
     scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
     admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
     assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

     With regard to computers, although the capitalization has been made against the write-
     off of old assets and necessary adjustments have been made in the Assets cum-
     Depreciation Register, but the Commission is of the view that these minor expenses
     towards computers are in the nature of O&M expenses, hence, such expenditure
     towards computers is not allowed in this order.

      Petitioner’s Submission
      The Statutory compliance of Indian Accounting Standards (INDAS) is mandatory on
      MPPGCL. Based on said standards, the Expenditure on Major inspection/capital
      Overhaul- Boiler/ Turbine was capitalized in the Audited books of Accounts of MPPGCL
      in FY 2023-24 at STPS, Sarni under Account Code 11.603 amounting to Rs 03.69
      Crore is towards:
       a) Work order for overhauling of LP Turbine, bearing inspection and associated
          works, overhauling of Generator & Excitation and overhauling of IP Turbine during
          CoH of Unit No. 11 at STPS PH-4, Sarni, amounting to Rs 1.69 Crore. The copies

    1545 dated 07.11.2022. The schedule of the Order as detailed below:

     i) Complete Repairing and refitting of 04 Nos. tyres of 12’-00’’ dia X 27’-00’’ long

    schedule is detailed as below:

           Valvoline premium blue 7800 plus 1/55 LTR amounting to Rs 0.30 Crore.

     In above mentioned works, an amount of Rs. 0.30 Crore has been spend against
     replacement of old Capital Asset by new one. The Write-off of old asset is in
     progress.

  d) Work of Capital overhauling of 1800mm wide x 13920mm long Apron Feeders of
     Wagon Tippler of CHP at STPS PH-4, Sarni, order placed on M/s Black Diamond
     Equipment Pvt. Ltd., Korba (C.G.), vide order No. 149 dated 28.04.2023 amounting
     to Rs. 0.85 Crore.
     Aforesaid Capitalization towards Capital Overhauling (COH) is claimed as per with
     Regulation 27.2 (a) of MPERC Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement
     of Assets when the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful
     life of the project read with Regulation 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations, 2020 which
     provides for “Additional capitalization on account of Change in law or compliance
     of any existing law”.
Commission’s Analysis:
The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 3.69 Crore towards Capital
Overhaul Expenditure under Regulation 27.2 (a) and Regulation 28.1 (b) of the
Regulations, 2020. Regulation 27.2 (a) provides that the additional capitalization
towards replacement of assets or equipment where the useful life of the assets is not
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 45
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     commensurate with the useful life of the project and such assets have been fully
     depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2
     further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
     scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
     admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
     assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

     In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
     expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (a) of the
     Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the adjustments of such assets in
     the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation and mentioned that the write off
     of old assets is in progress. Further, the petitioner has also not justified/established any
     adjustments in the gross fixed assets and cumulative depreciation towards replacement
     of existing assets in its Asset-cum-Depreciation register as well as its Annual Audited
     Accounts and mentioned that the write-off of old asset is in progress. Hence,
     expenditure towards Capital Overhauling is not allowed under Regulation 27.2 (a) in
     this Order at this stage.

     The aforesaid additional capitalization of Rs. 3.69 Crore towards Capital Overhaul
     Expenditure is also claimed under Regulation 28.1 (b) which provides for additional
     capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on account of change in law or
     compliance of any existing law. It is observed that the aforesaid additional capitalization
     towards capital overhaul of equipments is only book entry, due to change in accounting
     practices under Indian Accounting Standards (IS), whereas, the Commission is
     determining tariff under GAAP. The said expenditure of Rs. 3.69 Crore claimed by the
     petitioner also does not fall under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. It is
     also observed that petitioner is unable to justify aforementioned claim of additional
     expenditure under the Regulations, 2020. Hence, expenditure towards Capital
     Overhaul Expenditure is not allowed in this Order.

   Mono Oxide analyzer) for measurement of CO in flue gas at Economizer outlet left
   and right of unit No. 5, 1x500 MW, at SGTPS, Birsinghpur, vide order No. 2580
   dated 18.10.2022.
    For efficient combustion to occur, the monitoring of O 2% and CO at Economizer
    outlet, left and right, is one of the most essential requirements. At present, supply
    of excess air is being controlled by monitoring only O2% at Economizer outlet, left
    and right, through air flow auto loop, but it is not sufficient for proper monitoring of
    combustion. Therefore, procurement of a CO analyzer for an amount of Rs. 0.81
    Crore has been done.

    The above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of
    The AIR (PREVENTION & CONTROL OF POLLUTION) ACT, 1981, wherein
    continuous monitoring of level of pollutants (such as Carbon Mono-Oxide, etc.) is
    required on 8 hours basis. Therefore, aforesaid capitalization is claimed under
    proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations 2020, which provides for “change in law or
    compliance of any existing law”.
