     Commission’s Analysis:

67. The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 2.65 Crore towards
    Instrumentation and Controls. Aforesaid additional capitalization is claimed under
    Regulation 27.2(a) & (c) and 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020.

           a). The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.61 Crore towards
               up-gradation and revival of PLC system of Fire hydrant and Spray system
               under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that the
               additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment is
               necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation 27.2 further
               provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
               scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization
               may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in
               the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence
               check”.

               In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the
               said expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation
               27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the
               replacement of existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The
               petitioner has also not justified/established any adjustments in the gross fixed
               assets and the cumulative depreciation towards replacement of existing assets
               in its Asset-cum-Depreciation register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts.

               With regards to write-off of old assets, the petitioner mentioned that the old
               assets is in process of write-off. Hence, expenditure towards up-gradation and
               revival of Fire hydrant and Spray system is not allowed in this Order at this
               stage under Regulation 27.2(a) of the Regulation, 2020. The petitioner is at
               liberty to approach the Commission as and when, the old assets gets written-
               off in Annual Audited Accounts and Asset-cum-Depreciation register.

           b). The petitioner has claimed Rs 0.81 Crore under additional capitalization
              towards supply, installation and commissioning of a CO2 analyser under
              Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations, 2020 which provides for additional capital
              expenditure beyond original scope of work on account of Change in law or
              compliance of any existing law. The petitioner has mentioned that this
              capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of the AIR (Prevention
              & Control of Pollution) Act, 1981. It is observed that the petitioner has not
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 51
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

          specified any specific provision/clause of the AIR (Prevention & Control of
          Pollution) Act, 1981 under which additional capitalization is claimed. The said
          expenditure of Rs 0.81 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the
          Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not
          demonstrated any change in law event or compliance with existing law in
          relation to aforesaid AIR Act, 1981. Hence, expenditure towards procurement
          of a CO2 analyser is not allowed in this Order.

      c). The petitioner has claimed Rs 0.11 Crore under additional capitalization
          towards supply, installation & commissioning of different types of Flow meters
          & Transmitters at various location like water/ slurry pipe lines under
          Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations, 2020, which provides for additional capital
          expenditure beyond original scope of work on account of Change in law or
          compliance of any existing law. The petitioner has mentioned that this
          capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of the Water
          (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974. It is observed that the
          petitioner has not mentioned /specified any specific provision/clause of the
          Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974 under which additional
          capitalization is claimed. The said expenditure of Rs 0.11 Crore claimed by
          the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations,
          2020 as the petitioner has not demonstrated any change in law event or
          compliance with existing law in relation to aforesaid Water (Prevention and
          Control of Pollution) Act, 1974. Hence, the additional capitalization of Rs 0.11
          Crore towards supply, installation & commissioning of Flow meters &
          Transmitters is not allowed under Regulation 28.1 (b) in this Order.

      d). The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.56 Crore towards
          up-gradation of UPS system under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations,
          2020 which provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of
          assets or equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology.
          Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets
          deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the
          additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making
          necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
          depreciation, subject to prudence check”.

          With regards to write-off of old assets, the petitioner mentioned that the old
          assets is in process of write-off. Hence, expenditure towards UPS system for
          up-gradation is not allowed in this Order at this stage under Regulation 27.2
          (c) of the Regulation, 2020. The petitioner is at liberty to approach the
          Commission as and when, the old UPS system gets written-off in Annual
          Audited Accounts and Asset-cum-Depreciation register.
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 52
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


       e). The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.54 Crore towards
           up-gradation of PLC System under Regulation 27.2 (a) and 27.2 (c) of the
           Regulations, 2020 which provides that the additional capital expenditure
           towards the replacement of assets where useful life of the assets is not

           Further Regulation 27.2 (c) provides that the additional capital expenditure

        10.04.2023. out of which 1 No. Dell intel Core Laptop is used for Common
        Services. Therefore, booking of amount of Rs. 0.0023 Crore has been
        considered on share basis pertaining to Unit No. 5 at SGTPS.
        The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (a) of MPERC
        Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement of asset when the useful life
        of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project.
  Commission’s Analysis:
  The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.02 Crore towards different
  type of Computers under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides
  that the additional capital expenditure towards the replacement of assets where useful
  life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project and such
  assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these
  Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets
  deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the
  additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary
  adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to
  prudence check.”

  In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
  expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (a) of
  the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the adjustment of such assets

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 54
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

       in the Gross Fixed Assets and the cumulative depreciation, hence is not covered
       under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulation, 2020. Further, the Commission is of the
       view that the additional expenditure incurred by the petitioner towards computers is
       in the nature of Operation & Maintenance Expenses. Hence, expenditure towards
       computers is not allowed in this Order.

  iii. Capital Spares:-
       Petitioner’s Submission
       Capital spares amounting to Rs. 0.048 Crore pertaining to 2x105KVA UPS
       system have been purchased vide order No. 4740 dated 07.03.2023 along with
       the new UPS purchased as replacement to old UPS system and have been
       booked under Account Code 11.300, and capitalized in FY 2023-24 in the Audited
       Books of Accounts. The same is considered in the instant True up petition.
       The CoD of SGTPS PH-3 (1x500MW), Birsinghpur Unit is 28.08.2008; therefore,
       this unit is governed by MPERC - Terms and Conditions for determination of
       Generation Tariff Regulations, 2005 wherein there is no provision of Cut-off Date.
       The Regulation 2.5 (i) specifies Initial spare upto 2.5% of original capital Cost in
       case of coal based generating stations.
       Commission’s Analysis:
       Petitioner has claimed capital spares of Rs 0.05 Crore under Regulation 2.5(i) of
       Regulation, 2005, whereas Commission has dealt with the additional capitalization of
       capital spares under Regulation 26.1 (iii) of 2020 which provides that “procurement of
       initial capital spares within the original scope of work upto cut-off date in accordance
       with the provisions of Regulation 25”. Since, the capital spares of Rs. 0.05 Crore are
       claimed by the petitioner during FY 2023-24 are capitalized after the cut-off date of
       the project and this amount is not covered under the scope of Regulation 26.1 (iii),
       hence, not allowed in this Order.

68. In view of the above, the Commission has observed that none of the additional
    capitalization claimed by the petitioner in SGTPS PH-3, Birsinghpur is covered under
    the Regulations, 2020.

72. Vide Commission’s letter dated 31st December, 2024, the petitioner was asked to file
    certain details/ documents regarding the additional capitalization in SSTPP PH-1. By
    affidavit dated 15th February, 2025, the petitioner filed its response. The additional
    capitalization claimed by the petitioner in SSTPP PH-1 has been analysed under
    different heads of assets separately as given below:



     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 56
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

 i.   Land owned under full title:-
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The Capitalization under Account Code 10.101 amounting to Rs 2.98 Crore is
      towards Land Owned Under Full Title, vide Journal Voucher No. 680393 dated
      31.03.2024.
      As per INDAS-16, an Asset Retirement Obligation is a legal or contractual
      obligation to dismantle and remove an asset and to restore the site in which it is
      located on retirement of a tangible asset. The company estimates its obligation for
      assets retirement based on technical assessment and timing of future expenditure
      for required work considering the inflation & discounting factor.

      Therefore, as per statutory requirement of INDAS-16, MPPGCL has recognized
      the amount of Asset Retirement Obligation and capitalized the same in the Books
      of Accounts for FY 2023-24.

      The aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per proviso 28.1 (b) of MPERC
      Regulations 2020, which provides for additional capitalization on account of
      change in law or compliance of any existing law.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 2.98 Crore towards
      land owned under full title under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020
      which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work
      on account of change in law or compliance of any existing law. It is observed
      that the aforesaid additional capitalization is only due to change in book entry
      with respect to future Asset Retirement Obligation in accordance to IND-AS,
      whereas, Commission determined tariff under GAAP (Generally Accepted
      Accounting Principles). Therefore, the aforesaid additional capitalization is not
      covered under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence,
      expenditure towards land owned under full title is not allowed in this Order.

ii.   Land held under lease:
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The Capitalization under Account Code 10.102 amounting to Rs 0.20 Crore is
      towards Land Owned Land under lease, vide Journal Voucher No. 680393
      dated 31.03.2024.

      As per INDAS-16, an Asset Retirement Obligation is a legal or contractual
      obligation to dismantle and remove an asset and to restore the site in which it
      is located on retirement of a tangible asset. The company estimates its
      obligation for assets retirement based on technical assessment and timing of
      future expenditure for required work considering inflation & discounting factor.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 57
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


       Therefore, as per statutory requirement of INDAS-16, MPPGCL has recognized
       the amount of Asset Retirement Obligation and capitalized the same in the
       Books of Accounts for FY 2023-24. The aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as
       per proviso 28.1 (b) of MPERC Regulations 2020, which provides for additional
       capitalization on account of change in law or compliance of any existing law.

       Commission’s Analysis:
       The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.20 Crore towards
        land held under lease under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020
        which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work
        on account of change in law or compliance of any existing law. It is observed
        that the aforesaid additional capitalization is only due to change in book entry
        with respect to future Asset Retirement Obligation in accordance to IND-AS,
        whereas Commission determined tariff under GAAP (Generally Accepted
        Accounting Principles). Therefore, the aforesaid additional capitalization is not
        covered under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence,
        expenditure towards land held under lease is not allowed in this Order.

iii.   Other buildings:-
       Petitioner’s Submission
       The Capitalization under Account Code 10.233 amounting to Rs 0.47 Crore is
       towards other buildings as detailed below:
       a) Construction of Shed for Motor Maintenance Section EMD-1 in front of Service
          Building at SSTPP, Khandwa, vide order No. 807 dated 04.02.2022 amounting
          to Rs. 0.43 Crore. The shed was constructed in the plant area for Repair &
          Maintenance and Testing of various Heavy Motors installed in PH-1, locally
          within the Power Station, as the required arrangement was not available for
          trial and maintenance. Hence, the shed was constructed to meet out the said
          requirement.

       b) Construction of Radiography Source Storage Room (pit room) for unit No. 1 &
          2 at SSTPP, Khandwa vide order No. 283 dated 23.06.2023 amounting to Rs.
          0.036 Crore.
          The Boiler maintenance division (BMD) has a requirement of a source
          storage room (pit room) for storing radiography source (camera). To carry
          out the radiography testing work during forced outage of the units and the
          radiography testing agency must be available at the site along with
          machinery all times to avoid any delay from the testing side. Hence a source
          storage rooms was required to be constructed at minus level for storing
          Radiography Source (Camera), so that radiation of source could not affect
          human being and also comply to the Rule 20(2) of Atomic Energy (Radiation

 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 58
                                             MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

         Protection) Rules, 2004, which state that the user shall ensure physical
         security of the sources at all times i.e. ensuring security for Industrial
         Gamma Radiography Exposure Devices (IGREDs) during their use, storage
         and during transport. Therefore, it is a statutory requirement to keep the
         Source of Radiography at a safe place.
         Thus, the above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of
         directives of CEA, therefore aforesaid capitalization is claimed under
         proviso 28.1(b) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides for “change in law
         or compliance of any existing law”.

    Commission’s Analysis:
    Additional capitalization of Rs 0.47 Crore towards Other buildings is claimed by
    the petitioner under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 are as under:-

     (a) The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.43 Crore
         towards Construction of Shed for Motor Maintenance Section under
         Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations, 2020 which provides for additional
         capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on account of Change in
         law or compliance of any existing law. The petitioner has mentioned that
         this capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of CEA. The
         said expenditure of Rs 0.43 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall
         under the Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner
         has not demonstrated any change in law event or compliance with existing
         law in relation to CEA directives. It is observed that the petitioner has
         neither filed any specific directives of the CEA nor enclosed copy of such
         directives under which such additional capitalization is claimed. Hence,
         expenditure towards Construction of Shed is not allowed in this Order.

     (b) The petitioner has further claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.036
         Crore towards Construction of Radiography Source Storage Room under
         Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations, 2020 which provides additional capital
         expenditure beyond original scope of work on account of change in law or
         compliance of any existing law”. The petitioner stated that aforesaid
         expenditure was made towards compliance of Rule 20(2) of Atomic Energy
         Radiation Protection Rules, 2004 which stated that the user shall ensure
         physical security of the sources at all times, i.e. ensuring security for
         industrial Gamma Radiography exposure device during their use, storage
         and during transport. Therefore, it is a statutory requirement to keep the
         source of Radiography at a safe place. The same is covered under
         Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020, hence, this expenditure of Rs
         0.036 Crore is allowed under regulation 28.1(b) in this Order.


M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 59
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

iv.   Railway sidings:-
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The additional capitalization under Account Code 10.412 amounting to Rs 0.88
      Crore is towards Construction of “Railway Under Bridges-RUB” carried out
      through Deposit work.

      The Ministry of Railway, Government of India, vide No. 2013/W-I/Genl./30 pt-II
      dated 18.06.2013 created a policy regarding elimination of level crossing on New
      Rail line, Gauge Conversion and Doubling Projects, all over India for ensuring
      speed and safety point of view. The Railway Board has further simplified the
      procedure & policy regarding provision of accommodation works, i.e,. Diversion
      Road, Subway, RUB, ROB, Level Crossings, in case of new line, gauge
      conversion and doubling projects

      As per the Private Siding Agreement signed between Western Central Railway
      and MPPGCL at clause 9 of Annexure-3 (page-29), it has been directed by
      Railways to eliminate level crossing and build RUB/ROB/sub way, etc., by
      MPPGCL. The relevant page of the agreement is annexed.

      Thus, the above-mentioned capitalization at SSTPP, Khandwa is made towards
      compliance of directives of Ministry of Indian Railways, Government of India.
      Accordingly, aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC
      Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of the any
      statutory authorities”.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.88 Crore towards
      construction of Railway Under Bridges under Regulation 28.1 (a) of
      Regulations, 2020, which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond
      original scope of work on account of Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or
      for compliance of the order or directions of the any statutory authority, or order
      or decree of any court of law. The petitioner submitted that as per the Private
      Siding Agreement signed between Western Central Railway and MPPGCL at
      clause (9), it has been directed by Railways to eliminate level crossing and build
      RUB/ROB/sub way, etc. The Ministry of Indian Railways, Government of India,
      dated 18.06.2013 issued a policy regarding elimination of level crossing on New
      Rail line, Gauge Conversion and Doubling Projects, all over India for ensuring
      speed and safety point of view. As such, additional capitalization of these assets
      is covered under statutory requirement. Therefore, the said expenditure of Rs
      0.88 Crore claimed by the petitioner towards Railway Under Bridges-RUB is
      allowed in this Order under Regulation 28.1 (a) of the Regulations, 2020.

v.    Boundary wall for civil building:-
       Petitioner’s Submission
       The Capitalization under Account Code 10.420 amounting to Rs 0.40 Crore is
       towards Construction of Boundary wall around plant area of SSTPP, Khandwa.

       Work for construction of boundary wall around the plant area was awarded, vide
       order No. 4555 dated 07.08.2012, on M/s K.P. Mishra Engineers & contractor.
       Though the Boundary Wall was constructed in time, however, there was delay in
       passing the bills as the contractor was not accepting measurement and therefore
       not signing the RA Bills, delay in submission of royalty clearance and also as
       GST was introduced in India in July, 2017. As such the tax invoice was not
       submitted. The bills have now been cleared and accordingly the assets have
       been capitalized.

       The said capitalization is claimed under Proviso 27.1 (iv) of MPERC Regulations,
       2020, which provides for “Liabilities for works executed prior to the cut-off date”.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.40 Crore towards
      construction of boundary wall under Regulation 27.1 (iv) of Regulation 2020
      which provides for additional capitalization within the original scope and after
      the cut-off date on account of Liability for works executed prior to the cut-off
      date. On perusal of the details filed by the petitioner, it is observed that the
      aforesaid asset under additional capitalization actually made after the cut-off
      date of the project. The petitioner submitted that construction work of boundary
      wall was completed on time, however, delay in payment was on account of
      some measurement and GST issues, therefore the expenditure of Rs 0.40
      Crore covered under the Regulation 27.1 (iv) of Regulations, 2020. Hence,
      additional capitalization of Rs 0.40 Crore towards construction of boundary wall
      is allowed in this Order under Regulation 27.1 (iv) of the Regulations, 2020.

 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 61
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


        Therefore, an action plan has been prepared to control the seepage water by the

        recycled to the recovery system of ash disposal (Ash Water Recirculation
        System-AWRS), so that the target of Zero Liquid Discharge is achieved for

vii.   Coal handling plant and handling equipments:-
       Petitioner’s Submission
       The Capitalization under Account Code 10.515 amounting to Rs 0.84 Crore is
       towards:-
       a) Supply, installation and commissioning of 01 No. new In-motion-Weigh-Bridge
          (IMWB) having 140 MT Capacity as per RDSO specification No. WD-29-MISC-
           19 on EPC basic with all accessories and associated system on Rail Line
           between Bir Railway station to Power House at SSTPP, Khandwa, vide order
           No. 6310 dated 05.08.2022, placed on M/s Weightrack India Private Limited,
           Bhubaneshwar, amounting to 0.51 Crore.

           As per the Private Siding Agreement signed between Western Central Railway
           and MPPGCL at clause 9 of Annexure-3 (page-29), it has been directed by
           Railways to install and operate electronic in-motion weigh bridge house and
           weigh bridge siding by MPPGCL. The relevant page of the agreement is
           annexed for reference.
  M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 62
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

         The coal rakes arriving at SSTPP plant are being accurately weighed. This is
         essential for justification of coal quantity mentioned in Railway Receipt (RR)
         and useful for coal accounting which ultimately helps for preparation of Form
         TPS-15 and to workout landed cost of coal.
         The said capitalization is claimed under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC
         Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “compliance of order or
         directions of the any statutory authorities”.

     b) Order for supply of Portable Hydraulic Oil Filter Machine (online filtration
        system) for Servo system 68 grade Hydraulic oil for various power packs of
        2x600 MW, CHP, at SSTPP, Khandwa, placed on M/s Absolute Engineers,
        Bhiwandi (MH), vide order No. 9798 dated 05.12.2023 amounting to Rs. 0.05
        Crore.
         The 2x600 MW coal Handling Plant of SSTPP consists of various hydraulic
        equipment, auxiliaries, various types and different sizes of hydraulic power
        packs, which are in service since last 11 years. CHP-I consist of various
        equipment, like wagon tippler, side arm charger, apron feeder, stacker-
        reclaimer etc for coal rake unloading, evacuation of coal from hopper and
        stacking/ reclaiming purpose, having hydraulic system. All equipment are
        running under coal dust atmosphere and as a result the hydraulic oil which
        is in continuous use gets contaminated. Life of equipment power pack mainly
        depends on oil quality being used. Contaminated oil will affect smooth
        running of equipment and the equipment will get wear & tear. Therefore,
        filtration of oil is essential for enhancing the life of oil and to reduce the
        consumption of hydraulic oil. If oil is filtered from time to time, service life of
        oil will definitely be improved and it will reduce wear & tear of hydraulic parts
        & shall help to run all the auxiliaries smoothly.

        This portable Oil Filtering machine shall be utilized at SSTPP PH-1,
        Khandwa for filtering and recycling various Hydraulic oils in CHP area thus
        avoiding discard of used Oil (Hazardous material).

        The abovementioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives
        of The Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 and MoEF&CC Rules, therefore
        aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC
        Regulations 2020, which provides for “change in law or compliance of any
        existing law”.

    c). Order for supply of 01 No. Hammer type Pulverizer for coal sampling lab at
        SSTPP, Khandwa placed on M/s Ultimate Analytics, Kolkata, vide order No.
        10300 dated 18.12.2023, amounting to Rs. 0.013 Crore.



M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 63
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        The above item has been procured for Coal sampling purpose. For
        determination of GCV of coal “as received” at Power Stations, the samples of
        coal are taken as per the Bureau of Indian Standards IS:436 (Part1/Sec1)-1964
        wherein at clause 10.3 procedure of sample preparation has been specified. The
        procedure mentions that for Small Coal – the gross sample shall be crushed to
        3.55 mm in two steps, namely through 12.5mm by a Jaw crusher and finally to
        3.55 mm by a Palmac (Pulverizer) type reduction mill then the lumps are
        pulverized to meet the requirements for further testing.

        The above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of Bureau of
        Indian Standards IS:436 (Part1/Sec1)-1964 Methods for sampling of Coal &
        Coke, therefore aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of
        Regulations 2020, which provides for “change in law or compliance of any
        existing law”.

    d). The Capitalization under Account Code 10.515 amounting to Rs 0.27 Crore is
        towards Gap billing claimed by M/s L&T on account of Coal Handling Plant &
        equipment, vide voucher No. 680406 dated 31.03.2024.

        The said capitalization is claimed under Proviso 27.1 (iv) of the Regulations,
        2020 which provides for “Liabilities for works executed prior to the cut-off date.”


    Commission’s Analysis:
    Additional capitalization of Rs 0.84 Crore towards Coal handling plant and
    handling equipments has been claimed under Regulation 28.1 (a) & (b) and
    Regulation 27.1 (iv) of the Regulations, 2020 and analysis of the same are as
    follows:-.
         a) The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.51 Crore
            towards Supply, installation and commissioning of new in-motion-Weigh-
            Bridge under Regulation 28.1 (a) of Regulations, 2020 which provides
            additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on account
            of Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or
            directions of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of
            law. The petitioner submitted that as per the Private Siding Agreement
            signed between Western Central Railway and MPPGCL at clause 9, it has
            been directed by Railways to install and operate electronic in-motion
            weigh bridge house and weigh bridge siding by MPPGCL. Hence, Supply,
            installation and procurement/commissioning of electronic In-motion-
            Weigh-Bridge falls under statutory liabilities as part of agreement signed
            by MPPGCL with central government body, i.e., Railway and is now
            binding on the MPPGCL to fullfil the terms and conditions of Amendment.
            Therefore, the said expenditure of Rs 0.51 Crore claimed by the petitioner

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 64
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

               towards procurement of In-motion-Weigh-Bridge is allowed in this Order
               under Regulation 28.1 (a) of the Regulations, 2020.

          b) The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.05 Crore towards Portable Hydraulic Oil
             Filter Machine (online filtration system) under Regulation 28.1 (b) of
             Regulations, 2020 which provides for additional capital expenditure
             beyond original scope of work on account of Change in law or compliance
             of any existing law. The petitioner stated that aforesaid item shall be
             utilised for filtering and recycling generator transformer oil, station
             transformer oils and other transformer oils, thus avoiding discard of used
             oil (Hazardous material). Since, this capitalization is made towards
             compliance of directives of the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 read
             with Hazardous and Other Wastes (Management and Transboundary
             Movement) Rules, 2016, therefore, the same is covered under Regulation
             28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 0.05
             Crore towards such asset is allowed in this Order.

          c) The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.013 Crore towards Hammer type
             Pulverizer for coal sampling lab under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the
             Regulations, 2020 which provides for additional capital expenditure
             beyond original scope of work on account of Change in law or compliance
             of any existing law. The petitioner has mentioned that this capitalization is
             made towards compliance of Bureau of Indian Standards IS:436
             (Part1/Sec1)-1964 Methods for sampling of Coal and compliance of
             Bureau of Indian Standards. Considering above, aforesaid expenditure of
             Rs 0.013 Crore towards hammer type pulverizer is allowed in this Order.

          d) The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs 0.27 Crore Coal Handling
             Plant & equipment under Regulation 27.1 (iv) of Regulations, 2020 which
             provides for additional capital expenditure within the original scope and
             after the cut-off date on account of Liability for works executed prior to the
             cut-off date. On perusal of the details filed by the petitioner, it is observed
             that the aforesaid asset under additional capitalization is actually made
             after the cut-off date of the project. The petitioner submitted that
             procurement of coal handling plant and equipments was completed on
             time, however, delay in payment was on account of gap billing, therefore
             the expenditure of Rs 0.27 Crore is covered under the Regulation 27.1 (iv)
             of the Regulations, 2020. Therefore, additional capitalization of Rs 0.27
             Crore towards Coal Handling Plant & equipment is allowed in this Order.

viii.   Instrumentation And Control:-
        Petitioner’s Submission
        The Capitalization under Account Code 10.520 amounting to Rs 0.45 Crore is
        towards order for supply of Chloride analyzer, chiller along with dynamic
        calibrator and one-year Reagents installed in SWAS system for 2X600 MW,
        SSTPP, Khandwa, vide order No. 2267 dated 25.05.2023.

        The old Chloride analyzer Thermo scientific make, Orion 1817L, is declared
        obsolete by OEM and the spares support is discontinued by the OEM. The
        Survey reporting / write-off of old Chloride analyzer is in process.

        The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (c) of MPERC
        Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement of such assets or
        equipments is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology.

         Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.45 Crore towards supply
        of Chloride analyzer under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020, which
        provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or
        equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation 27.2
        further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
        scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
        admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
        assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

        With regards to write-off of old Chloride analyzer, the petitioner mentioned that the
        old Chloride analyzer is in process of write-off. Hence, expenditure towards old
        Chloride analyzer is not allowed in this Order at this stage under Regulation 27.2(c)
        of the Regulation, 2020. The petitioner is at liberty to approach the Commission as
        and when, the these old chloride analyzer gets written-off in Annual Audited
        Accounts and Asset-cum-Depreciation register.

x.   Chemical Lab Equipment.
       Petitioner’s Submission
     The Capitalization under Account Code 10.588 amounting to Rs 0.01 Crore is
     towards order for supply of Portable Turbidity Meter for Chemistry Lab vide
     order No. 366 dated 12.04.2023 placed on M/s ChromQ Instruments (OPC) Pvt.
     Ltd., Indore.

     This item has been added for testing of Drinking water and DM water in Power
     Plants. The Bureau of Indian Standards IS 10500:2012 specify the Organoleptic
     and Physical Parameters of Safe Drink Water. Any deviation from set limits
     results in water quality not suitable. Similarly, the Water (Prevention & control
     of Pollution) Act, 1974 and standards prescribed by the MP Pollution Control
     board also provides that Industry shall provide adequate facility for treatment of
     industrial waste water, including the bleed from boiler house and domestic to
     ensure that the treated effluent quality meets the standards. Therefore, for
     carrying out site testing of water quality, Portable Turbidity Meter is utmost
     essential.

     The said capitalization is claimed Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC Regulations, 2020
     which provides for compliance of “directions given by Statutory authorities.’

     Commission’s Analysis:
     The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.01 Crore towards
     Chemical lab Equipments under Regulation 28.1 (a) of Regulations, 2020 which
     provides additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on
     account of Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order
     or directions of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of
     law. The petitioner stated that as per the Water (Prevention & control of
     Pollution) Act, 1974 and standards prescribed by the MP Pollution Control

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 67
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

      board, Industry shall provide adequate facility for treatment of industrial waste
      water, including the bleed from boiler house and domestic to ensure that the
      treated effluent quality meets the standards. Therefore, for carrying out site
      testing of water quality, Portable Turbidity Meter is utmost essential. Since, this
      capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of The Bureau of Indian
      Standards IS 10500:2012 and Water (Prevention & control of Pollution) Act,
      1974, therefore, the same is covered under Regulation 28.1 (a) of the
      Regulations, 2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 0.01 Crore towards Chemical
      lab Equipments is allowed in this Order.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 70
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (a) of MPERC
     Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement when the useful life of the
     assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project.

    Commission’s Analysis:
    The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.18 Crore towards
    Others office Equipments under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020
    which provides that the additional capital expenditure towards the replacement of
    assets if the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of
    the project and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the
    provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of
    replacement of assets deployed under the original scope of the existing project
    after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission
    after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
    depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

    Further, the petitioner has also claimed some other office equipments under
    Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or
    for compliance of the order or directions of the any statutory authority, or order or
    decree of any court of law. Petitioner has also claimed some of the office
    equipments under Regulation 28.1 (b) which provides that any capital expenditure
    to be incurred on account of Change in law or compliance of any existing law &
    Regulation 28.1(d) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides that any capital
    expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher security and safety of the
    plant as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory
    authorities responsible for national security/ internal security.

    In view of the above provision under the respective Regulations, it is observed
    that the said expenditure claimed by the petitioner not fall under any clauses of
    the Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, as the petitioner mentioned
    that the old assets will write-off shortly. With regard to assets claimed under
    Regulation 27.2 (a), the petitioner has not justified/established any adjustments
    in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation towards replacement
    of existing assets in its Asset-cum-Depreciation register as well as its Annual
    Audited Accounts.

    Regarding the assets claimed under Regulation 28.1(b), the petitioner has not
    filed any specific law and/or compliance of existing law. For the assets claimed
    under Regulation 28.1 (d) the petitioner has not filed any advise or directive by
    appropriate Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for
    national security/internal security. The Commission is of the view that the
    additional expenditure incurred by the petitioner towards office equipments is in

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 71
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        the nature of Operation & Maintenance Expenses. Hence, expenditure towards
        other office equipments is not allowed in this Order at this stage.

             Khandwa, vide order No. 5790 dated 14.08.2023 and 01 No. Cooler Bill No.

              Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement when the useful life of the

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.07 Crore towards
        Computer under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that
        the additional capital expenditure towards the replacement of assets if the useful
        life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project and such

   M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 72
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

       assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these
       Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of
       assets deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date,
       the additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making
       necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation,
       subject to prudence check.”

       On perusal of the break-up of aforesaid assets, it is observed that the total value
       of order is Rs. 0.146 Crore, whereas the petitioner has indicated only Rs. 0.07
       Crore in summary table of the additional capitalization under this head, this may
       be typographical error. It is observed that the said expenditure claimed by the
       petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, as
       the petitioner has not shown the replacement of existing assets where the useful
       life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project. The
       Commission is of the view that the additional expenditure incurred by the petitioner
       towards computers is in the nature of Operation & Maintenance Expenses.
       Further, old assets are yet to be written-off as submitted by the petitioner. Hence,
       expenditure towards Computer is not allowed under Regulation 27.2 (a) in this
       Order at this stage.

xiv.   Capital Spares
       Petitioner’s Submission
       The capital spares booked under Account Code 11.300 amounting to Rs. 0.20
       Crore are capitalized in FY 2023-24 in the Audited Books of Accounts of FY 2023-
       24 and same is considered in the instant True up petition.
       The above mentioned Capitalization is claimed under proviso 27.1(vi) following
       clauses of MPERC Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Any liability for works
       admitted by the Commission after the cut-off date to the extent of discharge of
       such liabilities by actual payments;” read with proviso 25 of the Regulations, 2020
       towards ceiling limit of capital spares. The copy of store indent is annexed.

       Commission’s Analysis:
       The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.20 Crore towards
       capital spares under Regulation 27.1 (vi) of Regulations, 2020, which provides for
       additional capital expenditure within the original scope and after the cut-off date
       on account of liability for works admitted by the Commission after the cut-off date.
       It is observed that such spares have been capitalized after the cut-off date,
       therefore, the capital spares of Rs. 0.20 Crore claimed by the petitioner during FY
       2023-24 is not covered under Regulation 27 (vi) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence,
       this amount is not allowed in this Order.

    certain details/ documents regarding the additional capitalization in SSTPP PH-2. By
    affidavit dated 15th February, 2024, the petitioner filed its response to the queries raised
    by the Commission as given below:

     Railway sidings:
     Petitioner’s Submission
     The additional capitalization under Account Code 10.412 amounting to Rs 1.01 Crore
     is towards Construction of “Railway Under Bridges-RUB” carried out through Deposit
     work.
     The Ministry of Railway, Government of India, vide No. 2013/W-I/Genl./30 pt-II dated
     18.06.2013 created a policy regarding elimination of level crossing on New Rail line,
     Gauge Conversion and Doubling Projects, all over India for ensuring speed and safety
     point of view. The Railway Board has further simplified the procedure & policy regarding

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 76
                                             MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

provision of accommodation works i.e. Diversion Road, Subway, RUB, ROB, Level
Crossings, in case of new line, gauge conversion and doubling projects.

AS per the Private Siding Agreement signed between Western Central Railway and
MPPGCL at clause 9 of Annexure-3 (page-29), it has been directed by Railways to
eliminate level crossing and build RUB/ROB/sub way, etc., by MPPGCL. The relevant
page of the agreement is annexed.

Thus, the above-mentioned capitalization at SSTPP, Khandwa is made towards
compliance of directives of Ministry of Indian Railways, Government of India,
accordingly, aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC
Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of the any
statutory authorities”

Commission’s Analysis:
The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 1.01 Crore towards
Railway Under Bridges-RUB claimed under Regulation 28.1 (a) of Regulations, 2020
which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on
account of Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or
directions of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law. The
petitioner submitted as per the Private Siding Agreement signed between Western
Central Railway and MPPGCL, at clause 9, it has been directed by Railways to
eliminate level crossing and build RUB/ROB/sub way, etc. Ministry of Indian
Railways, Government of India, dated 18.06.2013 issued a policy regarding
elimination of level crossing on New Rail line, Gauge Conversion and Doubling
Projects, all over India for ensuring speed and safety point of view. Hence, aforesaid
assets under additional capitalization falls under statutory requirement. Therefore,
the said expenditure of Rs 1.01 Crore claimed by the petitioner towards Railway
Under Bridges-RUB is allowed in this Order under Regulation 28.1 (a) of the
Regulations, 2020.

Coal handling plant and handling equipments
Petitioner’s Submission
The Capitalization under Account Code 10.515 amounting to Rs 0.62 Crore is towards
       following:
a) Supply of “Portable Hydraulic Oil Filtration/ Cleaning Machine for HLP 68 grade
    Hydraulic Oil for CHP PH-2, SSTPP, Khandwa, vide order No. 11932 dated
    09.12.2022, amounting to Rs. 0.052 Crore.
     CHP of 2X660 MW, SSTPP Khandwa consists of various equipment, i.e., wagon
     tippler, side arm charger, apron feeder, stacker-reclaimer, etc., for coal rake
     unloading, evacuation of coal from hopper and stacking/ reclaiming purpose,

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 77
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

      having hydraulic systems. All equipment are running under coal dust atmosphere
      and the hydraulic oil, which is in continuous use gets contaminated. Life of
      equipment, power pack, mainly depends on oil quality being used. Contaminated
      oil will affect smooth running of equipment and the equipment will get wear & tear
      by using contaminated oil. Filtration is essential for enhancing the life of oil and to
      reduce the consumption of hydraulic oil. If oil is filtered and recycled from time to
      time, service life of oil will definitely be improved and it will reduce wear & tear of
      hydraulic parts & shall help to run all the auxiliaries smoothly.

      The Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 read with Hazardous and Other Wastes
      (Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016 mandates that no
      person carrying on any industry, operation or process shall discharge or emit or
      permit to discharge or emit pollutants. Further, no person shall handle or cause to
      be handled any hazardous substance except in accordance with such procedure
      and after complying with such safeguards as may be prescribed. Therefore, 01 No.
      Portable hydraulic oil filtration machine has been procured.

      The above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of
      The Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 and MoEF&CC Rules, therefore aforesaid
      capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of the Regulations 2020, which
      provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”.

b)    Supply of 01 No. Elecon make complete set of Gear box type, size: SBN- 355, vide
      order No. 5165 dated 28.07.2023, including freight charges, amounting to Rs. 0.57
      Crore.
      Elecon make Gear Box SBN 355 is installed to drive Rotary Breaker. M/s L&T
      Power has not supplied gearbox of this Model in mandatory spares. However,
      01 No. Gear Box was procured in year 2019 and the same has been consumed
      in Aug-2022. Unavailability of this complete Gearbox set would interrupt the
      operation of rotary breaker, in case of emergency. Therefore, the aforesaid
      Gearbox has been procured. The old gear box is being written-off shortly.
      The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations,
      2020 which provides for replacement when the useful life of the assets is not
      commensurate with the useful life of the project.

 Commission’s Analysis:
 The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.62 Crore towards Coal
 handling plant and handling equipments. Aforesaid additional capitalization is claimed
 under Regulation 28.1 (a) and 27.2(a) of the Regulations, 2020.



 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 78
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

       a) The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.052 Crore towards Portable Hydraulic
          Oil Filtration/ Cleaning Machine under Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations,
          2020 which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original
          scope of work on account of Change in law or compliance of any existing
          law. The petitioner stated that aforesaid item shall be utilised for filtering
          and recycling various types of lubricating oils used in CHP Equipments,
          thus avoiding discard of used oil (Hazardous material). Since, this
          capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of the

Chemical Lab Equipment:-
Petitioner’s Submission
The Capitalization under Account Code 10.588 amounting to Rs 0.29 Crore is towards:
a) Supply of 02 Nos. Portable Conductivity Meter and 03 Nos. Benchtop Conductivity
   Meter complete assembly, vide order No. 1653 dated 11.05.2023 amounting to Rs.
   0.084 Crore, for measuring conductivity/ TDS/ salinity and temperature.

    Conductivity is a vital parameter for supercritical thermal generating units and
    conductivity meter is used for the testing/ measuring conductivity of various water
    samples of units, Raw water, pre treatment plant, DM plant, etc., where portable
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 79
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

    conductivity meter has provided the flexibility to check the parameter at the site and
    Benchtop conductivity meter is used at chemical lab.
    The above mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of
     the WATER (PREVENTION & CONTROL OF POLLUTION) ACT, 1974,
     wherein continuous monitoring of level of pollutant in water. Therefore, aforesaid
     capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations 2020,
     which provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”.

b). Supply of Flue Gas Analyzer complete assembly for chemical laboratory, 2X660MW
     at SSTPP, Khandwa vide order No. 11128 dated 08.01.2024 amounting to Rs. 0.21
     Crore.
     Flue Gas Analyzer is used for testing/ measuring CO, O2, CO2 in flue gas path
     and these parameters are required in the calculation of combustion efficiency of
     boiler of the units. Further, this parameter provides the status of combustion.
     Further, SSTPP, Khandwa has to ensure compliance of the directions issued by
     CPCB under section 5 of Environment Protection Act, 1986, vide letter No.
     B33014/07/2019/IPC-II/TPP/10204 dated 13.12.2019, pertaining to the plant
     regarding compliance of emission standards pertaining to PM, SO x & NOx.

     Thus, the above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives
     of CPCB, Government of India and MoEF&CC Notifications/Acts. Therefore,
     aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC Regulations,
     2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of any statutory
     authorities.

     Commission’s Analysis
     The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.29 Crore towards
     Chemical lab equipment. Aforesaid additional capitalization is claimed under
     Regulation 28.1 of the Regulations, 2020.

       a). The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.084 Crore under additional capitalization
           towards Portable Conductivity Meter and Benchtop Conductivity Meter
           under Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations, 2020 which provides for
           additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on account of
           Change in law or compliance of any existing law. The petitioner has
           mentioned that this capitalization is made towards compliance of directives
           of Water (Prevention & Control of Pollution) Act, 1974. The said
           expenditure of Rs 0.084 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under
           the Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not
           demonstrated any change in law event or compliance with existing law in
           relation to aforesaid Water Act, 1974. It is observed that the petitioner has
           not cited any specific provision/clause of the Water (Prevention & Control
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 80
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

           of Pollution) Act, 1974 under which additional capitalization is claimed.
           Hence, expenditure towards conductivity meter is not allowed in this Order.

       b). The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.21 Crore
           towards supply of Flue Gas Analyzer complete assembly for chemical
           laboratory claimed under Regulation 28.1 (a) of Regulations, 2020 which
           provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work
           on account of Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of
           the order or directions of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of
           any court of law. The petitioner has mentioned that the aforesaid
           capitalization is made towards the compliance of the Environment
           (Protection) Rules, 1986 dated 13.12.2019, pertaining to the plant
           regarding compliance of emission standards pertaining to PM, SOx & NOx,
           CPCB, Government of India and MoEF&CC Notifications/Acts. Therefore,
           the said expenditure of Rs 0.21 Crore claimed by the petitioner towards
           supply of Flue Gas Analyzer is allowed in this Order under Regulation 28.1
           (a) of the Regulations, 2020.

Others office Equipments:-
Petitioner’s Submission
The Capitalization under Account Code 10.904 amounting to Rs 0.01 Crore is
towards: supply of Usha make heavy duty pedestal fans for plant at 2x660MW,
SSTPP, Khandwa vide order No. 17911 dated 31.03.2023, amounting to Rs. 0.013
Crore.

12 Nos. of Heavy duty Pedestal fans have been procured and installed at different
 plant locations to reduced higher winding temperature of Excitation transformers
 and Unit service transformer of unit No. 3 & 4, Hydrogen plant Rectifier
 Transformer, AVR temperature Unit No. 4, Higher bearing temperature of HT
 motors of Unit No. 3 & 4, Relay panels and BTS system at SSTPP, Khandwa.

This new item has been added for safety and to enhance the performance of existing
 Auxiliaries and is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(d) of MPERC Regulations, 2020
 which provides for “Any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for
 higher security and safety of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate
 Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for national security/
 internal security.

Commission’s Analysis
The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs. 0.01 Crore towards Others office
Equipments under Regulation 28.1(d) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides any
capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher security and safety

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 81
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory
authorities responsible for national security/ internal security”. On perusal of the
details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed that the petitioner has
not demonstrated that the aforesaid expenditure is made on advise or direction by
the Govt. agency or any statutory body in accordance to the Regulations, 2020. The

this Order.

Computers:-
Petitioner’s Submission
The Capitalization under Account Code 10.905 amounting to Rs 0.014 Crore is
towards, supply of 03 Nos. Desktop computers with all accessories, VOLTRIQ Intel
Core i5 10500 8GB/ 1000GB HDD/ Windows 10 professional at 2x660MW, SSTPP,
Khandwa vide GeM Contract No. GEMC-511687791787111 dated 05.04.2023,
amounting to Rs. 0.014 Crore. The survey reporting of old/faulty computers is in
process.
The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (a) of MPERC
Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement when the useful life of the assets
is not commensurate with the useful life of the project.

Commission’s Analysis
The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.014 Crore towards
Computer under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that the
additional capital expenditure towards the replacement of assets if the useful life of the
assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project and such assets have
been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations.
Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under
the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization
may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross
fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (a) of the
Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown write-off of such assets in annual
Audited Accounts and mentioned that the survey report of computers is in process.
The Commission is also of the view that the additional expenditure incurred by the
petitioner towards computers is in the nature of Operation & Maintenance Expenses.
Hence, expenditure towards Computer is not allowed in this Order at this stage.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 82
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


Capital Spares:-
Petitioner’s Submission
The capital spares booked under Account Code 11.300 amounting to Rs. 0.44 Crore
are capitalized in FY 2023-24 in the Audited Books of Accounts. The same is
considered in the instant True up petition.
The above mentioned Capitalization is claimed under proviso 27.1(vi) of MPERC
Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Any liability for works admitted by the
Commission after the cut-off date to the extent of discharge of such liabilities by actual
payments;” read with proviso 25 of MPERC Regulations, 2020 towards ceiling limit of
capital spares. The copy of store indent is annexed.

Commission’s Analysis
The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.44 Crore towards capital
spares under Regulation 27.1 (vi) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides for
additional capital expenditure within the original scope and after the cut-off date on
account of liability for works admitted by the Commission after the cut-off date. It is
observed that such spares have been capitalized after the cut-off date, therefore, the
capital spares of Rs. 0.44 Crore claimed by the petitioner during FY 2023-24 is not
covered under Regulation 27 (vi) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence, this amount is not
allowed in this Order.

Impact of Price Variation:-
Petitioner’s Submission
The 660 MW Unit No.3 of SSTPP Stage-II, Khandwa was put under Commercial
Operation w.e.f. 18.11.2018 00:00 Hrs. Similarly, the 660 MW Unit No.4 of SSTPP
Stage-II was put under Commercial Operation w.e.f. 27.03.2019. The final bills
related to Erection works have been regularized in FY 2023-24, wherein minor
variation in various account code wise asset have been noticed due to Price
Variation. The impact of this price variation amounts to Rs. 1.70 Crore.

The said capitalization is claimed under Proviso 27.1 (iv) of MPERC Regulations,
2020 which provides for “Liabilities for works executed prior to the cut-off date.’

Commission’s Analysis
The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs 1.70 Crore towards the impact of
price variation under Regulation 27.1 (iv) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides
for additional capital expenditure within the original scope and after the cut-off date
on account of Liability for works executed prior to the cut-off date. On perusal of the
details filed by the petitioner, the Commission has observed that the petitioner has
demonstrated that such assets/works were completed prior to cut-off date. The
petitioner has established/justified by submitting its certificate dated 27.3.2025 that

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 83
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     such assets/works in which impact of price variation was sought were admitted by
     the Commission at the time of determination of final tariff. In view of the above, the
     aforesaid assets are covered under the Regulation 27.1 (iv) of Regulations, 2020.
     Hence, additional capitalization of Rs 1.70 Crore towards impact of price variation is
     allowed in this Order.

      for emergency power under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, which
      provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment
      where the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project
      and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these
      Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets
      deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional
      capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments
      in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

      In view of the above provision under Regulations, the petitioner has informed that the
      old DG Set for emergency power are decapitalized/written off against aforesaid
      capitalization, which is considered under asset deduction. Hence, the said expenditure
      of Rs. 0.16 Crore claimed by the petitioner falls under the aforesaid provision mentioned
      in the Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has demonstrated
      necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation in its
      Asset-cum-Depreciation register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts. Hence, this
      expenditure towards DG Set for emergency power is allowed in this Order.

iv.    Other misc. equipment’s including fire protection system: -
      Petitioner’s submission:
      One No. 55 KW/75 HP, IE2, 415 Volt Induction motor has been procured vide order No.
      1333 dated 22.09.2023 amounting to Rs. 2.89 Lakhs, to be used as replacement of
      Induction motor in Pump set for Water Supply System at Gandhi Sagar HPS. This motor
      has been installed and commissioned in the Pump set in FY 2023-24 at Gandhi Sagar
      HPS. The old un-serviceable Induction Motor Kirloskar Make DSM-5 75 HP has
      been written-off.
      The expenses incurred towards above have been Capitalized in the books of Accounts
      under Account Code 10.599 vide Voucher No. 680197 dated 30.11.2023. The additional
      Capitalization amounts to Rs 0.03 Crore on MP share basis.
      The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (a) of MPERC Regulations,
      2020 which provides for replacement of assets when the useful life of the assets is not
      commensurate with the useful life of the project.



      M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 88
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


    Commission’s Analysis:
    The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.01 Crore on MP Share Basis
    towards Induction motor under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, which
    provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment
    where the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project
    and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these
    Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets
    deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional
    capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments
    in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

    In view of the above Provision under Regulations, the petitioner has informed that the
    induction motors are decapitalized/written off against aforesaid capitalization, which is
    considered under asset deduction. Hence, the said expenditure of Rs 0.01 Crore
    claimed by the petitioner falls under the aforesaid provision mentioned in the Regulation
    27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has demonstrated necessary
    adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation in its Asset-cum-
    Depreciation register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts. Hence, this expenditure
    towards Induction Motor is allowed in this Order.

91. Vide Commission’s letter dated 31st December, 2024, the petitioner was asked to file

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 90

i. Hydel Power Generation Plant: -
   Petitioner’s Submission
   The existing generators have been in operation for more than 35 years and have
   outlived their technical life. The windings of Unit-1 are Class ‘F’ & that of Unit-2 is Class
   ‘B’ and the poles are insulated with class ‘B’ insulation. The class ‘B’ insulation is
   hygroscopic in nature (i.e. easily absorbs moisture).

  On 22.12.2020 at 07:09 Hrs., fire outbreak incidence occurred (a Force Majeure Event)
  initially at bottom joint of machine, reportedly due to earth fault resulting severe damage
  to Generator of Unit No.1 Pench HPS. Further, due to severe fire outbreak, the
  condition of stator coils was found to be deteriorated (insulation burnt and melted
  soldering material penetrated inside the bars) and the Unit was found not reliable for
  further operations.

  For restoration of the Generator of Unit No.1, orders were placed on M/s Coral
  Rewinding India Private Limited, Erode (T.N.) vide No. 582 dated 27.05.2022 and No.
  185 dated 13.04.2023 for supply and replacement of 11 kV Generator Stator Bars,
  amounting to Rs. 3.83 Cores and Rs.3.66 Crore respectively. The Unit No.1 was
  ultimately restored and put back under operation on 28.07.2023.

  The expenses of capital nature incurred towards above restoration works have been
  Capitalized in the books of Accounts under Account Code 10.531 vide Voucher No.
  680680 dated 31.03.2024 and No. 680679 dated 31.03.2024. The additional
  Capitalization amounts to Rs 5.73 Crore on MP share basis. The deletion of asset on
  due to above mentioned replacement amounting to Rs. 0.93 Crore has been
  considered in this petition. The damaged 504 Nos. Stator Bars have been survey
  reported/ Written-off.
  The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2(b) of MPERC Regulations,
  2020 which provides for replacement of the asset or equipment if necessary on account
  of Force Majeure conditions.

 Commission’s Analysis:
 The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 5.73 Crore towards Hydel
 power generation plant under Regulation 27.2 (b) of the Regulations, 2020, which
 provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment
 if necessary, on account of change in law or Force Majeure conditions. Regulation 27.2
 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
 scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
 admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
 assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

  M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 91
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


 According to Regulation 3.1 (24) of the Regulations, 2020, Force Majeure means “the
 event or circumstance or combination of events or circumstances which partly or fully
 prevents the generating company to complete the project within the time specified in
 the Investment Approval, and only if such events or circumstances are not within the
 control of the generating company and could not have been avoided, had the
 generating company taken reasonable care or complied with prudent utility practices.

  The petitioner submitted that fire outbreak incidence (a Force Majeure Event) occurred
  initially at bottom joint of machine, due to earth fault resulting severe damage to
  Generator of Unit No.1, of Pench HPS and has claimed this work under force majeure
  events. The Commission observes that the expenses of capital nature incurred towards
  above restoration works have been Capitalized in the Annual Audited Accounts in FY
  2023-24. It is further observed that (the damaged Stator Bars have already been
  Written-off) and the deletion of asset due to above mentioned replacement amounting
  to Rs. 0.93 Crore has been allowed in this petition.

  It is further observed that the aforesaid work/assets have been written-off and
  adjustment of the same have already done in Annual Audited Accounts & Assets-cum-
  depreciation register. Since, the petitioner has demonstrated/justified with supporting
  documentary evidences that this work towards restoration of Generator of Unit No 1
  of Pench HPS falls under the events prescribed under the force majeure conditions.
  In view of above, additional capitalization of Rs 5.73 Crore towards hydel power
  generation plant is allowed in this Order under Regulation 27.2(b).

ii. Other transformers- Power House: -
    Petitioner’s Submission
  For compliance of directives of MP SLDC for Regional Energy Accounting and CEA
  (Installation and Operation of Meters) Regulations, 2006 read with subsequent
  amendments which specify that the accuracy of Current Transformers (CTs) and
  Voltage Transformers (VTS/CVTs) shall not be inferior to that of associated meters
  (0.2s), 9 Nos. of 0.2 accuracy class 132KV CVT (Current Voltage Transformer) have
  been commissioned at 2x80 MW Pench, HPS vide order no. 312 dated 15.03.2022.
  The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.542 amounting to Rs 0.10 Crore on
  MP share basis, is towards supply of 9 Nos. 0.2 accuracy class 132KV CVT (Current
  Voltage Transformer) at 2x80 MW Pench, HPS.

  The expenses of capital nature have been Capitalized in the books of Accounts under
  Account Code 10.542 vide Voucher No. 680496 dated 31.03.2024.
  The above-mentioned capital expenditure is made towards compliance of directives of
  CEA, Regulations, therefore, aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a)
  M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 92
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

   of the Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of any
   statutory authorities”

   Commission’s Analysis:
   The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.10 Crore towards Other
   transformers claimed under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities to meet
   award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions of the any statutory
   authority, or order or decree of any court of law”. The petitioner stated that aforesaid
   expenditure was made towards compliance of the directions of MP SLDC for State
   Energy Accounting and CEA (Installation and Operation of Meters) Regulations, 2006,
   hence, the same is covered under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 which
   provides for “additional capitalization on account of change in law or compliance of any
   existing law”. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 0.10 Crore towards other transformers of
   power house is allowed under Regulation 28.1 (b) in this Order.
