iv. Communication equipment-radio & high frequency carrier system: -
   Petitioner’s Submission
   MPPGCL has to ensure compliance of directives of MP SLDC and CEA (Technical
   Standards for Communication System in Power System Operations) Regulations, 2020
   which clearly specify that adequate Communication System is to be provided for
   reliable data and voice communication and tele-protection for power system at
   National, Level, Regional Level, Interstate Level and Intra State level. The Pench Hydel
   Power Station is located in core area of Pench Tiger Reserve forest, which prohibits
   laying of communication cables or any kind of structures. Therefore, for providing
   reliable 100Mbps Broadband Internet facility at Pench HPS and Office at Paoni, Radio
   (Wireless) Communication Equipments were required to be installed.

   Accordingly, an order was placed on M/s Borkar Broadband Services, Parseoni,
   Nagpur vide No. 456 dated 27.04.2022. The new assets are booked as additional

   supply & installation of RF equipment and providing 100 Mbps Unlimited BSNL
   Broadband Internet at Pench HPS.

    CEA, Regulations therefore aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a)
    of MPERC Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of
    any statutory authorities”

    Commission’s Analysis
    The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.002 Crore towards
    Communication equipment-radio & high frequency carrier system under Regulation
    28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance
    of the order or directions of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any
    court of law”. The petitioner informed that the aforesaid capitalization is done
    towards compliance of directives of MP SLDC and CEA (Technical Standards for
    Communication System in Power System Operations) Regulations, 2020, which
    clearly specifies that adequate communication system is to be provided for reliable

   M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 94

       i.     Hydel Power Generation Plant: -

              An order was placed on M/s Advent Electric Technologies Pvt. Ltd., Delhi, vide
              No. 159 dated 10.05.2023 for procurement of 30 Nos. CO2 gas filled cylinders
              of 45 Kg each and 04 Nos. of Nitrogen gas filled pilot cylinders of 2 Kg capacity

              Rajghat HPS.

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                    Page 98
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        The assets have been capitalized in the Books of Accounts under Account Code
        10.531 amounting to Rs. 0.14 Crore on MP Share basis vide Voucher No.
        680151 dated 30.06.2023.

        The Central Electricity Authority (Best Practices in operation and maintenance
        of Hydro Power Stations) recommends for mock trials of the fire fighting system
        along with evacuation system. Checking weight loss of the CO2 Cylinders and
        replenish as per recommendations of OEM.

        Further, CEA (Measures relating to safety and electric supply) Regulations,2023
        also mandates every generating stations, substations or switching stations, an
        artificial respirator, Fire extinguishers, first aid boxes, and gas masks shall be
        provided and kept in good working condition and locations of the same shall be
        displayed in the control room and operator cabin.
        Therefore, aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC
        Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of any
        statutory authorities” read with Proviso 28.1(d) of MPERC Regulations, 2020
        which provides for “Any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need
        for higher security and safety of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate
        Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for national security/
        internal security”.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs. 0.14 Crore towards
        procurement of CO2 gas filled cylinders ,Nitrogen gas filled pilot cylinders for
        Generator Protection under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities
        to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions of the
        any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law read with
        Regulation 28.1(d) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides any capital
        expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher security and safety
        of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or
        statutory authorities responsible for national security/ internal security”. The
        petitioner informed that the aforesaid capitalization is done towards
        compliance of CEA (Best Practices in operation and maintenance of Hydro
        Power Stations) and (Measures relating to safety and electric supply)
        Regulations, 2023.

        On perusal of the details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed
        that in para 6.2.3 of the Best practices in O&M of hydro “inspection of circuits
        for protection & control & mock trails of fire fighting system alongwith
        evacuation system. Checking weight loss of the CO2 cylinders and replenish
        as per recommendations of OEM”. Further Regulation 29 of the CEA

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 99
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        (Measures relating to safety and electric supply) also mandate the hydro
        power station for such work. In view of the above, such assets additions is
        covered under aforesaid CEA Regulations, therefore, the same have been
        allowed under Regulation 28.1 (a) and 28.1(d) of the Regulations, 2020.
        Hence, the said additional capitalization of Rs. 0.14 Crore is allowed in this
        Order.

  ii.   Other transformers- Power House: -
        Petitioner’s Submission
        The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.542 amounting to Rs 0.43
        Crore is towards Transfer of 1 No. 40 MVA, 132/33 KV, Transformer from ATPS
        PH-3, Chachai to Rajghat HPS vide order No. 1678 dated 22.03.2024.
        The asset was already approved by the Commission at ATPS, Chachai the same
        has now been deleted and transferred to Rajghat HPS, therefore this asset has
        been added at Rajghat HPS.

        In view of the above, MPPGCL requests the Commission to kindly allow the
        above capitalization on account of transfer of assets from ATPS, Chachai to
        Rajghat HPS.
        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner claimed the additional capitalization amounting to Rs. 0.43
        Crore towards transfer of 132/33 KV, Transformer from ATPS PH-3, Chachai
        to Rajghat HPS. As consequent impact, the amount of Rs. 0.43 Crore has
        already been considered under total asset deduction of Rs. 0.81 Crore at
        ATPS Chachai as reflected in corresponding table of subject true up petition.
        Correspondingly Normative Loan/Equity has also been reduced for ATPS
        Chachai, which is also reflected in subject true up petition. It is observed that
        the aforesaid additional capitalisation is towards book adjustment. Earlier the
        assets of Transformer was under ATPS Chachai and now it has been
        transferred to Rajghat HPS. Hence, the asset of Rs 0.43 Crore transfer from
        ATPS PH-3 to Rajghat HPS is allowed in this Order, accordingly.

        The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.04 Crore towards

        replacement of assets or equipment where the useful life of the assets is not

        fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations.
        Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets
        deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the
        additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making
        necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
        depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

        With regards to write-off of old batteries, the petitioner submitted that the old
        batteries is in the process of write-off. Hence, expenditure towards batteries
        Including Charging Equipment is not allowed in this Order at this stage under
        Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020. The petitioner is at liberty to
        approach the Commission as and when, the batteries gets written-off in
        Annual Audited Accounts and Asset-cum-Depreciation register.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 102
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        along with evacuation system. Checking weight loss of the CO2 Cylinders and
        replenish as per recommendations of OEM.
        Further, CEA (Measures relating to safety and electric supply) Regulations,2023
        also mandates every generating stations, substations or switching stations, an
        artificial respirator, Fire extinguishers, first aid boxes, and gas masks shall be
        provided and kept in good working condition and locations of the same shall be
        displayed in the control room and operator cabin.

        Therefore, aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC
        Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of any
        statutory authorities” read with Proviso 28.1(d) of MPERC Regulations, 2020
        which provides for “Any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need
        for higher security and safety of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate
        Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for national security/
        internal security”.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs. 0.01 Crore towards
        procurement of Other Misc. Equipments Including Fire Protection System
        under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities to meet award of
        arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions of the any statutory
        authority, or order or decree of any court of law read with Regulation 28.1(d)
        of the Regulations, 2020, which provides any capital expenditure to be
        incurred on account of need for higher security and safety of the plant as
        advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory
        authorities responsible for national security/ internal security”. The petitioner
        informed that the aforesaid capitalization is done towards compliance of CEA
        (Best Practices in operation and maintenance of Hydro Power Stations) and
        (Measures relating to safety and electric supply) Regulations, 2023. On
        perusal of the details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed that
        Regulation 29(1) & (5) of the CEA (Measures relating to safety and electric
        supply) also mandate the hydro power station for such work. In view of the
        above, such assets additions is covered under aforesaid CEA Regulations,
        therefore, the same has been allowed under Regulation 28.1 (a) and 28.1(d)
        of the Regulations, 2020. Hence, the said additional capitalization of Rs. 0.01
        Crore is allowed in this Order.

vii.      Computers:-

          Petitioner’s Submission
          The capitalization booked under Account Code 10.905 amounting to Rs 0.02
          Crore is towards:
           1. Procurement of 2 Nos. Dell intel core i5 15.6” Laptop (window 11
              professional) at Rajghat HPS, vide GeM Contract No. 511687787506678
              dated 15.03.2024.

           2. Procurement of 6 Nos. Desktop Computers, Hp intel core i5 12500 16 GB/
              1000GB HDD/ Windows-11 Professional at Rajghat HPS, vide GeM
              Contract No. 511687752183014 dated 23.01.2024. The old computers are
              being written-off shortly.
          The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (a) of MPERC
          Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement when the useful life of the
          assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project.

       Commission’s Analysis

       The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.03 Crore towards office
       equipments & Rs 0.02 Crore towards Computers under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the
       Regulations, 2020, which provides that the additional capitalization towards
       replacement of assets or equipment where the useful life of the assets is not
       commensurate with the useful life of the project and such assets have been fully
       depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2
       further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
       scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 104
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
        assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

        In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
        expenditure towards office equipments claimed by the petitioner does not fall under
        the Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the
        replacement of existing assets where the useful life of the assets is not
        commensurate with the useful life of the project and such assets have been fully
        depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations. Also the
        Commission is of the view that these minor expenses towards office equipments
        are in the nature of O&M expenses, hence, such expenditure is not allowed in this
        order.

        With regard to computers, although the capitalization has been made against the
        write-off of old assets and necessary adjustments have been made in the Assets
        cum-Depreciation Register, but the Commission is of the view that these minor
        expenses towards computers are in the nature of O&M expenses, hence, such
        expenditure is not allowed in this order.
viii.      Capital Over Haul Turbine & Generator (COH):

        Petitioner’s Submission
        The Statutory compliance of Indian Accounting Standards (INDAS) is mandatory on
        MPPGCL. Based on said standards, the Expenditure on Major inspection/capital
        Overhaul-Generator Turbine was capitalized in the Audited books of Accounts of
        MPPGCL in FY 2023-24 at Rajghat HPS under Account Code 11.603 amounting to
        Rs. 0.82 Crore on MP share basis. The same is claimed in the instant petition is
        towards:

        Work of Capital overhauling of Unit No. 3 at Rajghat HPS order placed on M/s
        Multitech Engineers, Ghaziabad (U.P.), vide order No. 81 dated 13.04.2023,
        Voucher No. 680005 dated 31.01.2024 and extension work order for Capital
        Overhauling placed to M/s Multitech Engineers Ghaziabad (U.P.), for Unit No.3 at
        Rajghat HPS, as per scope of work, vide order No. 1125 dated 12.01.2024 and
        Voucher No. 680550 dated 31.03.2024.

        Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations,
        20 which provides for “Additional capitalization on account of Change in law or
        compliance of any existing law”.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.82 Crore towards
        Capital Overhaul Expenditure under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020,

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 105
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

         which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work
         on account of change in law or compliance of any existing law. It is observed that
         the aforesaid additional capitalization is only book entry, due to change in
         accounting practices under Indian Accounting Standards, whereas, the
         Commission is determining tariff under GAAP. The said expenditure of Rs. 0.82
         Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the
         Regulations, 2020. It is also observed that petitioner is unable to justify its claim
         of additional expenditure under the Regulations, 2020. Hence, expenditure
         towards Boiler/Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure is not allowed in this Order.

     An order was placed on M/s Temflo Systems Private Limited, Ghaziabad towards

monitoring of Generator can therefore be done, thus contributing towards safety.

Petitioner’s Submission
For compliance of directives of MP SLDC for State Energy Accounting and CEA
(Installation and Operation of Meters) Regulations, 2006 read with subsequent
amendments, which specify that the accuracy of Current Transformers (CTs) and
Voltage Transformers (VTS/CVTs) shall not be inferior to that of associated meters

 These assets have been Capitalization in Books under Account Code 10.542
 amounting to Rs 0.99 Crore, vide voucher No. 680497 dated 31.03.2024.
 The above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of CEA,
 therefore, aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC
 Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of any statutory
 authorities”

 Commission’s Analysis:
 The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.99 Crore towards Other
 transformers has claimed under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities
 to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions of the any
 statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law”. The petitioner informed
 that the aforesaid capitalization is done towards compliance of directives of MP
 SLDC for State Energy Accounting and CEA (Installation and Operation of Meters)
 Regulations, 2006 which specifies the accuracy of interface meter shall be 0.2s,
 therefore, the said expenditure of Rs. 0.99 Crore towards other transformers (which
 is beyond original scope of work) is allowed in this Order.

iii.    Other transmission plant, transformer kiosks, sub-station equipment and
        other fixed apparatus: -
 Petitioner’s Submission
 (a) The Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 read with Hazardous and Other Wastes
     (Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016 mandates that no
     person carrying on any industry, operation or process shall discharge or emit or be
     permitted to be discharge or emit pollutants. Further, no person shall handle or
     cause to be handled any hazardous substance except in accordance with such
     procedure and after complying with such safeguards as may be prescribed. In
     compliance to the above MPPGCL has procured one number High Vacuum
     Transformer Oil Filtering machine and evacuation system confirming to IS 6034
     from M/s Krishna Engineering Company, Bhopal vide order no. 3272 dated
     21.03.2023. This Transformer Oil Filtering machine shall be utilized at Bargi HPS
       for filtering and recycling Generator Transformer Oil, Station Transformer Oils and
       other transformers oils thus avoiding discard of used Oil (Hazardous material).

       The new asset has been Capitalized in books of accounts under Account Code
       10.543 vide voucher No. 680685 dated 31.03.2024 amounting to Rs. 0.17 Crore.

       The above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of The
       Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 and MoEF&CC Rules, therefore aforesaid
       capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of the Regulations 2020, which
       provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”.

 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 109
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

 (b) Further, 33 Nos. 132/33KV Isolator TP gang operated without earth switch
     amounting to Rs 0.01 Crore vide order No. 1678 dated 22.03.2024 have been
     transferred from ATPS PH-3, Chachai to RABS HPS, Bargi Nagar and
     commissioned. The asset value is Rs. 01 Crore.
      The asset was already approved by the Commission at ATPS, Chachai the same
      has now been deleted and transferred to Bargi HPS, therefore this asset has been
      added at Bargi HPS.
      In view of the above, MPPGCL requests the Commission to kindly allow the
      above capitalization on account of transfer of assets from ATPS, Chachai to
      Bargi HPS.
      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.17 Crore towards
      Transformer Oil Filtering machine under Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations,
      2020 which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of
      work on account of Change in law or compliance of any existing law. The
      petitioner stated that aforesaid item shall be utilised for filtering and recycling
      generator transformer oil, station transformer oils and other transformer oils,
      thus avoiding discard of used oil (Hazardous material). Since, this capitalization
      is made towards compliance of directives of the Environment (Protection) Act,
      1986 read with Hazardous and Other Wastes (Management and Transboundary
      Movement) Rules, 2016, therefore, the same is covered under Regulation 28.1
      (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 0.17 Crore towards
      works related to transmission plant, transformers, sub-station equipment and
      other items is allowed in this Order
      Further, the petitioner claimed the additional capitalization amounting to Rs. 0.01
      Crore towards transfer of asset related to 33 Nos. 132/33KV Isolator TP gang
      operated without earth switch from ATPS PH 3 to Bargi HPS. It is observed that
      the amount of Rs. 0.01 Crore is not considered under total asset deduction of
      Rs. 0.81 Crore at ATPS PH 3 as reflected in corresponding table of True up
      petition. Since, the petitioner has not demonstrated/justified the transfer of
      assets to ATPS PH 3 to Bargi HPS. Hence, the Commission has not allowed the
      transfer of asset in this Order, at this stage.

     nagar. The old unserviceable Circuit breaker is being survey reported and
     written-off shortly.

     The above capitalization is claimed as per regulation 27.2 (a) of MPERC
     Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement when the useful life of the assets
     is not commensurate with the useful life of the project.

     Commission’s Analysis:
     The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.08 Crore towards
     Supply of Circuit breaker including installation charges under Regulation 27.2
     (a) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides that the additional capitalization
     towards replacement of assets or equipment where the useful life of the assets
     is not commensurate with the useful life of the project and such assets have
     been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations.
     Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed
     under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional
     capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary
     adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject
     to prudence check.”
     With regards to write-off of old unserviceable circuit breakers, the petitioner
     mentioned that the old unserviceable circuit breakers is in process of write-off
     and being written-off shortly. Hence, expenditure towards circuit breaker is not
     allowed in this Order at this stage under Regulation 27.2(a) of the Regulation,
     2020. The petitioner is at liberty to approach the Commission as and when, the
     old unserviceable circuit breakers gets written-off in Annual Audited Accounts
     and Asset-cum-Depreciation register.

      The new assets has been Capitalized in the books of Accounts under Account Code
      10.599 amounting to Rs. 0.02 Crore. These new assets are in addition to the existing
      Fire extinguishers based on safety recommendations.

      The Central Electricity Authority (Best Practices in operation and maintenance of
      Hydro Power Stations) recommends for mock trials of the fire fighting system along
      with evacuation system. Checking weight loss of the CO2 Cylinders and replenish
      as per recommendations of OEM.

      Further, CEA (Measures relating to safety and electric supply) Regulations,2023 also
      mandates every generating stations, substations or switching stations, an artificial
      respirator, Fire extinguishers, first aid boxes, and gas masks shall be provided and
      kept in good working condition and locations of the same shall be displayed in the
      control room and operator cabin.


 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 112
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

       Therefore, aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC
       Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of any
       statutory authorities” read with Proviso 28.1(d) of MPERC Regulations, 2020 which
       provides for “Any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher
       security and safety of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate Government
       Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for national security/ internal security”.

       Commission’s Analysis:
       The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs. 0.02 Crore towards Other
       Miscellaneous Equipments including Fire Protection System under Regulation
       28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for
       compliance of the order or directions of the any statutory authority, or order or
       decree of any court of law read with Regulation 28.1(d) of the Regulations, 2020,
       which provides any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for
       higher security and safety of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate
       Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for national security/
       internal security”. The petitioner has mentioned that the aforesaid capitalization
       is made towards compliance of CEA (Best Practices in operation and
       maintenance of Hydro Power Stations) and (Measures relating to safety and
       electric supply) Regulations, 2023, therefore, the said additional capitalization of
       Rs. 0.02 Crore towards fire-protection system (which is beyond original scope of
       work) is allowed in this Order.

     18 of the Factory Act, 1948 which states that it is the responsibility of the factory owner
     to provide portable water facility to the workers employed therein. Considering above,
     the said expenditure falls under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence,

    conditions.

    The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 6.02 Crore towards Hydel
    power generation plant under Regulation 27.2 (b) of the Regulations, 2020, which

    under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional

    prudence check.”

    with other accessories yet to be written-off is in the process of write-off. Hence,

    stage under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020. The petitioner is at liberty

b.    The Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 read with Hazardous and Other Wastes
      (Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016 mandates that no
      person carrying on any industry, operation or process shall discharge or emit or be
      permitted to be discharge or emit pollutants. Further, no person shall handle or
      cause to be handled any hazardous substance except in accordance with such
      procedure and after complying with such safeguards as may be prescribed.
      In compliance to the above MPPGCL has procured 01 No. of Oil Centrifuge Unit
      from M/s CBS Technologies Pvt. Ltd., Noida, vide order No. 3143 dated
      13.03.2023, amounting to Rs. 0.11 Crore.

      This Oil Centrifuge Unit shall be utilized at Bansagar HPS PH-2 for separating
      contamination in servo oil, being used by various auxiliaries of Hydel Power Station
      and to retain its property. The Oil shall be reused without any wastage, thus
      avoiding discard of used Oil (Hazardous material).

     The above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of
     The Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 and MoEF&CC Rules, therefore aforesaid
     capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations 2020, which
     provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”.

   Commission’s Analysis:
a. The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.20 Crore towards
   Cooling Water Motor Pump set under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations,
   2020, which provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of
   assets or equipment where the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with
   the useful life of the project and such assets have been fully depreciated in
   accordance with the provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further
   provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope
   of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
   admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross
   fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

     With regards to write-off of old assets, the petitioner mentioned that the old assets
     is in process of write-off. Hence, expenditure towards Cooling Water Motor Pump
     set is not allowed in this Order at this stage under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the
     Regulations, 2020. The petitioner is at liberty to approach the Commission as and
     when, the Assets gets written-off in Annual Audited Accounts and Asset-cum-
     Depreciation register.

b.   The petitioner has also claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.11 Crore towards
     Oil Centrifuge Unit under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 which

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 117
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on
        account of Change in law or compliance of any existing law. The petitioner has
        mentioned that the aforesaid item shall be utilised for separating contamination in
        servo oil/lubricating oil, being used in various auxiliaries of Hydel Power Station.
        The Oil shall be reused without any wastage, thus avoiding discard of used oil
        (Hazardous material). Since, this capitalization is made towards compliance of
        directives of the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 and MoEF&CC Rules,
        therefore, the same is covered under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations,
        2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 0.11 Crore towards auxiliaries in hydel power
        plant is allowed in this Order.

      Commission’s Analysis:

      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.01 Crore towards
      Computers under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides
      that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment
      where the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the

      hence, such expenditure towards computers is not allowed as capital expenses in
      this order.

(viii) Capital Over Hauling (COH):-
       Petitioner’s Submission
      The Statutory compliance of Indian Accounting Standards (INDAS) is mandatory on
      MPPGCL. Based on said standards, the Expenditure on Major inspection/capital
      Overhaul- Generator and Turbine was capitalized in the Audited books of Accounts
      of MPPGCL in FY 2023-24 at Bansagar HPS PH-1,2 & 3 under Account Code 11.603
      amounting to Rs 26.70 Crore is towards:
      a) Order for work contract for supply, erection, commissioning and restoration of
          damaged generator including dismantling, rectification, assembly of Stator core
          windings and Rotor Poles of BHEL make Generator of Unit No.1 at 3x105 MW,
           Bansagar PH-1, vide No. 1031 dated 28.01.2022, having total value of Rs. 18.07
           Crore. The total value issued on completion of CoH is Rs. 17.78 Crore. Out of
           this an amount of Rs. 6.02 Crore has been capitalized in account code 10.531
           as already described in Point A above. The balance value on completion of CoH
           is Rs.11.76 Crore, as capitalized in account code 11.603.
           Therefore, the supply portion of tangible asset (supply part) of Generator No.-1
           in this order, amounts to Rs. 8.70 Crore and the works portion amounts to Rs.
           3.06 Crore.


   M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 122
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

   b) Order for work contract for Capital Overhauling of Turbine of Unit No.1 at 3x105
      MW, Bansagar PH-1, vide No.783 dated 05.01.2021, having total value of Rs.
      09.95 Crore. The total asset value issued on completion of CoH is Rs. 9.60
      Crore, as capitalized in account code 11.603. The supply portion of tangible
      asset (supply part) of Turbine No.1, in this order, amounts to Rs. 7.83 Crore and
      the works portion amounts to Rs. 1.77 Crore.
   c)   Work Order for supply and repair works of Turbine and generator part of Unit
        No.1 at 3x105 MW, Bansagar PH-1 vide No.1804 dated 20.10.2022, having total
        value of Rs. 4.84 Crore. The total asset value issued on completion of CoH is
        Rs. 4.84 Crore, as capitalized in account code 11.603. The supply portion of
        tangible asset (supply part) in this order amounts to Rs. 1.70 Crore and the
        works portion amounts to Rs. 3.14 Crore.
   d) The Freight charges of the supply portion of tangible asset (supply part) for COH
      works of Turbine and Generator part of Unit No.3 at 3x105 MW, Bansagar PH-
      1, vide No.293 dated 14.08.2020, amounting to Rs. 0.097 Crore, has been
      capitalized in account code 11.603, in the Audited books of Accounts of
      MPPGCL in FY 2023-24.

      The Freight charges of the supply portion of tangible asset (supply part) for COH
      works of Turbine and generator part of Unit No.3 at 3x105 MW, Bansagar PH-
      1, vide No. 294 dated 14.08.2020, amounting to Rs. 0.39 Crore, has been
      capitalized in account code 11.603, in the Audited books of Accounts of
      MPPGCL in FY 2023-24.
   e) Order for supply of one runner disc for 11KV, BHEL make hydro TG set at
      3x105MW, Bansagar PH-1, vide No.3613 dated 18.01.2019, having total value
      of Rs. 0.46 Crore for the supply portion of tangible asset (supply part). An
      amount of Rs. 0.32 Crore towards this order has been capitalized in account
      code 11.603 in the Audited books of Accounts of MPPGCL in FY 2023-24.
        Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per Regulation 28.1(b) of MPERC
        Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Additional capitalization on account of
        Change in law or compliance of any existing law” read with Regulation 27.2 (a)
        of MPERC Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement of Assets when
        the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project.


   Commission’s Analysis:

   The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 26.70 Crore towards
   Generator and Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure under Regulation 28.1 (b) of
   the Regulations, 2020, which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond
   original scope of work on account of change in law or compliance of any existing

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 123
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        law. It is observed that the aforesaid additional capitalization is only book entry,
        due to change in accounting practices under Indian Accounting Standards,
        whereas, the Commission is determining tariff under GAAP. The said expenditure
        of Rs. 26.70 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under Regulation 28.1
        (b) of the Regulations, 2020. It is also observed that petitioner is unable to justify
        its claim of additional expenditure under the Regulations, 2020. Hence,
        expenditure towards Boiler/Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure is not allowed in
        this Order.

        Further, the petitioner has also claimed aforesaid additional capitalization towards
        Generator and Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure under Regulation 27.2 (a) of
        the Regulations, 2020, which provides that the additional capitalization towards
        replacement of assets or equipment where the useful life of the assets is not
        commensurate with the useful life of the project and such assets have been fully
        depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these Regulations. Regulation 27.2
        further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
        scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
        admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
        assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.

        In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
        expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (a) of
        the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the replacement of existing
        assets where the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of
        the project and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the
        provisions of these Regulations. The petitioner has also not justified/established any
        adjustments in the gross fixed assets and cumulative depreciation towards
        replacement of existing assets in its Asset-cum-Depreciation register as well as its
        Annual Audited Accounts. Hence, expenditure towards Generator and Turbine
        Capital Overhaul Expenditure is not allowed in this Order.

i.   Hydel Power Generation Plant:

     Petitioner’s Submission
     An order vide No. 2234 dated 29.11.2021 was placed on M/s Mona Generators
     Services Pvt. Ltd. Nehru Place, New Delhi for supply, erection, testing and
     commissioning of One No. 250KVA/ 200kW silent DG Set with direct injection water
     cooled, 6 cylinder, 4 stroke, 1500RPM, along with AMF Panel and cable. The same
     has been installed and commissioned in FY 2023-24 at Bansagar PH-4 HPS amounting
     to Rs 0.24 Crore. This asset has been added against old Genset (Diesel Generator
     Set) earlier transferred to Bansagar PH-2 (Silpara) for avoidance of flooding of Power
     Station due to floods in Common Water Carrier (CWC) Canal during rainy season. The
     DG Set supplies emergency power supply to Hydel power station and also the most
     important use for restoration post partial /total blackout in case of Grid failure under the
     coordination with SLDC.
     It is, further to submit that as per the directive issued from time to time in MP Electricity
     Grid Codes, in case of total regional black out, SLDC In-charge shall co-ordinate and
     follow the instructions of WRLDC for early restoration of the entire grid. After total
     collapse, for each power station, to avoid damage to the turbine, survival power is
     required. To meet the survival power, the diesel generating (DG) sets of sufficient
     capacity shall be available at each power station. Start-up power to the thermal station
     shall be given by the hydel stations and interstate supply.
     The expenses incurred towards above have been Capitalized in the books of Accounts
     under Account Code 10.531.
     Therefore, aforesaid capitalization is claimed Under Proviso 28.1(a) of MPERC
     Regulations, 2020 which provides for compliance of “order or directions of any statutory
     authorities”

     Commission’s Analysis:
     The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.24 Crore towards DG Set
     under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for “Liabilities to meet award of arbitration
     or for compliance of the order or directions of the any statutory authority, or order or
     decree of any court of law”. The petitioner has mentioned that this asset has been
     added against old Genset earlier transferred to Bansagar PH-2. It is observed that
     the petitioner has failed to demonstrate these transfer of assets from Bansagar PH-
     4 to Silpara HPS. Further, the petitioner informed that the aforesaid capitalization is
     done towards compliance of directives of MP Electricity Grid Codes. On perusal of
     the details and documents submitted by the petitioner, it is observed that the
     petitioner has not filed specific provision of the Grid Code under which such assets
     have been capitalized, therefore such assets additions is not covered under MP
     Electricity Grid Code, therefore, the same does not fall under Regulation 28.1 (a) of

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 126
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

       the Regulations, 2020. Hence, the said additional capitalization of Rs. 0.24 Crore is
       not allowed in this Order at this stage.

ii.    Auxiliaries In Hydel Power Plants :-
        Petitioner’s Submission
        The Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 read with Hazardous and Other Wastes
        (Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016 mandates that no person
        carrying on any industry, operation or process shall discharge or emit or permit to be
        discharges or emitted pollutants. Further, no person shall handle or cause to be
        handled any hazardous substance except in accordance with such procedure and
        after complying with such safeguards as may be prescribed.
        In compliance to the above MPPGCL has procured 01 No. of Oil Centrifuge Unit from
        M/s Ameya Agencies, Nagpur vide order No. 2604 dated 03.01.2022 This Oil
        Centrifuge Unit shall be utilized at Bansagar HPS PH-4 for separating contamination
        in servo oil, being used by various auxiliaries of Hydel Power Station. The Oil shall be
        reused without any wastage, thus avoiding discard of used Oil (Hazardous material).
        The new asset has been capitalized in books of accounts under Account Code 10.535
        amounting to Rs. 0.12 Crore.
        The above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of The
        Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 and MoEF&CC Rules, therefore aforesaid
        capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations 2020, which
        provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.12 Crore towards
        procurement of oil centrifuge unit under Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations, 2020
        which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on
        account of Change in law or compliance of any existing law. The petitioner has
        mentioned that the aforesaid item shall be utilised for separating contamination in
        servo oil, being used by various auxiliaries of Hydel Power Station. The oil shall be
        reused without any wastage, thus avoiding discard of used oil (Hazardous material).
        Since, this capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of the
        Environment (Protection) Act, 1986 read with Hazardous and Other Wastes
        (Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016, therefore, the same is
        covered under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Hence, this expenditure
        of Rs 0.12 Crore towards auxiliaries in hydel power plant is allowed in this Order.

       With regards to write-off of old unserviceable Circuit breaker, the petitioner mentioned

      Commission’s Analysis

i.     The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.022 Crore towards other
       office equipments under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides
       that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment where
       the useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project and
       such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of these
       Regulations. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets
       deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the
       additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary
       adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to
       prudence check.”

      With regards to write-off of old ACs, the petitioner mentioned that the old ACs are yet
      to be write-off and in the process of write-off. Hence, expenditure towards Air

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 132
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

      Conditioners is not allowed in this Order at this stage under Regulation 27.2 (a) of the
      Regulations, 2020. The petitioner is at liberty to approach the Commission as and
      when, the ACs gets written-off in Annual Audited Accounts and Asset-cum-
      Depreciation register.


   ii. The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs. 0.0016 Crore towards CC TV Camera
       system under Regulation 28.1(d) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides any capital
       expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher security and safety of the
       plant as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory
       authorities responsible for national security/ internal security”. On perusal of the
       details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed that this work has been
       carried out as per the directives given by the Intelligence Bureau (IB) (GOI). Further,
       as per the submission of the petitioner made in affidavit, IB directives are highly
       classified/confidential documents and could not be reproduced to others. Considering
       the submission made by the petitioner and as the same is covered under the
       Regulation 28.1 (d) of the Regulations, 2020, hence, the said expenditure of Rs
       0.0016 Crore towards CCTV Cameras is allowed in this Order.

125. In view of the above, the Commission has allowed only Rs 0.0016 Crore towards
     additional capitalization of CCTV Cameras under the Regulations, 2020 in Madhikheda
     HPS.

130. The petitioner has filed write-off/adjustments of assets of Rs.11.02 Crore in thermal &
     hydro power stations. It is observed that the corresponding equity / loan amount
     pertaining to write-off/adjustment assets have also been reduced from the equity-debt
     components of the respective power stations.

131. With regard to de-capitalization, Regulation 28.2 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that:

            “28.2 In case of de-capitalisation of assets of a generating company, the original
            cost of such asset as on the date of de- capitalisation shall be deducted from the
            value of gross fixed asset and corresponding loan as well as equity shall be
            deducted from outstanding loan and the equity respectively in the year such de-
            capitalisation takes place with corresponding adjustments in cumulative
            depreciation and cumulative repayment of loan, duly taking into consideration the
            year in which it was capitalised.”

     stations during the year and then worked out the closing GFA. The write-off/de-
     capitalization of assets in different power stations is discussed separately as follows:

     Debt-Equity Ratio:

151. With regard to Debt: equity ratio, Regulation 33 of the Regulations, 2020 provides as
     under:

     33.1 For new projects, the debt-equity ratio of 70:30 as on date of commercial operation
          shall be considered. If the equity actually deployed is more than 30% of the capital
          cost, equity in excess of 30% shall be treated as normative loan:
     Provided that:
          a. where equity actually deployed is less than 30% of the capital cost, actual equity
          shall be considered for determination of tariff:
          b. the equity invested in foreign currency shall be designated in Indian rupees on
          the date of each investment:
          c. any grant obtained for the execution of the project shall not be considered as a
          part of capital structure for the purpose of debt : equity ratio.


        Explanation.-The premium, if any, raised by the generating company while issuing
             share capital and investment of internal resources created out of its free
             reserve, for the funding of the project, shall be reckoned as paid up capital for
             the purpose of computing return on equity, only if such premium amount and
             internal resources are actually utilised for meeting the capital expenditure of
             the generating station.
     33.2 The generating company shall submit the resolution of the Board of the company
          regarding infusion of fund from internal resources in support of the utilization made
          or proposed to be made to meet the capital expenditure of the generating station.
     33.3 In case of the generating station declared under commercial operation prior to
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 144
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

           1.4.2019, debt- equity ratio allowed by the Commission for determination of tariff
           for the period ending 31.3.2019 shall be considered:
                  Provided that in case of a generating station which has completed its useful
           life as on or after 01.04.2019, if the equity actually deployed as on 01.04.2019 is
           more than 30% of the capital cost, equity in excess of 30% shall not be taken into
           account for tariff determination.
     33.4 In case of the generating station declared under commercial operation prior to
          1.4.2019, but where debt: equity ratio has not been determined by the
          Commission for determination of tariff for the period ending 31.3.2019, the
          Commission shall approve the debt : equity in accordance with Regulation 33.1
          of these Regulations.
     33.5 Any expenditure incurred or projected to be incurred on or after 1.4.2019 as may
          be admitted by the Commission as additional capital expenditure for determination
          of tariff, and renovation and modernisation expenditure for life extension shall be
          serviced in the manner specified in clause 33.1 of this Regulation

152. Regulation 33.3 of the Regulations provides that the Generating Station/Unit declared
     under commercial operation prior to 01.04.2019, debt-equity ratio allowed by the
     Commission for determination for period ending 31.03.2019 shall be considered. Based
     on above Regulation, the Commission has considered power station wise opening loan
     and equity as on 01st April, 2023 same as loan and equity balances as on 31st March,
     2023 considered in last true-up Order for FY 2022-23 issued on 7th March, 2024.

153. Further, Regulation 33.5 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that debt : equity ratio of
     any expenditure incurred as may be admitted by the Commission as additional capital
     expenditure for determination of tariff shall be determined in the manner specified in
     Regulations 33.1 of this Regulation. Therefore, the debt and equity component for the
     additional capitalization admitted in this Order has been considered accordingly.

     Annual Capacity (fixed) Charges:

154. Regulation 17 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that Annual Capacity (fixed) Charges
     derived on the basis of annual fixed cost (AFC) which shall consist of the following
     components:

             (a)     Return on Equity;
             (b)     Depreciation
             (c)     Interest on Loan Capital;
             (d)     Operation and Maintenance Expenses
             (e)     Interest on Working Capital;


     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 145
                                                          MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

              Provided that special allowance in lieu of R&M, where opted in accordance with
        Regulation 30 of these Regulations shall be recovered separately and shall not be
        considered for computation of working capital.

       Provision in Regulations:
  157. With Regards to Return on Equity, Regulation 34 of the Regulations, 2020 provides
       that:

        34 . Return on Equity:
        34.1Return on equity shall be computed in rupee terms on the equity base determined
                in accordance with Regulation 33 of these Regulations.
        34.2 Return on equity shall be computed at the base rate of 15.50% for thermal
                generating stations and hydro generating stations and at the base rate of 16.50%
                for the pumped storage hydro generating stations and run-of river generating
                stations with pondage.
             Provided that
             i.       in case of a new project, the rate of return of a new project shall be reduced
                      by 1.00% for such period as may be decided by the Commission, if the
                      generating station is found to be declared under commercial operation
                      without commissioning of any of the Restricted Governor Mode Operation
                      (RGMO)/ Free Governor Mode Operation (FGMO):
            ii.       in case of existing generating station any of the above requirements are

        M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 147
                                                         MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

                      found lacking based on the report submitted by the respective SLDC/RLDC,
                      RoE shall be reduced by 1% for the period for which the deficiency
                      continues.
         iii.         in case of a thermal generating station, with effect from 1.04.2020:
                (a)   rate of return on equity shall be reduced by 0.25% in case of failure to
                      achieve the ramp rate of 1% per minute:
                (b)   an additional rate of return on equity of 0.25% shall be allowed for every
                      incremental ramp rate of 1% per minute achieved over and above the ramp
                      rate of 1% per minute, subject to ceiling of additional rate of return on equity
                      of 1.00%:

     Commission’s Analysis:

158. In this Order, the Commission has considered the power station wise opening equity as
     on 1st April, 2023 same as the closing equity admitted in last true-up order for FY 2022-
     23 issued on 7th March, 2024.

159. In compliance to Regulation 34.2 of the Regulations, 2020, the petitioner in its reply
     filed in proceeding of MYT Order dated 4th March, 2025 in Petition No. 56 of 2024
     informed that the generating stations of MPPGCL having installed capacity above 25
     MW have RGMO/FGMO commissioned. The petitioner further submitted that all the
     thermal generating stations of MPPGCL are operating with the ramp rate of over 1%
     per minute and same has also been certified by the State Load Dispatch Center
     (SLDC).

160. With regard to ROE in respect of additional capitalization, after the cut-off date beyond
     the original scope of work, proviso of Regulation 34.2 of the Regulations ,2020, as
     amended provides that:

                “Provided also that return on equity in respect of additional capitalization after
                cutoff date beyond the original scope of work, excluding additional capitalization
                on account of emission control system, shall be computed at the weighted
                average rate of interest on actual loan portfolio of the generating station or in the
                absence of actual loan portfolio of the generating station, the weighted average
                rate of interest of the generating company ,as a whole shall be considered ,
                subject to ceiling of 14%”

161. The petitioner filed the additional capitalization in thermal and hydel power stations
     during FY 2023-24 and claimed return on equity on additional equity infusion in respect
     of additional capitalization. The Commission has considered the power station wise
     equity addition within original scope of work and beyond original scope of work
     separately only to the extent of additional capitalization considered in this true-up Order.

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                 Page 148
                                                       MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     The equity over and above the normative equity is considered as normative loan. The
     Commission has also considered the reduction of equity amount in respect of the asset
     deletion/de-capitalization in respective power stations during the year.

     eligible for return on equity in this true-up Order is worked out as given below:

      163. The Commission has determined the ROE on equity component within the original

           with proviso 34.2 of the Regulations, 2020, as amended. Aforesaid Regulation further
           provides that in the absence of actual loan portfolio of the generating station, the
           weighted average rate of interest of the generating company, as a whole shall be
           considered.

     b.        The depreciation on the Gross Block has been computed based on the following:-
                The rates for depreciation are considered as approved by Commission in
                 Appendix-I of the Regulations,2020.

                The salvage value of assets is considered as 10% i.e. none of the assets
                 are depreciated more than 90% of the gross value.

                Proviso 37.6 of the Regulations, 2020 specifies that the remaining
                 depreciable value as on 31st March of the year closing after a period of 12
                 years from the effective date of commercial operation of the station shall
                 be spread over the balance useful life. In this regard it is to submit as
                 under:-

                In accordance aforesaid said proviso, MPPGCL has worked out the
                 depreciation rate for balance life of assets for the stations which have
                 completed the useful life of 12 years.

                Gandhisagar HPS & SGTPS PH-1 Birsinghpur have outlived their useful
                 life, accordingly, estimated useful life has been considered for working out
                 the depreciation rate for balance life for asset additions made at these
                 stations.

                In case of asset addition made during the year, the depreciation is charged
                 on pro-rata basis based on the commercial operation of the assets for part
                 of the year.

                The Assets additions on account of need-based R&M works at STPS PH-
                 2&3 & SGTPS PH-1 are not considered as Special Allowance has been
                 opted for these units.

                The assets in the records of MPPGCL are only for its own share,
                 accordingly, depreciation is computed for MPPGCL share only

     Provision in Regulations:
168. With regard to Depreciation, Regulation 37 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that:

     “37.1“Depreciation shall be computed from the date of commercial operation of a
          generating station or unit thereof. In case of the tariff of all the units of a generating
          station for which a single tariff needs to be determined, the depreciation shall be
          computed from the effective date of commercial operation of the generating
          station taking into consideration the depreciation of individual units:

                     Provided that effective date of commercial operation shall be worked
              out by considering the actual date of commercial operation and installed
              capacity of all the units of the generating station for which single tariff needs to
              be determined.

     37.2 The value base for the purpose of depreciation shall be the capital cost of the
          asset admitted by the Commission. In case of multiple units of a generating
          station, weighted average life for the generating station shall be applied.

     37.3 The salvage value of the asset shall be considered as 10% and depreciation shall
          be allowed up to maximum of 90% of the capital cost of the asset:

                     Provided that in case of hydro generating station, the salvage value
           shall be as provided in the agreement, if any, signed by the developers with the
           State Government for development of the generating station:

                     Provided further that the capital cost of the assets of the hydro generating
           station for the purpose of computation of depreciated value shall correspond to
           the percentage of sale of electricity under long-term power purchase agreement
           at regulated tariff:

                     Provided also that any depreciation disallowed on account of lower
           availability of the generating station or generating unit shall not be allowed to be
           recovered at a later stage during the useful life or the extended life:

                     Provided also that the salvage value for IT equipment and software shall

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                 Page 154
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

           be considered as NIL and 100% value of the assets shall be considered
           depreciable.

     37.4 Land other than the land held under lease and the land for reservoir in case of
          hydro generating station shall not be a depreciable asset and its cost shall be
          excluded from the capital cost while computing depreciable value of the asset.
     37.5 Depreciation shall be calculated annually based on ‘Straight Line Method’ and at
          rates specified in Appendix-Ito these Regulations for the assets of the generating
          station.
     37.6 Depreciation shall be chargeable from the first Year of commercial operation. In
          case of commercial operation of the asset for part of the Year, depreciation shall
          be charged on pro rata basis:

                   Provided that the remaining depreciable value as on 31 st March of the
           year closing after a period of 12 years from the effective date of commercial
           operation of the station shall be spread over the balance useful life of the assets.

     37.7 In case of the existing projects, the balance depreciable value as on 1.4.2019 shall
           be worked out by deducting the cumulative depreciation as admitted by the
           Commission upto 31.3.2019 from the gross depreciable value of the assets.

     37.8 The generating company shall submit the details of proposed capital expenditure
          five years before the completion of useful life of the project along with justification
          and proposed life extension. The Commission based on prudence check of such
          submissions shall approve the depreciation on capital expenditure during the fag
          end of the project.

     37.9 In case of de-capitalization of assets in respect of generating station or unit
          thereof, the cumulative depreciation shall be adjusted by taking into account the
          depreciation recovered in tariff by the de-capitalized asset during its useful
          services.”

     Commission’s Analysis:
169. Regulation 37.2 stated that the value base for the purpose of depreciation shall be the
     capital cost of the asset admitted by the Commission. In accordance with the aforesaid
     Regulation, Gross Fixed Assets as on 31st March, 2023 admitted by the Commission in
     the last True-up order for FY 2022-23 issued on 7th March, 2024 in petition No. 71 of
     2023 is considered as the base opening figure of capital cost as on 01 st April, 2023 for
     all thermal and hydel power stations in this Order.

170. Further, the petitioner mentioned that in case of asset addition made during the year,
     the depreciation is charged on pro-rata basis, based on the commercial operation of
     the assets for part of the year.

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 155
                                                          MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


171. As per proviso 37.6 of the Regulations 2020 which provides that the remaining
       depreciable value as on 31st March of the year closing after a period of 12 years from
       the effective date of commercial operation of the station and shall be spread over the
       balance useful life of the assets. The petitioner submitted that it has already modified
       their Asset-cum-Depreciation registers in accordance with the aforesaid Regulations.

172. By affidavit dated 6th February, 2025, the petitioner has filed Assets-cum-Depreciation
     Registers. The Commission has considered Weighted Average rate of Depreciation as
     submitted by the petitioner in its Asset-cum-Depreciation register of FY 2023-24 for
     respective power stations.

173. The Commission observed that ATPS PH-3, SGTPS PH-1&2, SGTPS PH-3 and all
     Hydro power stations have completed useful life of 12 years. Accordingly, balance
     depreciation of these power stations has been spread over the balance useful life of
     the assets. Further, cumulative depreciation of STPS PH 2&3 already reaches to 90%
     of the GFA therefore, no depreciation is considered in this power station.

174. Accumulated Depreciation balances (as on 31st March, 2023) admitted by the
     Commission in last true-up order for FY 2022-23 for all power stations is considered as
     the base opening figures as on 01st April, 2023. Further, adjustment in Accumulated
     Depreciation towards de-capitalized assets is also considered to work out Adjusted
     Opening Accumulated Depreciation.

     Provision in Regulations:
179. Regulation 36 of the Regulations 2020, provides that:

     36.1 The loans arrived at in the manner indicated in Regulation 33 of these Regulations
          shall be considered as gross normative loan for calculation of interest on loan.
     36.2 The normative loan outstanding as on 1.4.2019 shall be worked out by deducting
          the cumulative repayment as admitted by the Commission up to 31.3.2019 from
          the gross normative loan. The repayment for each of the year of the tariff period
          2019-24 shall be deemed to be equal to the depreciation allowed for the
          corresponding year/period. In case of de- capitalization of assets, the repayment
          shall be adjusted by taking into account cumulative repayment on a pro rata basis
          and the adjustment should not exceed cumulative depreciation recovered upto
          the date of de-capitalisation of such asset
     36.3 Notwithstanding any moratorium period availed by the generating company, the
          repayment of loan shall be considered from the first year of commercial operation
          of the project and shall be equal to the depreciation allowed for the year or part of
          the year.

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 159
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     36.4 The rate of interest shall be the weighted average rate of interest calculated on
          the basis of the actual loan portfolio after providing appropriate accounting
          adjustment for interest capitalized:
                Provided that if there is no actual loan for a particular year but normative
          loan is still outstanding, the last available weighted average rate of interest shall
          be considered:
                Provided further that if the generating station does not have actual loan, then
          the weighted average rate of interest of the generating company as a whole shall
          be considered.
     36.5 The interest on loan shall be calculated on the normative average loan of the year
          by applying the weighted average rate of interest.

     Commission’s analysis:
180. In this Order, the Commission has considered power station wise opening loan as on
     1stApril, 2023 same as closing loan admitted in last true-up order for FY 2022-23 issued
     on 7th March, 2024.

181. The power station wise details of loan additions towards asset additions to the extent
     of additional capitalization is considered in this Order. The Commission has also
     considered the reduction of loan amount in respect of the assets deletion/de-capitalized
     in respective power stations during the year.

     weighted average rate of interest worked out by considering the actual loan portfolio.
     The Power Station- wise details of weighted average interest rate on loan worked out
     by the petitioner filed in its additional submission are as given below:

     Provision in Regulations:
191. Regarding the operation and maintenance expenses of thermal power stations,
     Regulation 40.1 & 40.2 of the Regulations, 2020, provides as under:

     40.1“The Operation and Maintenance expenses admissible to the thermal power
          stations declared under commercial operation prior to 01.04.2012 comprise of
          employee cost, Repair & Maintenance (R&M) cost and Administrative and
          General (A&G) cost. These norms for O&M exclude Pension and Terminal
          Benefits, EL encashment, and arrears to be paid to employees, taxes payable to
          the Government, and fees payable to MPERC. The generating company shall
          claim the rate, rent & taxes payable to the Government, cost of chemicals and
          consumables, fees to be paid to MPERC, EL encashment and any arrears paid
          to employees separately as actual. If the O&M expenses as per norms provided
          in these Regulations are more than the actual total O&M expenses including
          arrears if any, as per audited accounts of generating company, O&M expenses
          to the extent of normative O&M shall be allowed. The claim of pension and
          Terminal Benefits shall be dealt as per Regulation 39.4 of these Regulations.”

e)   Special allowance:
     Petitioner’s submission

195. With regard to the special allowance, the petitioner submitted the following:
            “The proviso 30 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that the Generating Company,
            in case of thermal generating stations, instead of availing R&M may opt to avail
            a “special allowance” in accordance with the norms specified in this Regulation,

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 165
                                                            MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

             as compensation for meeting the requirement of expenses including renovation
             and modernization beyond the useful life of the generating station or a unit
             thereof, and in such an event, upward revision of the capital cost shall not be
             allowed and the applicable operational norms shall not be relaxed but the special
             allowance shall be included in the annual fixed cost.

             The Commission vide MYT order dated 19.05.2021 in petition No. 53 of 2020
             has permitted Special Allowance for Units of PH-2 &3 STPS, Sarni from FY
             2019-20 to FY 2023-24. Accordingly, the Truing up of the same has been
             considered in the petition.

             Further, the Commission vide order dated 27.07.2023 in Petition No.11 of 2023,
             has permitted recovery of Special Allowance (w.e.f. 01.04.2023) for Units of PH-
             1, SGTPS, Birsinghpur on completion of Useful life. Similarly, the Commission
             vide order dated 17.01.2024 in Petition No.52 of 2023 has permitted Special
             Allowance (w.e.f. 27.02.2024) for Unit No.3, of PH-2, SGTPS, Birsinghpur on
             completion of Useful life. Accordingly, special allowance on thermal power
             stations worked out as below:-
     Table 91: Special Allowance submitted by the petitioner for FY 2022-23 (Rs. In Crore)
         S. No                                Particulars                    MPPGCL as per Norms
