           2           SGTPS PH-1                                                           39.90
           3           SGTPS Unit-3                                                         1.804
                                               Total                                       120.55

     Provision in the Regulations:
196. With regard to special allowance, Regulation 30.2 of the Regulations, 2020 provides
     that:
     30. Special allowance for Coal-based Thermal Generating station:
     30.1 In case of coal-based thermal generating station, the generating company,
          instead of availing R&M may opt to avail a “special allowance” in accordance with
          the norms specified in this Regulation, as compensation for meeting the
          requirement of expenses including renovation and modernisation beyond the
          useful life of the generating station or a unit thereof, and in such an event, upward
          revision of the capital cost shall not be allowed and the applicable operational
          norms shall not be relaxed but the special allowance shall be included in the
          annual fixed cost:
                    Provided that such option shall not be available for a generating station or
             unit thereof for which renovation and modernization has been undertaken and
             the expenditure has been admitted by the Commission before commencement
             of these Regulations, or for a generating station or unit which is in a depleted

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                    Page 166
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

             condition or operating under relaxed operational and performance norms:
                    Provided further that special allowance shall also be available for a
             generating station which has availed the special allowance during the tariff period
             2013-14 to 2015-16 or 2016-17 to 2018-19 as applicable from the date of
             completion of the useful life.
     30.2 The special allowance admissible to the generating station shall be @ Rs. 9.5
          lakh/MW/year for the tariff period 2019-24.
     30.3 In the event of granting special allowance by the Commission, the expenditure
          incurred or utilized from special allowance shall be maintained separately by the
          generating station and details of same shall be made available to the Commission
          as and when directed to furnish details of such expenditure.
     30.4 The special allowance allowed under this Regulation shall be transferred to a
          separate fund for utilization towards Renovation & Maintenance activities, for
          which detailed methodology shall be issued separately.

     Commission’s Analysis:
197. Petitioner has claimed special allowance in respect of STPS PH 2&3 and SGTPS PH
     1&2 (Unit No. 1,2 & 3). Regulation 30 of the Regulations, 2020 provides the special
     allowance for coal based thermal power stations, instead of availing R&M for meeting
     the requirement of expenses including R&M beyond the useful life of generating station.
     Regulation 30.2 of the Regulations 2020 further provides that the special allowance
     admissible to the generating station shall be @ Rs. 9.5 lakh/MW/year for the tariff period
     2019-24.

198. The Commission vide MYT order dated 19th May, 2021 allowed the special allowance
     for Unit No 6,7, 8 & 9 of STPS, Sarni PH-2&3. Further, vide order dated 27.07.2023 in
     Petition No. 11 of 2023, the Commission allowed special allowance for Unit No 1 & 2
     (2x210MW) of SGTPS, Birsinghpur, PH-1 after completion of useful life (25 years) of
     Units. Subsequently, vide Order dated 17.01.2024 in Petition No. 52 of 2023, the
     Commission also allowed special allowance for Unit No.3 of SGTPS, Birsinghpur, PH-
     2 for the period from 28.02.2024 to 31.03.2024 (33 days) for FY 2023-24.

199. With regard to special allowance, Respondent No. 1 MPPMCL in its response
     submitted that the petitioner has neither submitted a separate account in respect of the
     Special Allowances availed by it in respect to SGTPS PH-1 & 2, nor Utilization
     Certificates, therefore, the Additional Capitalization in respect of the Units ought to be
     disallowed and Special Allowance availed refunded to the answering Respondent.

200. With regard to special allowance, Regulation 30.3 of the Regulations, 2020 provides
     that in the event of granting special allowance, the expenditure incurred or utilized from
     special allowance shall be maintained separately by the generating station and details
     of same shall be made available to the Commission as and when directed.
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 167
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     from special allowance along with supporting documents.

     following:

         Special allowance for SGTPS PH-1&2, Birsinghpur have been worked out as
         detailed in Table 4.2.1.1 and Table 4.2.1.2 in the instant Petition. Further as desired
         by the Commission, the details of expenditure incurred / utilized from Special
         allowance of SGTPS PH-1 and Unit No.3 PH-2, Birsinghpur are annexed along with
         supporting documents (Order copies etc.) for kind reference please. In regard to
         STPS PH-2&3 it is to mention that the Units of STPS PH-2&3 are under forced
         outages in FY 2023-24, as such the Capacity (fixed) charges (including Special
         Allowance) on actual Availability, being 0.00%, are nil.

     Rs.39.90 Cr. and 1.804 Cr. respectively. The petitioner has also enclosed a list of orders
     alongwith the copies of work orders placed to different vender in this regard. The
     petitioner is directed to maintain separate record of expenditure carried out through
     special allowance fund and present the same before the Commission with next true-up
     petition. The petitioner is also directed to provide a copy of aforesaid details/documents

     Provision in Regulations:
206. regarding working capital for coal based generating stations, Regulation 38.1 (A) of the
     Regulations, 2020 provides that:

     38.1 “The Working Capital shall cover:
     A. Coal- based thermal generating stations
       i. Cost of coal towards stock, if applicable, for 15 days for pit-head generating
          stations and 30 days for non-pit-head generating stations for generation
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 169
                                                       MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

               corresponding to the normative annual plant availability factor or the maximum
               coal stock storage capacity whichever is lower;
       ii.     Advance payment for 30 days towards cost of coal for generation corresponding
               to the normative annual plant availability factor;
       iii.    Cost of secondary fuel oil for two months for generation corresponding to the
               normative annual plant availability factor, and in case of use of more than one
               secondary fuel oil, cost of fuel oil stock for the main secondary fuel oil;
      iv.      Maintenance spares @ 20% of operation and maintenance expenses specified in
               Regulation 39 and 40 of these Regulations;
       v.      Receivables equivalent to 45 days of capacity charges and energy charges for
               sale of electricity calculated on the normative annual plant availability factor; and
      vi.      Operation and maintenance expenses for one month.

207. Regarding working capital for hydel power stations, Regulation 38.1 (B) of the
     Regulations, 2020 provides that:

     B.        “Hydro Generating Station, the Working Capital shall include:
          (i) Receivables equivalent to 45 days of Annual fixed cost;
          (ii) Maintenance spares @ 15% of operation and maintenance expenses specified
                in Regulation 39 and 41 of these Regulations; and
          (iii) Operation and maintenance expenses for one month

208. With regard to rate of interest on working capital, Regulation38.3 of the Regulations,
     2020provides that:

              “Rate of interest on working capital shall be on normative basis and shall be
              considered as the bank rate1.4.2019 or as on 1 st April of the year during the tariff
              period 2019-20 to 2023-24 in which the generating station or a unit thereof, is
              declared under commercial operation, whichever is later:

                            Provided that in case of truing-up, the rate of interest on working
                   capital shall be considered at bank rate as on 1st April of each of the financial
                   year during the tariff period 2019-24.”

     Commission’s analysis:
209. While determining the interest on working capital, Regulation 38.2 of the Regulations,
     2020 provides that the cost of fuel shall be based on the landed fuel cost incurred by
     the generating company and gross calorific value of fuel as per actual weighted average
     for the three month preceding the first month for which tariff is to be determined and no
     fuel price escalation shall be provided during the tariff period for calculating the working
     capital. The components of working capital worked out by the Commission as per the
     provisions under the Regulations, 2020 as given below:

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                               Page 170

     Provision under Regulations
212. With Regard to Non-Tariff Income,Regulation 58.1 of the Regulations, 2020 provides
     that:


     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 174
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

      58.1 “The non-tariff net income in case of generating station on account of following
          shall be shared in the ratio of 50:50 with the beneficiaries and the generating
          company on annual basis:
      a) Income from rent of land or buildings;
      b) Income from sale of scrap;
      c) Income from sale of fly ash;
      d) Interest on advances to suppliers or contractors;
      e) Rental from staff quarters;
      f) Rental from contractors;
      g) Income from advertisements; and
      h) Interest on investments and bank balances:

                   Provided that the interest or dividend earned from investments made out
           of Return on Equity corresponding to the regulated business of the Generating
           Company shall not be included in Non-Tariff Income:
                   Provided further that the Generation Company shall submit full details of
           its forecast of Non-Tariff Income to the Commission. Non-tariff income shall also
           be trued-up based on audited accounts.

     Commission’s Analysis:

    The Power Station wise Other Income as per the audited books of accounts for
    FY 2023-24 as reflected in Note -26 amounts to Rs. 765.55 Crores.
    The aforesaid Other Income amounting to Rs. 765.55 Crores includes following
    income which has not been considered under the head Non–Tariff income in
    accordance with MPERC Regulations.

(a) Surcharge due to delayed payment by MPPMCL.
    MPPGCL has levied the surcharge on MPPMCL on delay in payment of Bills in
    accordance with proviso 55.1 of the Regulations, 2020 read with PPA’s entered
    with MPPMCL. The amount of surcharge worked out to Rs. 382.54 Crore.
    The proviso 58.1 of the Regulations 2020 does not include/specifies the income
    from Late Payment Surcharge due to delay in payment of Energy bills as part of
    Non-Tariff Income. Accordingly, MPPGCL humbly request Commission not to
    consider the Income from Surcharge due to delayed payment by MPPMCL as part
    of Non-Tariff Income.
    It is to submit that the Commission in the past True Up Orders has not considered
    surcharge on delayed payments as Non-Tariff Income. The said approach may
    kindly be considered.


(b) Interest Income on Security Deposit due to Unwinding of Discount:
    MPPGCL has adopted Indian Accounting Standard (INDAS) from FY 2016-17
    onwards. The said Accounting Standard provides for recognition of notional
    income on account of expected gain considering discounting of Interest on Security
    Deposit. Accordingly, in compliance to INDAS, MPPGCL has recognized notional
    income of Rs. 1.01 Crores at Power Stations in the Financial Statement of
    Accounts for FY 2023-24.
    Here it is to mention that this amount of Rs. 1.01 Crore is merely a Book
    adjustment, and no actual cash is received by MPPGCL. It is to mention that
    the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2017-18 to FY 2022-23 has not
    considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.
    MPPGCL, therefore requests before Commission to not to consider this- amount
    of Rs. 1.01 Crores as part of Non-Tariff income.


M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 176
                                             MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

 (c) Income from O&M Contracts –

     O&M of plant & Equipment of POWERGRID (PGCIL) at SGTPS, Birsinghpur
     and STPS, Sarni.
     MPPGCL has entered into contract with POWERGRID (PGCIL) for O&M of Plant
     & Equipments at Substations at SGTPS, Birsinghpur and STPS, Sarni.
     As per the requirement of financial accounts the amount received / receivable
     towards O&M charges is to be treated as Other Income in Audited Books of
     Accounts of MPPGCL. This amounts to total Rs. 0.51 Crore.
     As this receivable of Rs. 0.51 Crores is a sort of reimbursement towards
     expenditure incurred by MPPGCL for FY 2023-24, therefore, the same may not be
     considered as part of Non- Tariff income, as per Regulation 58.1 of the
     Regulations, 2020.
     It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2017-18 to FY
     2022-23 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.
     MPPGCL, therefore, requests before Hon’ble Commission to not to consider this
     amount of Rs. 0.51 Crores as part of Non-Tariff income.

 (d) Income from Grant.
     The MPERC Regulations, 2020 at proviso 21.5(e) provides as under:-
“21.5 The following shall be excluded or removed from the capital cost of
     the existing and new projects:

           ………..
   (e) any grant received from the Central or State Government or any
     statutory body or authority for the execution of the project which
     does not carry any liability of repayment shall be excluded from the
     Capital Cost for the purpose of computation of interest on loan, return
     on equity and depreciation;
     Accordingly, Commission in the True Up Tariff petition earlier years has not
     permitted the additional capital expenditure towards assets whose funding was
     met from Grant.
     Further, MPPGCL has adopted Indian Accounting Standard (INDAS) from FY
     2016-17 onwards. The said Accounting Standard provides for recognition of
     income as adjustment towards treatment of grant received. Accordingly, MPPGCL
     has recognized notional income of total Rs. 6.42 Crore at Note 26.1 (Other Income)
     in the Financial Statement of Accounts for FY 2023-24, which is merely a Book
     adjustment.
     It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2018-19 to FY
     2022-23 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.
     MPPGCL therefore humbly request Commission not to consider the Income from
     Grant amounting to Rs. 6.42 Crore as part of Non-Tariff Income.
 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 177
                                             MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

(e) Reversal of Excess Gratuity provision :
    MPPGCL has adopted Indian Accounting Standard (INDAS) from FY 2016-17. The
    said Accounting Standards read with Companies Act 1956 provides for recognition
    of provisions and reserves based on prescribed procedure. Accordingly, based on
    Actual Valuation Report, provision was created in the Books of Accounts for
    Pension & Gratuity in previous years. As of now the Terminal Liabilities towards
    Pension and Gratuity liabilities are borne by MPPTCL and same are claimed by
    them in their Tariff.
    Based on Actual Valuation got conducted by MPPGCL in FY 2023-24, it was found
    that excess provision was created in the Books of Accounts of MPPGCL for
    erstwhile MPSEB Cadre employees. Accordingly, the amount of Rs.14.17 Crores
    towards Gratuity Provision was reversed/written back and reflected in Note
    26.1 (Other Income) of Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2023-24 being
    adjustment book entry for excess provision made in earlier years.
    MPPGCL request Commission not consider the aforesaid Reversal of excess
    provisions of Rs. 14.17 Crores as part of Non-Tariff Income, being an adjustment
    book entry.

(f) Reversal of Excess Asset Retirement Obligation Provision:
    MPPGCL has adopted Indian Accounting Standard (INDAS) from FY 2016-17. As
    said Indian Accounting Standards, an Asset retirement obligation (ARO) is a legal
    or contractual obligation to dismantle and remove an asset and to restore the site
    in which it is located on retirement of a tangible asset. The company estimates its
    obligation for assets retirement based on technical assessment and timing of future
    expenditure for required work considering the inflation factor and discounting rate.
    Accordingly, provision towards Asset Retirement obligation was created in the
    books in the previous years.

    In FY 2023-24, the Asset retirement obligation (ARO) estimates were revised and
    accordingly the excess provision was reversed in in the Books of Accounts
    of MPPGCL amounting to Rs.46.85 Crore as reflected in Note 26.1 (Other Income)
    of Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2023-24 being adjustment book entry for
    excess provision made in earlier years.
    MPPGCL request Commission not consider the aforesaid Reversal of excess
    provision amounting to Rs. 46.85 Crore as part of Non-Tariff Income being an
    adjustment book entry.

(g) Interest on Fixed Deposit on R&M and e-Genco Loans.
    MPPGCL keeps the GoMP R&M Loan Fund as Flexi Fixed Deposit (FFD)
    amounting to Rs. 127 Crores approx. in the Union Bank of India and makes

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 178
                                            MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

    payment through this fund under R&M Scheme. This R&M fund also include Rs.
    78 Crore for ERP implementation.
    In the FY 2023-24, MPPGCL received/raccured Interest of Rs. 1.36 Crores on FFD
    from Union Bank of India on R&M Fund / e-Genco fund balance with Union Bank
    of India. The ERP implementation is under progress at MPPGCL. Further the
    works proposed through GoMP R&M Loan are mainly for compliance of statutory
    guidelines (Grid Code /CEA Regulations etc.) and need based schemes essential
    for efficient running of units.
    It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2017-18 to FY
    2022-23 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.
    MPPGCL request Commission not consider the Interest Rs. 1.36 Crore on above
    fixed deposits as part of Non-Tariff Income.

(h) Reversal of Ex-gratia provision.
    The Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 read with subsequent amendments provide for
    the payment of Bonus/ Ex-gratia to persons employed in certain establishments on
    the basis of profits or on the basis of production or productivity and for matters
    connected therewith. In compliance to the above act the erstwhile MPEB/ MPSEB
    from time to time had issued various orders/ circulars for payment of Bonus/ Ex-
    gratia to its employees.
    This procedure has also been adopted by MPPGCL and accordingly suitable
    provisions are made. Suitable arrangements have also been made by way of
    reversal entry towards the fund remaining un-utilized under the head Bonus/ Ex-
    gratia.
    An amount of Rs.2.74 Crore towards reversal of Ex-gratia provision is embedded
    in Misc. Receipts under Note 26.1 (Other Income) of Audited Books of Accounts
    for FY 2023-24. MPPGCL humbly request Commission not consider the Reversal
    of Ex-gratia provision amounting to Rs.2.74 as part of Non-Tariff Income being
    an adjustment entry.
    It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2018-19 to FY
    2022-23 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.

(i) Recovery of liquidated damages at SSTPP Khandwa Stage-I:-
    The unit No.1 & 2(2x600MW) of SSTPP PH-1 was commissioned on 01.02.2014
    and 28.12.2014 respectively. The Commission vide order dated 30.12.2017 in
    petition No. 09 of 2015 has determined the Final Generation Tariff of SSTPP PH-
    1 from CoD of respective units to 31.03.2016.
    In the above mentioned Final Tariff order in Petition No. 09 of 2015 at Para 73,
    Table No.9, Page 34 has determined the excess IDC and FC due to delay in
    commissioning amounting to Rs.565.12 Crore. The Commission has

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 179
                                             MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

    deducted/disallowed 50% of excess IDC & FC from capital cost claimed beyond
    schedule CoD of units amounting to Rs.282.56 Crore.
    Here it is to mention that the amount of Liquidated damages to be recovered from
    vendors/contractors on account of delay in completion of project was not finalized
    at the time of determination of Final Generation of unit No.1 & 2(2x600MW) of
    SSTPP PH-1 Khandwa.
    In the above context, it is to submit that the amount of Liquidated damages for
    delay in completion of project was finalized in FY 2022-23 amounting to Rs 168
    Crore approx. & out of which amount of Rs. 132.02 Crorewas recovered in FY
    2022-23 as reflected in Note-26.1 of Audited Accounts for FY 2022-23.
    The Commission in its True-up Tariff order dated 07.03.2024 for FY 2022-23, in
    Petition No. 71 of 2023, has deducted Rs. 66.01 Crores as Non Tariff Income
    towards Liquidated damages on account of delay in completion of project.
    This deduction is in additional to the earlier deduction of Excess IDC & FC of Rs.
    282.56 Crores as narrated above. Therefore, as on date the Hon’ble Commission
    has deducted Rs. 348.57 Crores (approx. 4.76% of approved Project cost) as on
    31.03.2023 on account of delay in completion of SSTPP PH-1 project.
    In this regard it is humbly submitted that the Regulation 23.5 of MPERC
    Regulations, 2020 provides as under:
      “If the delay is attributable either in entirety or in part to the
       generating company or its contractor or supplier or agency, in such
       cases, IDC and IEDC beyond SCOD may be disallowed after due
       prudence check either in entirety or on pro-rata basis
       corresponding to the period of delay not condoned and the
       liquidated damages, if any, recovered from the contractor or
       supplier or agency shall be retained by the generating company.”
    The above Regulation provides that the liquidated damages, recovered from the
    contractor or supplier are allowed to be retained by the generating company in
    such circumstances.
    The Commission has already provided the benefit to the beneficiary amounting to
    Rs. 348.57 Crores which is more than double of the Liquidated damages recovery
    made by MPPGCL, amounting to Rs. 168 Crores.
    In light of above Regulation, MPPGCL humbly requests before the Hon’ble
    Commission to not to consider this amount of Rs. 36.86 Crores towards
    recovery of balance Liquidated Damages as part of Non-Tariff income.

(j) Foreign exchange gain on reinstatement of liabilities:-
     As per INDAS-21 the liabilities on closing date are to be reinstated at the closing
     rate and hence the retention in foreign currency against EPC Contract of SSTPP



M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 180
                                            MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     PH-2 has been reinstated accordingly to comply with the said IND-AS and hence
     the accounting entry of difference has been considered in FY 2023-24.
     Further, when the actual payments against these liabilities are made in future,
     suitable recognition of gain/loss shall be accounted in the books of accounts of
     corresponding year.
     MPPGCL humbly requests the Commission not to consider the Foreign exchange
     gain on reinstatement of liabilities provision amounting to
     Rs. 2.0 Crores as part of Non-Tariff Income, as per proviso 58.1 of MPERC
     Regulations, 2020, as the same shall be settled in future, i.e., on a subsequent
     date when actual payments are made and captured in Books of Accounts.

(k) Recovery of Ash Transportation Expenses:-
    In compliance to MoEF&CC Notification, MPPGCL has an obligation to transport
    ash produced from its Thermal plants to designated entities and bear the cost of
    transportation. The said cost of Transportation is recoverable from beneficiary
    (MPPMCL) as per Regulations of Commission.
    In this regard, MPPGCL has incurred expenditure of Rs.176.41 Crore towards
    Transportation of Fly Ash during FY 2023-24 as reflected in Note 31.1 at Serial
    No.8 of Audited Financial Statements of FY 2023-24. The said amount towards
    Recovery of Ash Transportation Expenses was billed to MPPMCL and reflected in
    Note 26.1 (Other Income) of Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2023-24.
    The proviso 58.1 of the Regulations 2020 does not include/specifies Recovery of
    Ash Transportation Expenses as part of Non-Tariff Income. Further, the recovery
    was made as per Regulations of MPERC Accordingly, MPPGCL request
    Commission not to consider the Recovery of Ash Transportation Expenses
    amounting to Rs. 176.41 Crore as part of Non-Tariff Income.

(l) Coal Penalty:-
    In accordance with Fuel Supply Agreement (FSA), Coal is to be transported from
    Coal Mines to the Thermal Power Stations of MPPGCL. Rail-cum-Road (RCR) is
    one of the modes through which coal is transported by Contractors to the Thermal
    Power Stations of MPPGCL. The transportation contract provides for various
    terms and conditions towards Quantity, Loading/uploading, delivery timing etc and
    various penalties are prescribed in contract for non-compliances of said terms and
    conditions.
    Considering above, Coal penalty amounting to Rs.48.71 Crore was recovered in
    FY 2023-24 from the contractors and same was reflected in Note 26.1 (Other
    Income) of Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2023-24. It is to mention that said
    penalties was adjusted/recovered against the Bills/invoices raised by the
    contractors and for the point of presentation of Financial Statements as per

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 181
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

         Companies Act, the amount of penalty is shown separately under the Head- Other
         income. Further proviso 58.1 of the Regulations 2020 does not include/specifies
         coal penalties as part of Non-Tariff Income.
         Accordingly, MPPGCL request Commission not to consider coal penalties
         amounting to Rs. 48.71 Crore as part of Non-Tariff Income.

         In view of above submission, the total Non-Tariff Income works out to Rs. 46.85
         Crores. Accordingly, as per Regulations 58.1 of MPERC Regulations, 2020, the
         50% of this amount, i.e., Rs. 23.42 Crores is to be shared with beneficiary
         (MPPMCL), as already submitted by MPPGCL in the instant petition. MPPGCL
         humbly requests the Hon’ble Commission to kindly consider this 50% amount of
         Non-Tariff Income, amounting to Rs. 23.42 Crores to be shared with beneficiary
         (MPPMCL).

215. On going through the above-mentioned reason stated by the petitioner for not
     considering liquidated damages recovered in SSTPP Khandwa PH-1 in non-tariff
     income, Hon’ble Appellate Tribunal for Electricity (APTEL) in its Judgment in Appeal
     No. 72 of 2010 has held the following:

     “7.4. The delay in execution of a generating project could occur due to following
     reasons:
        i) due to factors entirely attributable to the generating company, e.g., imprudence in
        selecting the contractors/suppliers and in executing contractual agreements
        including terms and conditions of the contracts, delay in award of contracts, delay in
        providing inputs like making land available to the contractors, delay in payments to
        contractors/suppliers as per the terms of contract, mismanagement of finances,
        slackness in project management like improper co-ordination between the various
        contractors, etc.
        ii) due to factors beyond the control of the generating company e.g. delay caused
        due to force majeure like natural calamity or any other reasons which clearly
        establish, beyond any doubt, that there has been no imprudence on the part of the
        generating company in executing the project

        iii)situation not covered by (i) & (ii) above. In our opinion in the first case the entire
        cost due to time over run has to be borne by the generating company. However, the
        Liquidated Damages (LDs) and insurance proceeds on account of delay, if any,
        received by the generating company could be retained by the generating company.
        In the second case the generating company could be given benefit of the additional
        cost incurred due to time over-run. However, the consumers should get full benefit
        of the LDs recovered from the contractors/suppliers of the generating company and
        the insurance proceeds, if any, to reduce the capital cost. In the third case the
        additional cost due to time overrun including the LDs and insurance proceeds could
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 182
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

        be shared between the generating company and the consumer. It would also be
        prudent to consider the delay with respect to some benchmarks rather than
        depending on the provisions of the contract between the generating company and
        its contractors/suppliers. If the time schedule is taken as per the terms of the
        contract, this may result in imprudent time schedule not in accordance with good
        industry practices.”

216. With regard to sharing of impact on account of time overrun due to mix of controllable
     and uncontrollable factors, Hon’ble APTEL in its Judgment has ruled as follows:

     “7.12. In view of above, we feel that this case falls under category (iii) described in Para
     7.4. Accordingly, following the principles of prudence check laid down by us, the cost
     of time over run has to be shared equally between the generating company and the
     consumers. Admittedly, there is no enhancement in cost of the contract price of the
     equipment as no price variation escalation was permissible to BHEL beyond the
     schedule date of completion of the Project according to the terms of the agreement.
     The impact of time over run beyond the contractual schedule is only on IDC and
     overhead costs. Accordingly, the same have to be shared between the generating
     company and the consumers. Excess IDC and overhead costs for time overrun from
     scheduled date of commissioning to actual date of commissioning has to be worked out
     on prorate basis with respect to total actual time taken in commissioning of the unit.
     50% of the excess IDC and overhead costs will have to be disallowed. Deduction on
     account of 50% of the Liquidity Damages received by the Appellant from its
     suppliers/contractors has also to be allowed from the capital cost, to give due credit for
     LDs to the consumers. This issue is answered accordingly.

217. In final tariff Order of SSTPP PH-1 dated 30th March, 2017 in Petition No 9 of 2017, the
     Commission had directed the following with regards to Liquidated damages:

         62. On perusal of above reply of petitioner, the petitioner is directed to furnish the
         detailed information of actual LD/ penalty as and when deducted from contractor/
         vendor. The aforesaid information be filed with the next petition for true up of
         SSTPP Stage-I, Unit No. 1and 2.”

218. Accordingly, as per the above direction of the Commission and in line with the above
     Judgment towards “Deduction on account of 50% of the Liquidity Damages received by
     the Appellant from its suppliers/contractors has also to be allowed from the capital cost,
     to give due credit for LDs to the consumers”, the Commission has considered 50%
     of LD damage costs recovery of Rs. 18.43 Crore and considered under non-tariff
     income of SSTPP PH-1.

219. Accordingly, the total non-tariff income of Rs. 46.85 Crore for the power stations in
     accordance with the Regulations, 2020 is allowed in this Order. The break-up of 50%

     Commission’s Analysis:
222. Regarding the other charges, Regulation 40.1 and 41.1 of the Regulations, 2020 for
     thermal and hydel power stations, respectively, provides the following:

     “40.1The Operation and Maintenance expenses admissible to the thermal power
          stations declared under commercial operation prior to 01.04.2012 comprise of
          employee cost, Repair & Maintenance (R&M) cost and Administrative and
          General (A&G) cost. These norms for O&M exclude Pension and Terminal
          Benefits, EL encashment, and arrears to be paid to employees, taxes payable to
          the Government, and fees payable to MPERC. The generating company shall
          claim the rate, rent & taxes payable to the Government, cost of chemicals and
          consumables, fees to be paid to MPERC, EL encashment and any arrears paid to
          employees separately as actual. If the O&M expenses as per norms provided in
          these Regulations are more than the actual total O&M expenses including arrears
          if any, as per audited accounts of generating company, the O&M expenses to the
          extent of normative O&M expenses shall be allowed. The claim of pension and
          Terminal Benefits shall be dealt as per Regulation 39.4 of these Regulations.

     41.1The Operation and Maintenance expenses admissible to existing hydro power
          stations comprise of employee cost, Repair & Maintenance (R&M) cost and
          Administrative and General (A&G) cost. These norms for O&M exclude Pension
          and Terminal Benefits, EL encashment, and arrears to be paid to employees,
          taxes payable to the Government, and fees payable to MPERC. The generating
          company shall claim the rate, rent & taxes payable to the Government, cost of
          chemicals and consumables, fees to be paid to MPERC, EL encashment and any
          arrears paid to employees separately as actual. If the O&M expenses as per
          norms provided in these Regulations are more than the actual total O&M
          expenses including arrears if any, as per audited accounts of generating company,
          the O&M expenses to the extent of normative O&M expenses shall be allowed.
          The claim of pension and Terminal Benefits shall be dealt as per Regulation 39.4
          of these Regulations.”

223. Regarding water charges, electricity duty and cess Regulation 65(2) of the Regulations,
     2020 provides as follows:

         “Electricity duty, cess and water charges if payable by the Generating Company for
         generation of electricity from the power stations to the State Government, shall be
         considered and allowed by the Commission separately by considering normative
         parameters specified in these Regulations and shall be trued-up on actuals.”
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 185
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


224. Further, with regard to recovery of MPERC Fee and publication expenses, Regulation
     65.1 (i) of the Regulations, 2020 provides as under:

             “The application filing fee and the expenses incurred on publication of notices in the
             application for approval of tariff, may in the discretion of the Commission, be allowed
             to be recovered by the generating company directly from the beneficiaries.”

225. The Commission issued MYT Order for MPPGCL thermal and hydro power stations on
     19th May, 2021. With regard to other charges, in Para 174, 176 & 178 of the MYT Order,

              174. The Commission observed that the O&M norms for MPPGCL’s thermal and

                   Regulation 40.1 and 41.1 respectively.

              176.The petitioner is allowed to recover the fee paid to MPERC and publication
                   expenses as per Regulation 65.1 (i) of the Regulations, 2020 on submission
                   of documentary evidence.

              178.“In view of the above, the petitioner is also allowed to recover the water
                   charges paid to Water Resources Department, Government of MP as per
                   Regulation 65.2 of the Regulations, 2020 on submission of documentary
                   evidence.”

226. In view of the provisions under the Regulations, 2020 and MYT Order, the following
     other charges have been considered in this Order:

       i.      The petitioner is allowed to recover Rent, Rates and Taxes and cost of chemicals
               & consumables on actual basis in thermal and hydro power stations in
               accordance to Regulation 40.1 & 41.1 respectively;

       ii.     The petitioner has claimed MPERC Fee and publication expenses on actual
               basis. The petitioner is allowed to recover the same in accordance to Regulation
               65.1(i) of the Regulations, 2020.

      iii.     The petitioner is also allowed to recover water charges in accordance to
               Regulation 65.2 of the Regulations, 2020 and amendment thereof. However, in
               accordance to the 2nd proviso of Regulation 40.2 of the Regulations, 2020, as
               amended, water charges shall be allowed to the extent of normative specific
               water consumption as per the MoEF&CC notification.

      iv.      The petitioner has claimed Rs. 23.93 Crore towards EL Encashment in thermal
               and hydro power stations. The petitioner is allowed to recover the EL

             Encashment in accordance to the Regulation 40.1 for thermal power stations
             and 41.1 for hydel power stations.

     Sharing of gains:

227. Regarding the performance-based truing-up of energy charges on account of
     controllable parameters, Regulation 56.1 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that the
     generating company shall work out gains based on the actual performance of
     applicable controllable parameters as under:

                    Station Heat rate
                    Secondary Fuel Oil Consumption
                    Auxiliary Energy Consumption

228. Further on sharing of gains, Regulation 56.2 provides that

     56.2     The financial gains by the generating company, on account of controllable
              parameters shall be shared between generating company and the beneficiaries
              on annual basis. The financial gains computed as per the following formulae in
              case of generating station other than hydro generating stations on account of
              operational parameters as shown in Regulation 56.1 of these Regulations shall
              be shared in the ratio of 50:50 between the generating stations and
              beneficiaries.
            Net Gain = (ECRN– ECRA) x Scheduled Generation

229. In view of the above Regulation, it was observed by the Commission that the generating
     company shall carry out the truing-up of tariff of generating station based on the
     controllable performance parameters like Station Heat Rate, Secondary fuel oil
     consumption and Auxiliary Energy consumption. Vide letter dated 31st December, 2024
     the petitioner was asked to file the annual details of aforesaid performance parameters
     actually achieved vis-à-vis normative parameters under the Regulations, 2020. The
     petitioner was also asked to file the details of financial gain if any, on account of
     controllable parameters and shared with the beneficiaries in light of the Regulation 56.2
     of the Regulations, 2020.

230. In response to the above query, the petitioner by affidavit dated 20 th January, 2024
     submitted the following:

         As desired by Commission, the Month-wise details of Operational performance
         parameters actually achieved vis-a-vis normative parameters for FY 2023-24, under
         the Regulations, 2020 namely, Station Heat Rate, Secondary Oil Consumption and
         Auxiliary Energy Consumption are submitted.


     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 187
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

         It is to submit that Regulations 56.1 & 56.2 provides that the financial gains by the
         generating company on account of controllable parameters shall be shared between
         generating company and the beneficiaries on annual basis.

         From the above, it is noted that the Regulations clearly specify that the gain
         achieved by the Company need to be shared with beneficiary. As such since
         MPPGCL, as a Company, in totality has not achieved any financial gains on account
         of controllable parameters, therefore in accordance with regulations 56.2, no
         sharing of gains with the beneficiaries is required. MPPGCL requests Commission
         to kindly consider the same.

         However, as desired by the Commission, the Power Station wise financial gain /
         loss has been worked out. Considering only gains, it is also to submit that at ATPS
         PH-3 minor gain of Rs. 19.58 Crores has been achieved, at STPS PH-4 gain of
         Rs. 30.53 Crores has been achieved, at SGTPS PH-1&2 gain of Rs. 6.31 Crores
         has been achieved and at SGTPS PH-3 gain of Rs. 7.57 Crores approx. has
         been achieved individually in FY 2023-24, therefore, the total sharable gain
         (50%) works out to Rs. 31.99 Crores, whereas rests of the Power stations have
         not achieved any Financial Gains on account of Controllable parameters as per the
         regulation. The Power House wise detailed working in this regard along with
         Summary are submitted.

     Commission’s Analysis

231. By affidavit 20th January, 2024, the petitioner has submitted the following power station
     wise financial gain/loss on account of controllable parameters:

     Table 103: Power Station/House wise Financial Gain/Loss for FY 2023-24
                                                                     (Rs. in Crore)
                      Power Station                    Amount      Gain sharing
                                                                   with MPPMCL
      ATPS PH-3                                            19.58              9.79
      STPS PH-2&3                                           0.00              0.00
      STPS PH-4                                            30.53             15.27
      SGTPS PH-1&2                                          6.31              3.15
      SGTPS PH-3                                            7.57              3.78
      SSTPP PH-1                                         -175.09              0.00
      SSTPP PH-2                                         -150.31              0.00
      Total                                              -261.41             31.99

232. On perusal of the details filed by the petitioner, it is observed that actual operating
     parameters achieved by the petitioner’s thermal power stations (except ATPS PH-3,

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 188
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     STPS PH-4, SGTPS PH- 1&2 and SGTPS PH-3) during FY 2023-24 were inferior than
     the normative parameters, therefore, the petitioner incurred loss in above mentioned
     generating stations on account of the inferior performance and poor actual operating
     parameters achieved by it during FY 2023-24, however, the petitioner had achieved
     financial gains in four of the generating stations namely, ATPS PH-3, STPS PH-4,
     SGTPS PH 1&2 and SGTPS PH-3.

233. Regulation 56.2 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that the financial gains by a
     generating company on account of controllable parameters shall be shared between
     generating company and the beneficiaries in the ratio of 50:50. It is, therefore directed
     that the gains achieved in above generating stations namely ATPS PH-3, STPS PH-4,
     SGTPS PH 1&2 and SGTPS PH-3 be passed on to the beneficiaries of the respective
     Generating Stations in accordance to the provisions under the Regulations, 2020.

234. The aforesaid Regulations do not provide for sharing of loss incurred by the generating
     company. Therefore, the loss incurred by the petitioner on the above-mentioned
     stations on account of inferior operating parameters shall not be passed on to the
     beneficiary.

     of the Regulations, 2020, as amended provides that:

           65.3 Expenses towards Fly Ash Utilization & transportation shall be payable in
           accordance to the directives issued by Government of India, Ministry of
           Environment, Forest & Climate Change vide Notification No. S.O. 5481 (E) dated
           31.12.2021 and subsequent amendment issued from time to time.

           Provided that the generating company shall maintain separate accounts/records
           for expenses towards Fly ash Utilization & transportation reconciled with the
           Annual Audited Accounts and duly certified by the Statutory Auditor. The
           generating company shall submit complete details of aforesaid expenses to
           the procurer in Form TPS 19 A along with supporting documents.

237. In view of the above, the petitioner is allowed to recover fly ash utilization and
     transportation expenses in accordance to the Regulation 65.3 of the Regulations, 2020,

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 189
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

     as amended. Further, petitioner is directed to comply with the directives related to
     maintenance of separate records/accounts for expenses towards Fly Ash Utilization
     and Transportation and details of same shall be made available to the procurer in
     accordance to the Regulations.

242. This Order is for the true-up of multi-year tariff order dated 19th May, 2021 and
     Corrigendum dated 9th September, 2021 to the extent it was applicable for FY 2023-
     24. The petitioner must take steps to implement the order after giving seven (7) days’
     public notice in accordance with Clause 1.30 of MPERC (Details to be furnished and
     fee payable by licensee or generating company for determination of tariff and manner
     of making application) Regulations, 2004 and its amendments and recalculate its bills
     for the energy supplied to Distribution Companies of the State / M.P. Power
     Management Company Ltd. since 1st April, 2023 to 31st March, 2024. The petitioner
     must also provide information to the Commission in support of having complied with
     this Order. The surplus amount as a result of this Order shall be passed on to MP Power
     Management Company Ltd. / three Distribution Companies of the state in terms of
     Regulation 9.11 of the Regulations, 2020 in six equal monthly instalments during FY
     2025-26.

243. With the above directions, this petition No. 76 of 2024 is disposed of.

     (Prashant Chaturvedi)                                             (Gopal Srivastava)
           Member                                                        Member (Law)

     Date : 8th April, 2025
     Place: Bhopal

                                                                                     Annexure-I

      Petitioner’s Response on the comments offered by the Respondent No.1
      (MPPMCL) along with the observations:
(i)   MPPMCL’s Comment:
      Auxiliary Consumption in respect of ATPS Chachai PH-3, SGTPS Birsinghpur PH-3
      and SSTPP Khandwa PH-1 have been in excess to the normative by 0.21%, 0.87%
      and 0.09% respectively. The auxiliary consumption being controllable in the hands
      ought to be disallowed to the extent of being in excess. In all prudence, the excessive
      consumption to the extent being in excess ought to have been avoided by the
      Petitioner.

      Petitioner’s Reply:
      MPPGCL has determined and claimed the monthly Energy Charges Rate (ECR) of its
      Thermal Power Stations on normative Auxiliary consumption, normative Gross Station
      Heat Rate, normative sp. Oil consumption, Wt. Average Landed price of Coal, Wt.
      Average GCV of coal as received, Wt. Avg. landed price of secondary fuel, in
      accordance with the formula specified for determination of ECR as per Regulation 43.2
      of the Regulations, 2020.

      Further, the Energy Charges have been determined on the certified Scheduled energy
      as per monthly State Energy Accounts of SLDC. The actual Auxiliary consumption has
      no role in calculation of Energy Charges Rate (ECR). The non-performance in respect
      of operating parameters is not passed on to consumers of state of MP.

      MPPGCL wishes to submit that the statements made by the Respondent No.1
      (MPPMCL) in the instant para are baseless and misleading, therefore may not be
      considered.

      Observation:
      The subject petition is for true-up of Annual Capacity (Fixed) Charges in respect of
      additional capitalization in thermal and hydel power stations during FY 2023-24 in terms
      of Regulation 9.4 of the Regulations, 2020. The issue does not pertain to subject
      petition. However, with regard to poor performance of thermal power stations in respect
      of inferior operating parameters, the petitioner in MYT Order allowed to claim energy
      charges on the normative operating parameters only and any non-performance in
      respect of operating parameters in any of the thermal power stations is not passed on
      to the consumers of the State.




      M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 199
                                                     MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

(ii)    MPPMCL’s Comment:
        There has been excessive consumption of Specific Fuel Oil to the extent of 0.20, 0.09
        and 0.25 ml/kWh in respect of SGTPS Birsinghpur PH-3, SSTPP Khandwa PH-1 and
        SSTPP Khandwa PH-3. The same, being controllable in the hands, ought to be
        disallowed to the extent of being in excess. In all prudence, the excessive consumption
        to the extent being in excess ought to have been avoided by the Petitioner.

        Petitioner’s Reply:
        MPPGCL has determined and claimed the monthly Energy Charges Rate (ECR) of its
        Thermal Power Stations on normative Auxiliary consumption, normative Gross Station
        Heat Rate, normative sp. Oil consumption, Wt. Average Landed price of Coal, Wt.
        Average GCV of coal as received, Wt. Avg. landed price of secondary fuel, in
        accordance with the formula specified for determination of ECR as per Regulation 43.2
        of the Regulations, 2020.

        Further, the Energy Charges have been determined on the certified Scheduled energy
        as per monthly State Energy Accounts of SLDC. The actual Sp. Oil consumption has
        no role in calculation of Energy Charges Rate (ECR). The non-performance in respect
        of operating parameters is not passed on to consumers of state of MP.

        MPPGCL wishes to submit that the statements made by the Respondent No.1
        (MPPMCL) in the instant para are baseless and misleading, therefore may not be
        considered.

        Observation:
        The subject petition is for true-up of Annual Capacity (Fixed) Charges in respect of
        additional capitalization in thermal and hydel power stations during FY 2023-24 in terms
        of Regulation 9.4 of the Regulations, 2020. The issue does not pertain to subject
        petition. However, with regard to poor performance of thermal power stations in respect
        of inferior operating parameters, the petitioner in MYT Order allowed to claim energy
        charges on the normative operating parameters only and any non-performance in
        respect of operating parameters in any of the thermal power stations is not passed on
        to the consumers of the State.

(iii)   MPPMCL’s Comment:
        It is incorrect on part of the Petitioner to assail that the Petitioner, as a Company has
        not achieved any financial gains and therefore the same should not be shared with the
        Respondents. It is submitted that for the reasons the Tariff of the Petitioner company
        is determined unit-wise and not as a Company in whole, the gains from controllable
        factors in respect of each of such units be shared with the Respondents.



        M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 200
                                             MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

Petitioner’s Reply:
It is to submit that the Regulations 56.1 & 56.2 of MPERC Regulations,2020 provides
that the financial gains by the generating company on account of controllable
parameters shall be shared between generating company and the beneficiaries on
annual basis.

From the above, it is noted that the Regulations clearly specify that the gain achieved
by the Company need to be shared with beneficiary. As such since MPPGCL, as a
Company, in totality, has not achieved any financial gains on account of controllable
parameters, therefore in accordance with regulations 56.2, no sharing of gains with the
beneficiaries is required. MPPGCL humbly requests Commission to kindly consider the
same.

However, the Power Station wise financial gain / loss have been worked out.
Considering only gains, it is to submit that at ATPS PH-3 minor gain of Rs. 19.58 Crores
has been achieved, at STPS PH-4 gain of Rs. 30.53 Crore has been achieved, at
SGTPS PH-1&2 gain of Rs. 6.31 Crore has been achieved and at SGTPS PH-3 gain
of Rs. 7.57 Crores approx. has been achieved individually in FY 2023-24. Rest of the
Power stations have not achieved any Financial Gains on account of Controllable
parameters as per the Regulation. Therefore, 50% of the total sharable gain works out
to Rs. 31.99 Crore.

MPPGCL wishes to further submit that the provisional sharing of gains due to variation
in norms have already been passed on to MPPMCL (Respondent No.1) through
Monthly Energy Bills for FY 2023-24. The same shall be reconciled on the basis of
Trueup tariff order for FY 2023-24.

Power House wise detailed working towards sharing of gains, have already been
submitted by MPPGCL as additional information before the Commission vide MPPGCL
Letter No. 07-12/CS-MPPGCL/ MPERC/ TU FY 2023-24/ Pt. 76 of 2024/ 67 dated
20.01.2025.

In view of the above, MPPGCL submits that the statements made by the Respondent
No.1 (MPPMCL) in the instant Para may not be considered, as all the details have
already been submitted before the Commission.

Observation:
The petitioner has submitted the Thermal Power Station-wise monthly details for actual
Operational performance parameters achieved during FY 2023-24 vis-a-vis normative
parameters under the Regulations, 2020 namely, Station Heat Rate, Secondary Oil
Consumption and Auxiliary Energy Consumption. Since the Commission has already

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 201
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

       determined power station/ house-wise tariff in MYT Order, therefore, financial gain/ loss
       in respect of performance parameters fixed in MYT Order has been examined for all
       thermal power stations, separately. The financial gain wherever achieved by the
       petitioner in any of the thermal power stations on account of better operating
       parameters have been worked out separately for each power station and same has
       been allowed to be shared with beneficiary at the ratio of 50:50 in accordance to the
       Regulation 56 of the Regulations, 2020.

(iv)   MPPMCL’s Comment:
       The Petitioner has availed Special Allowance in respect of STPS PH-2&3, SGTPS PH-
       1 and SGTPS Unit-3 to the tune of Rs. 120.55 Crore. STPS PH-2&3 stand
       decommissioned and there has been no expenditure towards Renovation and
       Modernization. Therefore, the entire Special Allowance of Rs. 78.85 which could not
       be serviced into the purpose for which it was granted, should be refunded to the
       answering Respondent. Further, the Special Allowance availed by the Petitioner,
       admittedly, could not be serviced by the Petitioner into SGTPS PH-1 and SGTPS Unit-
       3 to the tune of Rs. 1.20 Crore and Rs. 0.05 Crore respectively. The same, should be
       refunded to the answering Respondent. Furthermore, for the reasons that the Petitioner
       has not demonstrated as to how the said Allowance, to the extent alleged to be utilized,
       has actually been utilized and for the reasons that the Petitioner has not furnished

       Special Allowance availed by it in respect to STPS PH-2&3 nor a Utilization Certificate

       Petitioner’s Reply:
       The proviso 30 of the Regulations, 2020 {RG-26 (IV) of 2020}" provides that the
       Generating Company, in case of thermal generating stations, instead of availing R&M
       may opt to avail a “Special Allowance” in accordance with the norms specified in the
       Regulations, as compensation for meeting the requirement of expenses including
       renovation and modernization beyond the useful life of the generating station or a unit
       thereof, and in such an event, upward revision of the capital cost shall not be allowed
       and the applicable operational norms shall not be relaxed but the special allowance
       shall be included in the Annual Fixed Cost. The Commission vide MYT order dated
       19.05.2021 in petition No. 53 of 2020 has permitted Special Allowance for Units of PH-
       2 & 3 STPS, Sarni upto FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24.
       M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 202
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024


The Commission vide order dated 27.07.2023 in Petition No.11 of 2023, has permitted
recovery of Special Allowance (w.e.f. 01.04.2023) for PH-1, SGTPS, Birsinghpur on
completion of Useful life. Similarly, the Commission vide order dated 17.01.2024 in
Petition No. 52 of 2023 has permitted Special Allowance (w.e.f. 27.02.2024) for Unit
No.3, PH-2, SGTPS, Birsinghpur on completion of Useful life.

Accordingly, Special allowance for SGTPS PH-1&2, Birsinghpur have been worked out
as detailed in Table 4.2.1.1 on normative availability and Table 4.2.1.2 on actual
availability of the power station in the instant Petition. Since, the Special Allowance
forms the component of Annual Fixed Cost, therefore for its recovery, actual Plant
Availability Factor is applied on prorate basis viz-a-viz Normative Plant Availability
factor. On doing so, the special allowance for SGTPS PH-1 reduces from Rs. 39.90
Crores to Rs. 38.70 Crores (diff. Rs. 1.20 Crores). Similarly, for SGTPS Unit No.3 (PH-
2) it reduces from Rs. 1.804 Crores to Rs. 1.75 Crores (diff. Rs. 0.05 Crores). It appears
that these figures have been mis-understood by MPPMCL in its observation.

The details of expenditure incurred / utilized from Special allowance along with
supporting documents pertaining to SGTPS PH-1 amounts to Rs. 39.33 Crores and
Unit No.3 PH-2, amounts to Rs. 1.81 Crores and have been submitted to the
Commission vide letter No. 103 dated 06.02.2025.

In regard to STPS PH-2&3, it is to mention here that the Special Allowance for this
Power Station has not been claimed in the monthly Energy Bills issued to MPPMCL for
FY 2023-24. Therefore, the issue of refund does not arise.

In view of the above, MPPGCL submits that the statements made by the Respondent
No.1 (MPPMCL) in the instant Para may not be considered, as all the details have
already been submitted before the Commission.

                                                                                   Annexure-II

   Petitioner’s Response on the comments offered by the Stakeholder along with
   the observations:

   Stakeholder Comment:
1. From the perusal of the petition, it is known that M.P. Power Generating Company has
   constructed two units of ultra-modern, super critical Japanese technology, Singaji
   Thermal Power Station Phase-2 capacity of 660 MW each, total capacity 1320 MW,
   under EPC contract with the country's renowned private engineering enterprise L. & T.,
   whose cost is approximately Rs. 8000 crore and COD is 18 November 2018 and 27
   March, 2019 respectively, whose performance has been very poor in the financial year
   2023-24 and has not been able to achieve any of the normative parameters set by the
   Commission, which is mentioned in the verification petition as follows:-

   (i)     As per clause 2.1.2, the Normative Annual Plant Availability Factor (NAPAF) of
           the plant was only 72.67 per cent as against 85 per cent prescribed by the
           Commission, resulting in loss of fixed charges of Rs 160.05 Crore.

   (ii)    As per clause 2.1.5, instead of the Specific Coal Consumption (SHR) of 2186
           Kcal per unit determined by the Commission for power generation, coal
           consumption is mentioned as 8327 Kcal per unit. This means that instead of the
           prescribed 625 grams per unit, 40 grams more coal, i.e. 665 grams per unit has
           been consumed in power generation. As a result, about 3.2 LMT of coal has
           been burnt in the total power generation of 7857 MUs. The cost of which has
           resulted in a loss of about Rs. 145 Crore, considering the standard specific heat
           of 3500 Kcal/Kg and the price as Rs. 4500 per MT.

   (iii)   According to clause 2.1.6, the specific fuel oil consumption prescribed by the
           Commission is mentioned 1.5 times more, i.e. 52025. Due to which fuel oil worth
           about Rs. 15 crores has been burnt more.

   Thus, in brief, a total loss of Rs. 320 Crore has been incurred by MPPGCL due to the
   prevailing mismanagement in the company, incompetent and inexperienced Managing
   Director posted in company, as well as misuse of the natural resources of the nation
   MPPMCL is purchasing expensive electricity from NTPC or other private sectors due
   to which Consumers have received expensive electricity.

   Madhya Pradesh Power Generating Company has not mentioned any reason for the
   poor performance of the plant in the subject petition. It is not justified under Electricity

   M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 213
                                            MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024

Act 2003 for the Commission to dispose of the subject petition merely by doing only
mathematical calculation for truing up of AFC.
Petitioner’s Reply:
The Norms for Operation are set by the Commission in the respective Multi Year Tariff
Regulations. The norms for operation for the control period FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24
are applicable in the instant petition. MPPGCL has already submitted its Power Station
wise actual Performance parameters vis-à-vis the respective Norms in Chapter -2 of
True up tariff Petition FY 2023-24.

The Commission as a Regulator keeps an eye on all the Power Stations in regard to
their performance and in respect of compliance of the existing laws, directive or
guidelines provided by statutory Authorities & Acts. In this respect MPPGCL submits
that Power Station wise detailed quarterly reports in the form of MIS & RIMS are being
regularly submitted before the Commission. Periodic Review Meetings are also
conducted by the Commission covering all aspects & functioning of MPPGCL.

The quarterly MIS Reports for FY-24 in respect of the Power Stations of MPPGCL,
were submitted vide MPPGCL‘s Letters No. 1605 dated 24.08.2023, No.2074 dated
22.11.2023, No. 379 dated 23.02.2024 and No. 884 dated 28.05.2024, wherein all the
details in respect of the performance of all the Power Stations of MPPGCL, including
SSTPP Stage-2, Khandwa, have already been submitted.

MPPGCL has determined and claimed the monthly Energy Charges Rate (ECR) of its
Thermal Power Stations on normative Auxiliary consumption, normative Gross Station
Heat Rate, normative sp. Oil consumption, Wt. Average Landed price of Coal, Wt.
Average GCV of coal as received, Wt. Avg. landed price of secondary fuel, in
accordance with the formula specified for determination of ECR as per Regulation 43.2
of Regulations, 2020. Further, the Energy Charges have been determined on the
certified Scheduled energy as per monthly State Energy Accounts of SLDC. The actual
SHR, actual Sp. Coal consumption & actual Sp. Secondary fuel Oil has no role in
calculation / determination of Energy Charges Rate (ECR).

In view of the above, MPPGCL submits that the statements made by the Shri Rajendra
Agrawal in the instant para are misleading, therefore, may not be considered.

Observation:
The Commission has already taken up the issues of poor performance of the Units of
SSTPP PH-2, separately while analyzing the Quarterly MIS Reports for FY 2023-24 of
MPPGCL’s Power Stations. The Commission is closely monitoring the performance of
petitioner’s thermal power stations specially SSTPP PH-2 through review meetings
being conducted regularly and issued directives to take measures to improve the

2. Stakeholder’s Comment:
   It is also noteworthy that the EPC contract signed with M/s L&T has a condition that the
   company will conduct a successful Performance Guarantee Test of each unit within 3
   months of setting up the entire plant and starting of the power plant, i.e., Commissioning
   of the power plant, which is still pending despite the completion of a long period of 5
   years, Since CoD.

   Therefore it is requested to the commission that :-
   (i)   Direct the petitioner to provide information regarding the reasons for poor
         performance of Singaji Thermal Power Station Stage-2 for the FY 2023-24,
         permanent/temporary deficiencies in the plant, efforts being made for
         improvement, if any, etc. and also inform the Commission.
   (ii)  Direct the petitioner to conduct the performance guarantee test as per the
         contract by running all the main equipment of each unit such as
         boiler/turbine/generator and rest of the plant equipment (BTG & BOP) etc.
         together at the capacity of 660 MW, at prescribed technical parameters and
         prescribed coal consumption continuously for 72 hours and, if successful, send
           the report to the Commission and upload it on the website so that public can
           access to the information. It is noteworthy that without the performance
           guarantee test, the 660 MW capacity of the unit would be doubtful.

   Petitioner’s Reply:
   In this context, MPPGCL submits that the Volume-I: Commercial, Section-7 Special
   Conditions of Contract (SCC) of EPC Tender Specification – Commercial, at Para
   7.4.2.10,provides that:-
           "Upon the completion of trial/reliability operation, as soon as practicable, or
           at such time as may be otherwise agreed to by the parties concerned, the
           Contractor shall notify in writing to the Owner that the plant is ready for
           performance tests, only after liquidating all the major defects".

   Accordingly, M/s L&T has successfully completed major Performance Guarantee (PG)
   tests in Unit No. 3 and 4 (except the PG test of turbine of Unit No. 3) in 2x660MW Units
   of SSTPP PH-2 and their test reports have also been approved. The list of tests
   conducted along with their status is annexed.

   At present, continuous generation from both these units is being achieved as per the
   schedule, based on system demand.

                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2023-24 in P-76/2024



   The Unit No. 3 of 2x660 MW Shri Singaji Thermal Power Station Stage-2 was under
   capital overhaul from 30.07.2024 to 04.11.2024. The LP Turbine was closely inspected
   and detailed testing, maintenance and alignment was carried out by M/s L&T in its
   Hazira Plant (Gujarat). Subsequently, the Turbine Driven Boiler Feed Pump was put
   under maintenance in Dec.’24 and corrective action was taken, accordingly.
   Thereafter, since 25.01.2025, the Unit No. 3 is running at near to 660 MW full load. The
   overall performance data file (CSV file) has been collected by M/s L&T on 27.01.2025
   for conducting PG Test of Unit No. 3. M/s L&T has forwarded this full load data to M/s
   LMTG, Japan for analysis and necessary course correction to meet the PG test
   requirements. Once cleared by M/s LMTG, M/s L&T will provide the date for conducting
   PG Tests & its team will be deputed to MPPGCL site. Copy of letters dated 29.01.2025,
   11.02.2025, 13.02.2025 and 18.02.2025 in this regard are attached.

   Further, it is submitted that, since 10.11.2024 the Unit No. 3 of SSTPP PH-2 is in
   continuous operation for more than 100 days.
   In view of the above, MPPGCL requests the Commission to please not to consider the
   comments of Shri Rajendra Agrawal.

   Observation:
   The Commission has already taken up the issue of poor performance of SSTPP PH-2
   and delay in execution of performance guarantee tests separately through review
   meetings conducted regularly and sought Report in this regard. The Annual Capacity
   Charges for SSTPP PH-2 has been allowed in this Order in accordance with the
   provisions under the Regulations, 2020.

   from M/s PFC for Singaji Thermal Power Plant Phase-2 is Rs. 4125 Crore, interest rate

Petitioner’s Reply:
In regards to restructuring of loan of SSTPP Phase-2, it is submitted that MPPGCL was
initially sanctioned the loan by M/s PFC at the rate of 12.75% per annum for
construction of Shri Singaji Thermal Power Project (SSTPP), Khandwa, Phase-2,
through loan number 20701004 dated 29.09.2011.

Regarding the above interest rate, correspondence was done with M/s Power Finance
Corporation (PFC) to reduce the same. In response, M/s PFC vide letter dated
13.07.2021 has offered special interest rate of 9.10% on monthly basis linked to 30
days average rate for AAA Corporate 10-year bond for outstanding debt and further
disbursement under existing sanctioned loans of MPPGCL. A formula has been
mentioned in the PFC’s aforesaid letter towards calculation of applicable interest rates.
For comparison with the above, the current Interest Rate of M/s PFC (w.e.f 20.03.2024)
for Generation Projects in India is also enclosed for kind information please. The
effective annualized rates are also shown on page 3 of Annexure-5. MPPGCL falls
under the Category A+. As such, the Monthly rate applicable for Generation Projects
is 10.90%, and corresponding Effective Annualized Rate is 11.46%. Therefore the Wt.
average rate of interest claimed by MPPGCL in the instant true up petition is much less
than 11.46%.

Observation:
Weighted average rate of interest has been considered in this order in accordance to
the Regulations, 2020. Further, the Petitioner has already been directed to make efforts
to reduce the weighted average rate of interest through restructuring/ re-financing of
loan with reduced rate of interest in accordance with the Regulations, 2020..
